<!-- Managed by lore (https://github.com/BYK/loreai) — manual edits are imported on next session. -->

## Long-term Knowledge

### Architecture

<!-- lore:01a18d59-f408-74e8-9e7c-a594c07288d3 -->
* **.lore.md is lore-managed: never hand-edit; commits are captures-only (DOT-305); conflicts resolve to origin wholesale**: ~/code/personal/.lore.md is a rendered view over Lore's canonical store (lore.db); regenerate with \`lore data export\`, import with \`lore data recover\`. Entries keyed by \`\<!-- lore:UUID -->\` comments with \[\[UUID]] cross-links; maintenance is supersession (amend in place), not deletion. Standing rules: NEVER hand-edit .lore.md, and .lore.md COMMITS are captures-only (DOT-305) — the daemon rewrites the file on its own schedule and hand-fixes race the next rewrite (each heal can mint new defects; measured corruption 2026-11-07: title/body swaps between entries, deleted-correct-entry + false trap, wrong pointer). Guard: drift-test known-rule text (e.g. grep the uv --frozen rule after each re-distillation); drift guard merged as PR #145, retro follow-ups PR #147. Rebase-conflict rule (2026-11-10): take ORIGIN's version wholesale — lore-managed, append-style, origin is newer merged truth, the gateway re-syncs session entries next session anyway; record via rerere. \`lore data recover --yes\` returning 'no change' means the gateway already captured the amendment. No direct \`lore\` tool in Code Mode.

<!-- lore:01a1c1c9-94c1-743d-b834-c49e347edab5 -->
* **agentic-project-template: three-project structure, copier-answers lifecycle, layering contracts**: Structure decision: the standalone agentic Python testing initiative is THREE projects, never one sprawling implementation — (1) cross-repository propagation program spec (template updates become reviewable PRs in child projects), (2) copier-testkit (its own stamped Python project; library first, pytest adapter second, no CLI for its own sake), (3) copier-cat project-creation/agent-operation spec — a separate design flow. Layering contracts: a feature extends exactly ONE layer (or one component of one layer) — 'everywhere' features are declined; composition merges additively and idempotently (verified by the stamp test; existing pins/env/tasks survive); CODING\_STANDARDS\_HEADER is written once and NEVER rewritten. .copier-answers\*.yml files: NEVER EDIT MANUALLY — copier owns their lifecycle and hand edits get clobbered on the next \`copier update\` (the template's self-stamp flow adding include\_alint directly is template-managed, not precedent).

<!-- lore:01a1c3d7-de5e-77d1-a01e-2f408f80a570 -->
* **ai-prompt-library AGENTS.md conventions: flat vs structured, generated/, no force-push**: ai-prompt-library repo conventions (AGENTS.md): prompts/ is the library — flat collections (single .md or dir with SKILL.md; bundle.py detects flat by absence of events/ and roles/) vs structured (events/, roles/, skills/, templates/, context/). generated/ (root + per-collection) is bundler output — overwrite freely, never hand-edit. impromptu/ is a separate sub-project being extracted to its own repo — never apply library conventions to it. tests/, tools/, examples/ are empty placeholders; no test suite. Always run Skillet via \`npx -y @sentry/skillet@latest\` (never a bare install). research/\<topic>/ holds research for proposed project principles. NEVER force-push the repo; a one-time sync note is resolved by whoever pulls it deleting the block, committing, and pushing. Commit style: Scoped Commits. No CI configured — gh reports no checks on library branches.

<!-- lore:01a18637-83e2-759f-ba94-5b00d66fa0a6 -->
* **Ansible remove tag: never implicit — bare run is provision-only**: Uninstalling is always a deliberate \`--tags remove\`; remove tasks are tagged \`never\`, so a bare (untagged) run does provision only and can never tear a topic down — new topics inherit this from template\_dir. Always scope real runs with a tag (\`--tags install\` is the common one). Tag semantics (docs/ARCHITECTURE.md): provision = bootstrap+prerequisites+upgrade+install; bootstrap = first-time system-tool setup (never silent); prerequisites = core deps (git/curl/xcode-cli); upgrade = system-level refresh; install = topic packages; remove = teardown, explicit only. Topic tag hierarchy: pkg-manager (apt/homebrew/docker) → runtime-manager (mise/ollama) → app (everything else).

<!-- lore:01a1f506-1a52-7475-94be-267101695afc -->
* **claude\_credentials harness: zsh from supplied PATH, no host-shell fallback**: dotfiles tests/claude\_credentials/ design (verified 2026-11-19): claude\_credentials\_harness.py invoke() resolves zsh via shutil.which from the SUPPLIED test PATH — never the host shell; RuntimeError 'zsh is required' when absent. Module invariant: direct \`claude\` uses the OAuth profile (CLAUDE\_CONFIG\_DIR=$HOME/.claude-direct, no gateway API credentials); the claude-lore wrapper preserves the gateway route (ANTHROPIC\_BASE\_URL) + ANTHROPIC\_API\_KEY — 'Claude's OAuth profile must not inherit gateway API credentials'. conftest claude\_environment fixture: fake claude binary reports profile paths and credential presence (set/unset), NEVER secret values; PATH '{tmp\_path}:/usr/bin:/bin' deliberately excludes /usr/local/bin (chezmoi's CI install location). CI installs zsh (apt — Ubuntu runners lack it) and chezmoi (get.chezmoi.io -t v2.74.1 -b /usr/local/bin) before pytest; tests/scripts/test\_ci\_workflow.py guards both steps (its zsh guard matches the FIRST 'apt-get install' step — another apt-get step added earlier false-fails). Bump the chezmoi CI pin in step with any local mise/brew pin (gita precedent).

<!-- lore:01a1d89d-27d7-733f-9eb1-79d6568a7d80 -->
* **Copier is one-directional: template→subproject only; answers file is the update memory**: Copier flows template → subproject only: \`copier copy\` renders; \`copier update\` re-renders at a newer template ref; nothing flows project → template. The answers file (\_src\_path + \_commit + answers \[\[01a1d838-19c3-7a0c-8387-cd692ae70e6b]]) is the memory that makes update safe and already serves as each layer's identity — what stacks lacked was never which templates but what each did to shared files, which is why CC-6 (cross-layer resolution report) is the real gap and the stack file is more derivative than presented. Pushing project fixes upstream is copier-cat's separate 'phone home' (C2) ambition — keep it out of stack-composition work. Under stacks, render-derived ownership can't attribute an edit to a layer (python pin → base; roborev pin → agentic layer; same file) — chunk-level attribution needed for C2.

<!-- lore:01a1db63-638a-74df-a64d-bbd75e95c6f8 -->
* **copier\_cat merge registry.py: adopt supplies empty base, accepts() dispatch, merge-file fallback**: src/copier\_cat/merge/registry.py — Registry.merge(path, base, ours, theirs) -> MergeResult (registry.py:34). The registry never invents a merge base: adopt supplies the empty tree (decision-2) and callers pass it through. Dispatch is content- and path-aware: the first merger whose accepts() returns True takes the merge; git merge-file is the fallback when none accept. Existing tests (tests/test\_mergers.py): test\_toml\_named\_lists\_merge\_by\_name, test\_yaml\_named\_lists\_merge\_by\_name, and Registry(mergers=(TomlMerger(), YamlMerger())) composition; the CLI test drives \`app, \["merge", "--ours", ..., "--theirs", ...]\`.

<!-- lore:01a1d855-dfe6-7f32-a0e5-1df19996d306 -->
* **copier-cat composition engine: one 5-stage pipeline, per-format adapters**: copier-cat composition architecture: ONE process for every file type with small format-specific adapters — neither a universal merge algorithm nor per-format systems. Shared 5-stage pipeline: PARSE (format-specific) → IDENTIFY chunks (shared) → MERGE by identity + ordering (shared) → REPORT every decision (shared) → SERIALIZE once (format-specific). Structured formats (TOML/YAML/JSON) are trees: chunks by path, additive across paths, explicit rule on same-path collision; arrays need identity rules (e.g. \`id\`) or append/replace/dedupe diverge. Markdown: named regions \`\<!-- cc:begin name -->\`…\`\<!-- cc:end name -->\` (heading-path IDs less reliable). Plain text: whole-file replace / ordered concatenation / line merge (.gitignore = ordered line set); arbitrary text may be uncomposable (\`enable\_feature false\` vs \`true\`) — no reliable identity → whole-file ownership or review, never an invented merge; unsupported overlap fails visibly, never silently picks a winner. Binary: never text-merge. Two tiers: structured files join the clean data path; a layer shipping a literal fragment (APT's mise.toml today) works standalone but can't join tier one — converting it is the main migration cost.

<!-- lore:01a1db63-637f-7b55-b6bf-3e9497ed1339 -->
* **copier-cat PRINCIPLES.md consent boundary: --yes scopes, writes need consent at the action**: copier-cat PRINCIPLES.md (constitution): deterministic machinery first; AI may classify and propose but is never the merge engine; all output reviewable; no project modification, overwrite, publication, or source-template patch lands without explicit human consent AT the action. ccc init: automation never prompts, and missing config never selects a default stack; never force-reset or directly edit managed OpenCode config. Assembly spec: \`--yes\` approves ONLY the displayed new-destination write — not agent installation, source-template changes, publishing, telemetry, or updates to an existing project; cancellation leaves no approved destination. Planning/inspection/preview may run automatically; consequential actions need explicit review at the action — a conversational request is not blanket permission for unrelated writes. Why: agent loops must never convert exploration into consequential writes \[\[01a1db58-c1d6-7fcb-9f8f-d30f9ea83e13]].

<!-- lore:01a1d829-2c14-74a5-9a6e-28b918dc676a -->
* **copier-cat scope: single-template copier workflows never need it; stacks only**: A single Copier template is self-describing (questions, files, answers file), so \`copier copy\`/\`copier update\` work with zero copier-cat involvement forever — the lock-in guarantee holds. copier-cat exists ONLY for the stack case: two templates rendering to one destination and claiming the same file (e.g. python-project-template + agentic-project-template both claiming mise.toml). Copier itself has no concept of two templates owning one file — a missing concept in the problem space, not a missing copier-cat feature. Adding copier-cat to single-template flows solves a non-problem. Design thread: \[\[01a1d13b-c05f-7589-a7af-a2d3fe1ba079]].

<!-- lore:01a1ad94-b187-7b45-9c16-57f0aa354037 -->
* **DOT-222 payload relocation: chezmoi source root moved dotfiles/ → home/dotfiles/**: Main commit 939304ec (2026-10-28, DOT-222) moved the chezmoi payload: old dotfiles/ tree deleted (root-level dotfiles/ confirmed gone 2026-11-05), files relocated byte-for-byte under home/; .chezmoiroot names home/ as source root; AGENTS.md/docs cite root docs/. Implications: (1) pre-move branches rebase cleanly via rename detection BECAUSE the relocation was byte-identical — prove with hash comparison (\`git ls-tree \<rev>:\<path>\`); (2) tests reference the payload via the conftest seam: tests/conftest.py:34 \`PAYLOAD\_ROOT = REPO / 'home'\` (import \`from payload\_seam import PAYLOAD\_ROOT\`), suites use PAYLOAD\_ROOT/'dotfiles'; tests/gita re-derives REPO\_ROOT = Path(\_\_file\_\_).parents\[2] instead (pre-existing, equivalent — not a finding); (3) role\_path-based defaults ({{ role\_path }}/../../config/...) resolve at runtime — unaffected; (4) prose comments citing dotfiles/ are stale but harmless. Trap: a wrong path mapping in hash comparison returns e3b0c442 (empty-blob hash) for every file — the tell you're hashing nonexistent paths.

<!-- lore:01a1eb56-93c8-7dfa-b66e-344c8a7fd1fa -->
* **dotfiles roles/services: shared launchd/systemd user-unit role and the DOT-343 converge-order fix**: home/dotfiles/roles/services is the shared role for installing launchd agents and systemd user units (mirrors roles/guards: import\_role, tasks\_from routing, defaults for every parameter). It exists because docs/TOPIC-RECIPES.md taught the buggy bootout→bootstrap→enable order (DOT-343); a per-topic fix would let the recipe teach the next daemon topic to reintroduce the bug. apps/backlog's per-topic templates were DELETED, not left unused — a leftover template is the copy a future edit updates by mistake. Converge order: enable first and unconditional → presence via launchctl print → teardown wait → bootstrap. Migration: backlog's systemd unit renames to com.backlog-py.daemon.service; a Debian host holding the old unit needs one \`systemctl --user disable backlog-daemon.service\`. Render tests cannot catch this bug class — the buggy command renders perfectly (DOT-343 passed every render check) — so prove convergence by running the role's own command at throwaway labels (render with real facts, converge to running, tear down) plus mutation testing (4 mutations caught: chain order, && gating of enable, dropped --foreground, drifted log path).

<!-- lore:01a1eb5c-ce71-7034-8d63-4de3c75bf34f -->
* **git topic owns package + global gitconfig; topic resolution schedules providers before consumers**: DOT-352 (PR #178): the git topic OWNS both the git package and the global git configuration — consumer topics (e.g. Chrome) consume Git's provider declaration instead of managing git config themselves; aligns with DOT-134 (git topic owns git config and extensions; git-ai is its first extension). Topic resolution therefore schedules PROVIDERS BEFORE CONSUMERS (commit 0a52b66a). The focused test pins the ordering: removing Git's provider declaration makes it fail with Chrome before Git (mutation-verified 2026-11-17; original file restored byte-for-byte). Trap: a Chrome-side failure from reordering reads like a Chrome bug but is a scheduling/ownership bug in the git topic.

<!-- lore:01a1eb5f-8c04-786e-8bf7-2b3dcffd5730 -->
* **gita topic layout capture: ~/.local/state/layout/tree.yaml counts-only design**: Every gita topic install writes ~/.local/state/layout/tree.yaml recording which type trees exist, how personal/ nests, and repo counts per directory — counts ONLY: repo names stay in the committed catalogue and work paths never leave the machine (spec.md § Data). Chose counts-only over recording repo names/paths because the capture file must be safe as machine-local evidence for redge-cli's R1/R2 catalogue work. The type-directory skeleton provisions the LAYOUT.md phase-3 directories (3 type trees + 4 personal subdirs) in the same install so a fresh machine gets the full shape in one run — prevents any registration pointing into a vanished location (the DOT-366 failure mode). Capture self-heals: any real topic run overwrites the file, or \`rm ~/.local/state/layout/tree.yaml\` and the next install re-captures. Verified 2026-11-17 (PR #177).

<!-- lore:01a19fcb-c3e2-7a74-bd45-342d03684f49 -->
* **lore-gateway service templates (systemd + launchd): doctor watchdog; ECONNREFUSED = gateway down**: lore-gateway.service.j2 (systemd, PR #88/TASK-243) and lore-gateway.plist.j2 (macOS launchd agent) keep the Lore gateway alive. systemd: Restart=always, RestartSec=3. launchd: RunAtLoad + KeepAlive, runs \`lore start --local\` in FOREGROUND (no --bg) so launchd owns the lifecycle. Both source ONLY ~/.config/loreai/env.zsh (never mise env — PYMUPDF\_MESSAGE from the mise fragment never reaches the service), export LORE\_WORKER\_API\_KEY="${LORE\_WORKER\_API\_KEY:-$OPENROUTER\_API\_KEY}", then run a 10s health loop reading ~/.local/share/lore/gateway.port and greping \`lore doctor\` for 'gateway reachable: responding on port $port' — failure exits 1 and triggers restart. All 33 provider baseURLs pin http://127.0.0.1:3207/v1, so a dead gateway = ECONNREFUSED on every model call (2026-10-28→11-01 outage; the plugin would autostart the gateway in-process but never loads). Probe: unauthenticated POST → 401 'No cookie auth credentials found'; prefixed model 'openrouter/z-ai/glm-5.3' → 400; bare 'z-ai/glm-5.3' + clean bearer → 200. Workers auth with the gateway's own env keys; sessions forward their own clean Authorization header.

<!-- lore:01a1851d-9900-7814-a6bd-feacc0dcaa15 -->
* **restart-stale: ctime not mtime; exact comm match; never SIGKILL**: dotfiles/roles/restart-stale answers 'is the running process older than its binary?' with one inequality: process start < binary ctime. mtime/birthtime rejected — Homebrew bottles carry archive-internal dates; ctime is kernel-set on write-into-place, cannot travel in an archive, and is safe-when-wrong (false positive = cheap restart). Match is exact full-path comm (prefix/bundle match would answer for post-update-spawned helpers); unparseable ps rows raise (unknown ≠ not-running). Never SIGKILL — GUI quit is a negotiation: osascript quit with timeout → poll ps → reopen derived bundle only after confirmed exit. Bare \`systemctl --user\` needs XDG\_RUNTIME\_DIR — use the systemd module. Topics append one state-based check at end of install; no per-topic notify coupling.

<!-- lore:01a1ec2a-e3d5-7413-903c-e3e4513fae6b -->
* **run-role.sh vs executable\_update.sh: dotfiles converge/upgrade entry points**: Two deliberate entry points (verified 2026-11-17 from source): \`run-role.sh \<topic>\` = converge topic to declared state (installs missing, fixes drift, NEVER upgrades); \`executable\_update.sh \[full|minimal]\` = the real updater — upgrades ansible first out-of-band, then \`mise exec -- ansible-playbook -i localhost, playbook.yaml --tags "upgrade,\<profile>"\`. \`run-role.sh \<topic> upgrade\` runs only that topic's upgrade tag (home/dotfiles/apps/\<topic>/tasks/upgrade.yaml exists for 1password, anki, apt, backlog, bitwarden, chrome), refreshing topic-owned state only. Tool binaries upgrade out-of-band by design (\`brew upgrade opencode\`, \`mise upgrade --yes pipx:ansible\`) — app-tier topics don't own their binary (opencode's upgrade.yaml states brew ownership); tied to DOT-343: a topic run must never surprise-restart a daemon. \`minimal\` is an ansible tag gating attachment tasks via ansible\_run\_tags (playbook.yaml post\_tasks); \`-K\` prompts sudo once (ansible caches it). run-role.sh has NO --check flag — dry-run via \`mise exec -- ansible-playbook -C ...\`.

<!-- lore:01a1c19b-8ccd-755f-a433-6e13f0537189 -->
* **update.sh/run-role.sh: out-of-band ansible upgrade, -K Linux-only, three alphabetical tier blocks**: update.sh upgrades ansible OUT-OF-BAND FIRST (\`mise upgrade --yes pipx:ansible\`) because ansible cannot safely upgrade itself mid-run (swapping its own venv → ansible-core module\_utils skew → 'Unknown profile name'); the in-playbook mise upgrade task excludes ansible. Final invocation: \`mise exec -- ansible-playbook -i localhost, playbook.yaml --tags "upgrade,${profile}" "${become\_args\[@]}"\` — \`-i localhost,\` (trailing comma) is an inline host list, not an inventory file. \`-K\` (--ask-become-pass) is Linux-only: Linux topics elevate per-task via become: true (sudo prompted once, cached in-memory for the run), macOS brew tasks are user-owned. The playbook has three tier blocks (pkg-manager, runtime-manager, app) with topics alphabetical within a tier; lifecycle tags \[bootstrap, prerequisites, upgrade, install, remove, provision].

### Decision

<!-- lore:01a1d866-6927-74d6-a775-25e3dff7966b -->
* **.copier-answers.\<slot-name>.yml: name by slot, role lives in stack.yml**: CC-9 answers-file naming (2026-11-13): name answers files by SLOT, never role — .copier-answers.python.yml, .copier-answers.agentic.yml (matches Copier's own .copier-answers.main.yml convention). Role (base/overlay/specialization) goes in stack.yml: slots: - name: python, role: base, answers: .copier-answers.python.yml. Slot names must be stable and unique within the stack. Rejected: role-in-filename (.copier-answers.python.base.yml) — with hyphen-joined names like .copier-answers.python-project-template-base.yml there is no reliable way to tell where the template name ends and the role begins; the dot separator is the only unambiguous delimiter. Rationale: a role can change without invalidating the answers file's update history (answers files power copier update \[\[01a1d838-19c3-7a0c-8387-cd692ae70e6b]]). Edge case motivating slot language: one project template may occupy multiple slots with different answers — 'layer' would blur them. No existing answers files renamed yet.

<!-- lore:01a184d7-2833-786b-80c6-73fcc657c6f8 -->
* **.gitattributes: line endings repo-owned; lock files merge=binary (PR #34)**: PR #34 (2026-10-01, commit 94e31f9) adds .gitattributes (8 lines): \`\* text=auto eol=lf\` — line endings belong to the repo, not the machine; cross-machine checkouts must not introduce CRLF churn. Both lock files (uv.lock, tools/skill-trust uv.lock) get \`merge=binary\` — textual lock merges corrupt; a lock conflict is a re-lock signal, never a hand-merge. Landed after a census found 0 CRLF files in the tree (clean baseline, no renormalization churn to manage).

<!-- lore:01a19f87-782d-75a3-a3f7-6cdcafee6597 -->
* **Adopted the 12 vendored dirs**: adopted the 12 vendored dirs as path: skills entries in ~/.

<!-- lore:01a1cdbc-1131-7bc9-b814-ec5a51ff21d9 -->
* **agentic-project-template layering contracts: one layer only, additive stamping, CODING\_STANDARDS\_HEADER immutable**: agentic-project-template spec layering contracts (established 2026-11-09): (1) layering must extend exactly ONE layer (or one component of one layer) — a feature that lives 'everywhere' is a grab-bag and is declined; (2) composition/layering never clobbers what it lands on — stamping onto an existing directory merges additively and idempotently (verified by stamp test; existing tool pins, env, tasks survive); (3) CODING\_STANDARDS\_HEADER is written once and NEVER rewritten — it is the project's prose; only managed blocks are rewritten. These bind any future propagation/composition feature design.

<!-- lore:01a1c1c9-94b4-7148-b626-227b66112890 -->
* **agentic-project-template T3/T4/T5: propagation contract approved yes-to-all; rollout order locked**: User approved T3/T4/T5 propagation contracts yes-to-all (2026-11-09); do not re-litigate — t3-propagation-contract.md authoritative. Authoritative preservation rule (user, 2026-11-09): Copier's smart diff is THE preservation mechanism — the updater stops and reports conflicts for agent-assisted review; it NEVER auto-resolves or silently discards child customizations. Conflict markers, .rej files, unexpected deletions, provenance failures, and protected workflow changes fail closed; a child may pin/opt out of a layer only via a reviewed configuration change. PR granularity: one independently reviewable update PR per source template; layer failures don't block unrelated layers. Ownership: meta owns what every base needs, base owns what every project needs, overlay owns only its domain; Copier-cat owns composition; redge is not a propagation owner. Rollout order (T4): agentic/meta → Python base → flagship (after publication + provenance repair) → redge/ballet. Template PR #4 stays OPEN-draft DIRTY, to be revised for the accepted ownership split; reconcile with origin/main when timing is right.

<!-- lore:01a1e0c7-ba3f-7cd4-bf8a-3a6e5e77a375 -->
* **agentic-project-template: propagate one-writer-per-worktree constraint, not comfort scripts**: Ruling from the DOT-57 probe-cost analysis (2026-11-15): what generalizes from the dotfiles coordination work to agentic-project-template is the CONSTRAINT 'one writer per worktree', NOT session-state.sh. Pushing a status script into the template was rejected: it would propagate the workaround to every stamped project, making shared checkouts more comfortable to live in and entrenching the anti-pattern (same reasoning as the earlier prek hook decision). DOT-57 notes already slate the coordination layer for extraction to the template, so the inheritance path exists for the constraint itself. Why: templates multiply whatever they carry — carry root-cause fixes, never workarounds.

<!-- lore:01a1d20c-7318-7de8-83af-d6b379e5bd36 -->
* **AGENTS.md is an incident ledger — rule/evidence split proposed to docs/INCIDENTS.md**: AGENTS.md (1,063 lines) is an incident ledger, not an oversized rulebook: 24 dated 'measured' paragraphs + 5 cited commit SHAs, each rule welded to the incident that produced it; the retro process is the ledger's author and nothing ever retires a rule. docs/ already holds 31 files / 5,051 lines with 19 linked from AGENTS.md. Proposed split (pending; deserves its own task + review, never an inline end-of-session edit): binding instruction stays in AGENTS.md — short, imperative, no dates (e.g. 'Commit with \`git commit -o \<paths>\`, never a bare \`git commit\`' = 3 lines vs ~30 lines of b3ad740/5692a86/f5ac99f7 forensics); measured incidents move to a ledger doc (docs/INCIDENTS.md), linked once. Sidesteps a CODING\_STANDARDS.md split that would duplicate what docs/ already covers.

<!-- lore:01a1d849-fbae-75c4-bbba-ff5ecb8933bb -->
* **APT layers must expose shared-file content as data keys, not literal fragments (CC-7)**: For CC-7 zero-conflict stack composition, a layer must expose its shared-file content as DATA (template variables with structured defaults) rather than a literal file fragment. Today agentic-project-template ships an actual mise.toml fragment — composing it with another layer hits problem A (base silently wins / .rej) and needs fallback mergers. Proposed schema shape (2026-11-13, awaiting user sign-off): \`mise\_tools: {type: yaml, default: {roborev: latest}}\`, \`mise\_tasks: {type: yaml, default: {review: roborev status}}\` — cc deep-merges these per key before copier renders \[\[01a1d83e-58c5-7552-b26d-db118c07cf3a]]. Free-text files can't take this shape; they keep marked sections with by-name identity (CC-6 residue). Migration required before APT composes with zero conflicts.

<!-- lore:01a1c2bd-6c72-7fd0-a538-46c7291877f0 -->
* **APT-42: stamp.py \_refresh\_alint\_agents\_md generates the AGENTS.md alint block; kept separate from CTCT-12/APT-43**: agentic-project-template (PR #10, branch apt-42-alint-instructions) generates alint's agent instructions: apply\_fragments calls \_refresh\_alint\_agents\_md after composing AGENTS.md — it runs \`alint export-agents-md --inline --output AGENTS.md\` via \`mise exec --yes github:asamarts/alint@latest\`, early-returning when .alint.yml is absent. A prek hook \`alint-agents-md\` (always\_run, pass\_filenames false) refreshes the block before checks. Rejected: requiring a global alint executable — CI runners have none; @latest matches the mise.toml pin. APT-42 is instruction-generation ONLY: dependency/hook work was deliberately split into CTCT-12 and APT-43 (see the alint-mirror entry), never overloaded onto PR #10.

<!-- lore:01a1ed66-7d45-75b4-9a25-bc0ca2460ed0 -->
* **APT-47/CTCT-15: template refreshes plugin; merge PR #17 before rebasing PR #3**: APT-47 delivered contract (2026-11-17; PR #17 agentic-project-template, PR #3 copier-template-copier-template, both draft, CI green/CLEAN): the TEMPLATE declares and refreshes the reviewed plugin before installation and handles project/user trust idempotently, preserving the pure dotagents-install task; the CHILD consumes that contract but keeps its customized bootstrap (uv sync + uv run prek install) — dotagents-add exists in the child yet its custom install path deliberately does NOT call it (user-approved scope: 'Only APT-47 plugin refresh; preserve current child choices'). Merge order: review+merge PR #17 FIRST, then rebase PR #3 onto updated main and review again. A broad layer update to the child produced conflicts and was discarded — CTCT-13 (preserve customized files during meta-template self-update) is the follow-up. 8 Matt Pocock skills installed via dotagents: tdd, grilling, writing-for-agents, code-review, diagnosing-bugs, pr, prototype, wizard.

<!-- lore:01a1db6f-618d-7955-9976-742a447ca571 -->
* **APT-49 constraints: deterministic violations at commit-time/CI, judgment stays review guidance**: Design constraints for adopting upstream-conformant workflow feedback in stamped projects (APT-49, 2026-11-14): (1) reuse/extend the existing backlog-workflow adapter and harness — never add another workflow system; (2) deterministic record violations are enforced at commit-time or CI; judgment-only workflow rules stay review guidance — never gate on judgment; (3) keep library skill authoring separate from stamped deployment; (4) publication and source-template modifications require separate approval \[\[01a1db63-637f-7b55-b6bf-3e9497ed1339]]; (5) every new skill reference is declared in agents.toml in the same implementation change. Why: prevents workflow-system proliferation and keeps unverifiable judgment out of mechanical gates.

<!-- lore:01a1d20c-7311-7428-89f7-f31d23ab869c -->
* **backlog-py autoCommit stays false — gate and usefulness inversely related**: Decision (2026-11-12): leave backlog-py autoCommit=false. The auto-commit is well-built (restricted pathspec, clean-before gate, rollback on failure) but its safety gate and usefulness are inversely related: a probe scoped to backlog/ found 8 dirty paths from concurrent sessions, so the gate would skip most mutations (log warning only), and when it did fire it would still commit from the shared main-checkout index — 'narrowing the blast radius without moving the blast.' Good complement to worktree isolation (DOT-57), poor substitute for it. Revisit after DOT-57 lands.

<!-- lore:01a185bb-fb6f-770f-8f57-3ffa7da43fb5 -->
* **backlog-py CLI: claim flags, terminal transitions via task\_edit not orchestration, no task show, worktrunk absent**: dotfiles task queue is backlog-md-py (DOT-109, v2.2.0); the REAL store is ~/backlog (backlogDir, config.yml, projectRoot /Users/ben.davis) — ~/dotfiles/backlog is EMPTY. IDs are TASK-N (files backlog/tasks/task-NNN - title.md; filenames contain spaces). CLI: task list/create/edit/archive — NO \`task show\`, no --claim flag. Claim: \`backlog-py orchestration claim \<ID> --actor \<name> --expected-version \<N>\` — both flags REQUIRED; version 'none' → 0. Re-claiming a stale inprogress claim FAILS — release the lease first, then claim fresh. Terminal transitions via orchestration are REFUSED ('Orchestration transition is not allowed') — use \`task\_edit status:Done\` (verified TASK-230, 2026-11-01). MCP orchestration\_claim/release/transition signatures are (unknown) — the CLI (mise pipx install) is the working path; MCP task\_\* tools take a \`project\` param. Actor identity convention: 'opencode'. Claim commits land on the shared main checkout — re-claiming from a worktree can conflict with its task-file edits. worktrunk NOT installed — use plain \`git worktree\`.

<!-- lore:01a19fa3-0576-71e1-b0cf-f4bc733095dd -->
* **backlog/ writes go straight to main (2026-10-23 carve-out)**: Author ruling 2026-10-23 (documented in AGENTS.md): backlog/ writes — task create/edit and orchestration claim/release — commit straight to main; code/docs/policy changes stay on feature branches. Verified 2026-11-02: claim commit e90348f is the tip of both main and agent/dot-73. Rationale: the task queue is coordination state other sessions read immediately; gating claims behind PR review would stall parallel sessions. Documented gap: a claim committed on one machine becomes visible on the other only after pull. When reviewing a backlog-only commit, main-branch landing is expected, not a finding.

<!-- lore:01a1d838-19b7-7c96-8216-2cab4b0f17af -->
* **CC-7 forward-first: copier update -a per layer; stack.yml is ordering-only**: Decision (2026-11-13, CC-7 rewritten to forward-first): Copier already handles per-layer forward updates natively — each answers file carries its own \_src\_path and \_commit, and \`copier update -a \<answers-file>\` selects which layer. Chose delegating to \`copier update\` over reimplementing update/merge in cc because duplication violates the graduation principle (nothing cc produces requires cc). So stack.yml needs ONLY ordering — the one thing copier has no model for — not source/ref duplication. Forward flow 'copier, copier, then resolve': update base answers, update layer answers, then hand shared-file conflicts to CC-6's per-chunk resolution (the real contribution; merger registry needed only there). No-lock-in: delete stack.yml and the two plain \`copier update\` commands still work. Constraint: layers drift independently — research/ pins base at \_commit v0.1.0 but the agentic layer at a local path with a moving \_commit.

<!-- lore:01a1c210-bb58-7a1c-b59e-474c8d4964df -->
* **chezmoi apply: main-checkout single-writer; syntax-check=source, converge=deployed**: Author ruling: agents are hard-denied \`chezmoi apply\`; apply runs from the MAIN checkout only, by the author or the main-checkout session. Rejected alternative: applying from worktree branches — two agents applying from two branches overwrite each other (motivating incident: 20 days of uncaptured work in aliases.zsh). Validation split: \`ansible-playbook --syntax-check\` validates SOURCE; converge/idempotence runs validate the DEPLOYED state after apply. Related ruling (2026-10-03): docs/ is repo-only and never deploys; repo-root tooling (tests/, scripts/) sits outside the chezmoi source state automatically.

<!-- lore:01a1e1eb-cd17-7b98-809a-cca83a0154b9 -->
* **CodeBurn stays per-commit cost source; git-ai ses\_ session-ID join is the gated upgrade path**: Decided 2026-11-15 (DOT-275 cost attribution, dotfiles repo): CodeBurn STAYS the cost recommendation — cost is the requirement and git-ai notes lack it. Evaluated alternative: git-ai notes carry LITERAL session IDs bound to exact OpenCode session + model (e.g. tools/skill-trust/README.md lines 63-71 → session ses\_f407ae36affe10UBPV2WhMeSJ3, tool=opencode, model=z-ai/glm-5.3-flash, human\_author=Ben Davis) — stronger session↔commit linkage than CodeBurn's timestamp-window heuristic. Joining git-ai's ses\_… IDs with CodeBurn's cost data would yield a REAL per-commit figure instead of an attributed one. Gates before pursuing: (1) was uninstalling git-ai deliberate? It was recording, pushed to a shared ref, then stopped — reinstalling re-opens that decision; (2) does \`git ai usage\` yield cost for OpenCode sessions? Doc examples show Claude Code (claude-opus-4-8); Claude Code reports token counts in transcripts, OpenCode may not supply the same data. Pursuing requires git-ai reinstall + re-recording and verifying its local DB holds the advertised token counts.

<!-- lore:01a1ed66-7d22-7229-bd81-42aedb902d60 -->
* **Copier-cat agent-package workflow: Agent Plugins v1 generator contract**: Settled contract for Copier-cat's first workflow (2026-11-17 handoff; Copier-cat session runs FIRST, skillify follows once the generator exists): generate portable agent packages as SEPARATE local Git repos per template/package — no hidden generated repos, no silent modification of personal agent config. Offer BOTH an interactive package wizard and equivalent noninteractive answers. Initial coverage: skills, workflows, optional runtime adapters only. Canonical format: Agent Plugins v1 (required plugin.json, optional skills/ using SKILL.md, optional mcp.json, reverse-domain vendor extensions). Copier is the generator; deterministic structure/policy lives in tooling. Compatibility bar: real dotagents CLI installs in isolated global AND project scopes (disposable HOME/DOTAGENTS\_HOME, throwaway Git repos); any OpenCode adapter needs isolated OpenCode loading proof, never file inference. Rejected: assuming lowercase skill.md or .agents/agents/\<name>/agent.md orchestrator install — unproven against dotagents.

<!-- lore:01a1d894-8582-7873-a6c8-8209286a5973 -->
* **copier-cat agentic layer: compatibility proposals — agent proposes, deterministic CLI validates**: copier-cat design direction (2026-11-13): the agentic layer is a thin integration over the CLI — deterministic code owns rendering, evidence, merging, and application; the agent only PROPOSES mappings, adapters, and template patches ('compatibility proposals'), never becoming a second implementation of cc. The agent receives structured evidence (templates, shared paths, per-side contributions, constraints), never 'make these compatible.' Three proposal kinds: (1) local adapter — declarative config, no generated code; (2) overlay change to structured contributions — smallest, most reusable; (3) base change exposing a composition seam. Trust tiers: propose + edit isolated copies allowed; current-project edits need review; source-template edits need separate explicit contribution action. Never silently rewrite installed templates or weaken validation to make tests pass. Accepted proposals become durable artifacts (evidence, patches, tests, validation report) reused by future stacks. MVP: deterministic check → structured failure report → agent patches isolated copies → validate base-only/overlay-only/composed → human reviews diffs.

<!-- lore:01a1db63-6371-7414-8f16-6c8c932869bf -->
* **copier-cat assembly MVP: DOC-9 spec, six-ticket plan, Backlog tracker convention**: Tracker decision (2026-11-14, user-approved): Backlog (backlog-py) is copier-cat's local tracker; /to-tickets publishes one file per issue under .scratch/\<feature>/issues/ with \`ready-for-agent\` as the triage label; /setup-matt-pocock-skills declined as unnecessary \[\[01a1c20e-6f9a-7a2d-92b6-532beecd3cea]]. Canonical spec is DOC-9 — the 2026-11-14 backlog migration deleted cc-assembly-mvp-spec.md, agentic-init-research.md, cli-workflow-review.md et al., folding them into DOC-9. Six tickets under .scratch/deterministic-project-assembly/issues/ (all ready-for-agent): 01 assemble-python-from-named-stack (no blockers; first vertical slice: assemble DEST --stack STACK, read-only preview, write only after approval); 02 compose-independent-agentic-overlay (blocked by 01); 03 share-human-and-agent-command-discovery (blocked by 01); 04 install-optional-conversational-workflow (blocked by 02+03); 05 review-first-use-without-expanding-mvp (blocked by 02); 06 design-reusable-workflow-distribution (no blockers; explicitly outside MVP). MCP/host plugins are adapters over the same CLI operations; --guided is the public walkthrough name, --staged only a prototype-compat alias.

<!-- lore:01a1d8d9-24a2-73c5-959a-52b7b0f72c7c -->
* **copier-cat cc consultant: grill intent, app-first coverage (Q21)**: cc consultant design (grilling round 6, 2026-11-13): must feel like 'a good project engineer asking what you want to build,' not a template CLI with a chat wrapper. Four ask-matt principles: grill intent, not discoverable facts; use runnable evidence when discussion can't settle a design question; keep work self-contained before handing to another session; learn from each project to progressively shrink recurring agent judgment. Coverage (Q21, user-confirmed): when the request exceeds a known stack, first understand the application — language/runtime, workflows, delivery constraints — then recommend existing templates (base/specialization/overlays) or name what's missing; start from the application's purpose, never 'which template?'; unknown needs become adaptation work, not invented templates. Extends CC-7 MVP \[\[01a1d8b2-94c2-7a17-b3fb-7dd399efb431]].

<!-- lore:01a1dadb-17bc-78ed-a022-44cbb963fb35 -->
* **copier-cat cc guided questions: human-readable metadata-driven, never raw JSON mappings**: Requirement (2026-11-14, user feedback recorded in copier-cat CC-17, CC-14, and backlog/docs/cli-workflow-review.md): guided questions in the conversational \`cc\` project-creation flow must be human-readable and metadata-driven — NEVER raw JSON mappings. Why: raw JSON reads as machine config rather than conversation; both humans and agents need readable, metadata-described prompts. CC-14 (ship conversational project creation through an installable cc skill) and CC-17 (review the CLI against human and agent workflows) are To Do. Prototype phase is closed — remaining interaction details get decided during implementation with in-session feedback, not more prototype iterations \[\[01a1dad1-1464-7d35-b5f0-f0c3825b8037]]. Composition design: \[\[01a1dac4-d588-7416-b7f8-30ee77e97ea5]].

<!-- lore:01a1db58-c1a2-7532-aa45-7a9936ec167e -->
* **copier-cat CC-11 MVP: assemble DESTINATION --stack scope, non-goals, language-independent agentic overlay**: CC-11 (copier-cat, settled with maintainer 2026-11-14): MVP = deterministic project creation + agent-callable CLI; first end-to-end command shape \`assemble DESTINATION --stack agentic-python\`. Copier owns rendering + updates; cc owns recipe/plan/discovery/structured composition/reporting — reuse CC-7 composition \[\[01a1dac4-d588-7416-b7f8-30ee77e97ea5]], no second implementation. First slice: python-project-template alone, then Python + agentic overlay via TOML structured contributions + disjoint file paths; templates stay independently renderable + updatable. The agentic overlay must NOT require or inherit from Python — it is the language-independent contribution contract (TypeScript/Rust overlays follow). Same-key collisions need an explicit rule or human review; unsupported overlaps surface, never silently pick. Test seams: CLI acceptance tests + existing composition-engine tests. Non-goals: autonomous natural-language planning, MCP, meta-template slots, universal semantic compatibility, arbitrary template generation. Ship list in backlog task cc-11; spec: backlog/docs/cc-assembly-mvp-spec.md (DOC-9).

<!-- lore:01a1d8a4-3b2b-701a-b2a2-7bc029a18bfd -->
* **copier-cat CC-15 canonical homes: GLOSSARY/spec/decisions/drafts/ROADMAP/tasks**: CC-15 unifies copier-cat design into canonical homes (recorded 2026-11-13): GLOSSARY.md = stable domain language only; spec.md = product capabilities + requirements; backlog decisions = accepted hard-to-reverse tradeoffs; drafts = unresolved questions with a safe default + owner; ROADMAP.md = capability sequencing; tasks = independent buildable slices; handoff document = historical evidence afterward, NOT another requirements source. Workflow ruling: run /to-spec → /to-tickets → fresh /implement sessions rather than treating CC-11 as permission to build the entire design (CC-11 = the implementation trigger). Rationale: design prose scattered across handoffs and chat becomes competing requirements sources; each artifact type gets exactly one home. Matches the ask-matt flow \[\[01a1c20e-6f9a-7a2d-92b6-532beecd3cea]] and grilling-frontier mechanics \[\[01a1c217-89c0-7604-967c-1d4752c0d6b1]].

<!-- lore:01a1dac9-cf4d-7852-8eee-9ffd54efb4fb -->
* **copier-cat CC-17: derive agent workflows from human CLI operations (CLI owns actions, skills guide, MCP transports)**: CC-17 review plan (2026-11-14), following the backlog-py model — CLI owns actions, skill = guidance, MCP = transport: (1) walk the human CLI workflows and tune the CC-7 MVP \[\[01a1dac4-d588-7416-b7f8-30ee77e97ea5]] from real use; (2) derive agent workflows from those same deterministic operations; (3) update skills and the CC-5 MCP accordingly. Standing design rule: agent-facing surfaces (skills, MCP) are DERIVED from human CLI operations, never designed independently — the CLI is the single source of deterministic operations, so skills/MCP stay thin layers over it. Review map saved at backlog/docs/cli-workflow-review.md (human CLI walkthroughs, first-use feedback, derivation plan).

<!-- lore:01a1dae2-cfc2-70be-a860-f2c9260c4b28 -->
* **copier-cat CC-18 telemetry/JSON consent boundaries**: copier-cat consent policy (ticket CC-18, 2026-11-14): validation ALWAYS runs — including guided mode — with no extra approval gate. Bug reporting and general telemetry are SEPARATE opt-ins; reports require transparent payload review plus explicit consent; local-only reporting must work without Sentry. \`--json\` without \`--yes\` produces a safe preview (no write); \`--json --yes\` is the explicit write approval. Rationale: telemetry consent must never ride along on assembly approval — each consent is its own decision. Telemetry/bug-report implementation stays deferred unless it blocks ordinary MVP use.

<!-- lore:01a1d85b-d91f-7c2d-9251-6d6b8d3d4b01 -->
* **copier-cat CC-8: binary composition out of scope; base/layer are stack roles, not template kinds**: CC-8 research rulings (2026-11-13): (1) Binary composition is explicitly OUT of scope — never merge, transform, or synthesize binary files; exploring binary handling is a separate project, not a near-term copier-cat capability. (2) 'Base' and 'layer' are ROLES in a stack, not different kinds of copier templates — a template is base-like because of the concerns it owns and its stack position, not because copier treats it specially; this preserves independent usability with plain copier and avoids making python-project-template an exclusive bottom every other template must inherit from. (3) stack.yml: the minimal common case stays an ordered answers list; the extended form adds role:/requires: fields — do NOT add all fields now; record role/prerequisite semantics as the next design decision instead of prematurely treating every relationship as 'additive layer'.

<!-- lore:01a1d87f-7c47-72b6-9d11-d9e6d07e252b -->
* **copier-cat CC-9 slot cardinality: numeric min/max fields, not exactly-one/optional-one/any vocabulary**: CC-9 slot cardinality design (2026-11-13): user REJECTED the exactly-one/optional-one/any vocabulary — cardinality must be numeric expressions. Chose \`min\`/\`max\` integer fields over raw YAML keys (\`>=1\`, \`exactly: 1\`) because min/max is widely understood in schema validation and covers every case (min:1/max:1 = exactly one; min:0/max:1 = zero or one; min:1 no max = at least one; min:0 no max = unbounded; min:2/max:4 = range) without inventing a mini-language or needing separate fields per case; \`>=1\` is readable but awkward as a structured field. Slot schema: foundation (role: base, min: 1, max: 1), application (role: specialization, min: 0, max: 1), overlays (role: overlay, min: 0, no max = unbounded). Required templates stay SEPARATE from cardinality: the requires block names slot + template (e.g. slot: foundation, template: opencode-overlay) — 'must be among them' is a requirement, not a count. Supersedes the vocabulary decision recorded earlier in CC-9. Related: CC-7 two-problem split \[\[01a1d855-dfd0-7a99-9d02-602c72f4923b]].

<!-- lore:01a1d873-52d3-7317-8d09-ff8b83cec568 -->
* **copier-cat CC-9 slots: stack definition canonical, meta-template rejected**: CC-9 slot model (2026-11-13): slots reintroduced with a precise purpose — a slot is a constrained position in a reusable stack definition declaring what kind of project template may fill it; direct composition still needs NO slots (slots add precision, not mandatory plumbing — revises the same session's earlier 'drop slots' position). Three-concept separation: PROJECT TEMPLATE (declares purpose) → STACK DEFINITION (declares positions) → CONCRETE STACK (selects templates). Illustrative web-application stack (vocabulary only, not a proposed file format): base (required, exactly-one), specialization (optional, exactly-one), overlays (optional, any). Slots give completeness, boundaries, cardinality, choice, early feedback — but a role match does NOT prove compatibility; five checks remain: slot role, slot cardinality, \`requires\`, \`conflicts\_with\`, composition. Naming: canonical glossary term is 'stack definition'; 'meta-template' REJECTED because Copier already uses it for a template that generates another template.

<!-- lore:01a1d873-52c8-7d73-b1ec-24caf84175a0 -->
* **copier-cat CC-9 stack validation: base required, similarity warns**: CC-9 design direction (2026-11-13, recorded in backlog ticket CC-9): start with ONE hard rule — a stack must have at least one base; any number of specializations and overlays allowed. Three incompatibility kinds: (1) completeness — no base, or an explicit \`requires\` is missing → Error; (2) declared incompatibility — explicit \`conflicts\_with\` → Error; (3) potential competition — similar roles/capabilities/owned paths → Warn, never block. 'Template collision' = two templates affect the same file/chunk AND composition cannot resolve their contributions cleanly — explicitly NOT two templates merely sharing a role. python-project-template + fastapi-web-template + django-web-template may compete but is legitimate (separate monorepo apps, main+admin, migration alternatives). Template authors later declare \`requires\`/\`conflicts\_with\`/\`provides\` so validation stays deterministic. Principle: 'One base required; explicit requirements and conflicts enforceable; semantic similarity only advisory.' Extends CC-7 \[\[01a1d855-dfd0-7a99-9d02-602c72f4923b]].

<!-- lore:01a1d866-691b-750c-a4b4-5b6870dca4fd -->
* **copier-cat CC-9 stack vocabulary: slot/role/layer — layer never names a stack member**: CC-9 vocabulary (2026-11-13; user's design test: stack vocabulary must be understandable separately from in-project Jinja templates). Seven concepts: project template (reusable source that generates/updates a project, e.g. python-project-template); slot (ordered application of a project template within a stack); role (slot's function: base/overlay/specialization); layer (internal organization WITHIN one project template — environment, review loops — NEVER a stack member, per user directive); Jinja template; Jinja macro; subproject (Copier's rendered result). Key sentence: 'A project template contains Jinja templates. A stack assigns project templates to slots.' A Jinja macro can never be a slot — no answers file, no independent copier update lifecycle. Rejected: layer-for-stack-members (dual use recreates the ambiguity), Unit, Module (collides with source modules), Component, Instance (rendered-object connotation), parent/child (inheritance connotation). Plain 'template' stays Copier's default; say 'project template' only when Jinja templates/macros share the context. Sibling decision to copier-cat CC-7's composition split \[\[01a1d855-dfd0-7a99-9d02-602c72f4923b]].

<!-- lore:01a1daef-5df6-7200-a311-a8aa732bc974 -->
* **copier-cat ccc CLI: side-effect-free help; --dry-run/--json read-only; --yes approves displayed write only**: copier-cat CLI workflow decision (backlog decision-6; CC-17 'Review the CLI against human and agent workflows'; 2026-11-14): discovery surfaces are \`ccc assemble --help\`, \`ccc help --json\`, \`ccc assemble --help --json\` — all verified to perform NO initialization or writes, so probing help blind is safe. Flag semantics: \`--dry-run\`/\`--json\` are read-only and never paired with \`--yes\`; \`--yes\` approves ONLY the displayed destination write. Positional form: \`ccc assemble \<project-dir> --stack \<name>\` — first arg is the new project directory, \`--stack\` names the template composition \[\[01a1dac4-d588-7416-b7f8-30ee77e97ea5]]. MVP scope includes production schema + enforcement tests, not just prototype checks. Rationale: help must stay side-effect-free because both humans and agents discover the CLI by running it (CC-17's review criterion).

<!-- lore:01a1dad1-1473-7dbd-8b29-2c8a08ef55a4 -->
* **copier-cat CLI: missing config never makes automation interactive**: Assembly-UX design principle (prototype.py init\_command comment): a missing config must never make automation interactive. Noninteractive detection: \`--json\` in argv OR \`--yes\` in argv OR stdin/stderr not a TTY; first-run init runs only when config is missing AND interactive. JSON init without an explicit flag returns 0 non-interactively; explicit \`--json\` init returns status \`approval-required\`, destination None, exit 1. help/version must remain immediate — interactive init never gates them. BUILTIN\_DEFAULTS: output=default, selector=fuzzy, preview\_cleanup=true, default\_stack=None. Commands: assemble, stack, use, adopt, contribute, catalog, init; stacks: agentic-python, python, agentic. Prototype-level decision — production behavior still open in backlog/docs/cli-workflow-review.md (corrupt-config handling, config preservation, explicit --no-init policy, init-before-every-command vs workflow-commands-only).

<!-- lore:01a1d8d9-24a9-7276-8908-d7840386ab62 -->
* **copier-cat contribution candidate: paste-ready Markdown, fresh-session implementation (Q22)**: Contribution workflow (Q22, grilling round 6, deliberately simple initial version): cc emits a paste-ready Markdown contribution candidate — target template's local path or clone source, evidence from the assembly, proposed changes, open decisions, consent boundaries, exact validation commands — safe to paste into a fresh session with no access to the originating conversation. Flow: cc produces the candidate → user starts a fresh session in the local template project → that session reviews and decides whether to implement. Constraints: no background patch application, no hidden cross-repository work; generating the candidate edits and publishes nothing. Extends CC-7 MVP \[\[01a1d8b2-94c2-7a17-b3fb-7dd399efb431]].

<!-- lore:01a1d8b2-94d8-7baa-99dd-ad2fae7f2034 -->
* **copier-cat decision-7: abstract Copier render commands — unified stack ops for CLI + agent sessions**: decision-7 (copier-cat backlog, 2026-11-13): cc abstracts commands to render from Copier for BOTH CLI and agent sessions — Copier remains the rendering engine underneath; cc removes command-line/workflow complexity, never reimplements rendering. Unified operation model: create, preview, update, inspect, and template discovery all operate on a stack recipe; a one-template recipe is valid with no composition-specific config (Python alone = degenerate stack; Python + agentic = base + overlay — same stack model). cc constructs native Copier operations and documents the equivalents; exact command names stay an implementation choice. Agent fallback (Q8): deterministic-composition failure → review-required compatibility report; an agent may propose a compatibility adapter or template patch only as a SEPARATE approved action — ordinary stack creation never silently modifies source templates to make a plan succeed.

<!-- lore:01a1db58-c1d0-7046-9c60-b9cc316b454c -->
* **copier-cat license: FSL-1.1-MIT (PR #5)**: copier-cat adopted the official FSL-1.1-MIT license text (PR #5, merged 2026-11-14; package metadata + roadmap note updated). FSL-1.1-MIT is source-available with a future MIT conversion. Kept independent of the design PR stack (#1–#4) so licensing could merge on its own schedule.

<!-- lore:01a1dae2-cfbe-7129-968b-50830b7aadd1 -->
* **copier-cat MVP: one real ccc assemble path; never wrap APT's CLI**: Decision (2026-11-14): copier-cat's MVP is ONE real \`ccc assemble\` path — cc owns stack recipes, plan, discovery, structured composition, reporting; Copier keeps rendering/updates; templates stay independently renderable and updatable. Chose cc-native assembly OVER wrapping agentic-project-template's CLI: APT's \`new --base\` wrapper works today but is temporary architecture — wrapping would preserve it instead of giving cc ownership of recipes and composition. MVP interface: \`ccc stack list\`; \`ccc stack show agentic-python\` (identity/source/role/required answers); \`ccc assemble research --stack agentic-python --dry-run\` (read-only plan, no --yes); same without --dry-run (reviewed creation); \`--guided\` (deliberate answer review). Assembly sequence: resolve stack → metadata-driven questions → isolated render preview → compose shared TOML/.gitignore → auto-validate → approve destination/scope → assemble + report native update commands. Deferred: fuzzy selection, \`stack new/edit/use\`, init customization, telemetry/MCP, stack-wide updates. CC-17 = run the one real workflow, record friction; catalog ships the two known stacks. \[\[01a1d8a4-3b37-734f-ad5b-f6c25bda859f]]

<!-- lore:01a1d894-8574-7cc2-beb9-b27d95da9e28 -->
* **copier-cat overlay scaffold: structural not semantic compatibility; overlays declare structured contributions**: copier-cat design direction (2026-11-13): cc can make an overlay STRUCTURALLY compatible with a base — never semantically (valid TOML ≠ correct project). Authoring flow: \`cc overlay scaffold --from-project \<stamped-project>\` derives base template source/revision, answers, rendered paths, and structured keys; project-local edits are classified as candidate overlay contributions for human review (base template ≠ stamped project — a hand-added roborev is not base structure). Overlays declare structured contributions (mise\_tools/mise\_tasks mappings) rather than literal file fragments — chose structured over literal because fragments lock the overlay to one base language; structured contributions let one agentic overlay serve Python, TypeScript, and Rust bases unchanged. Bases and overlays share a contribution contract (destination + structure), not inheritance. MVP: TOML first; prove with Python base + agentic overlay, then a TypeScript fixture asserting valid TOML, retained base entries, no duplicate keys, idempotent re-composition. Extends CC-7 MVP \[\[01a1d88d-e275-7056-84c7-0f3e7f157d3d]].

<!-- lore:01a1d86a-06b0-719c-8e83-ca460237a77a -->
* **copier-cat stack model: slots dropped for role-based vocabulary (template/role/stack/layer/application)**: copier-cat stack composition (2026-11-13, user-challenged): DROP slots from the basic model — a template's purpose is not its position in a stack, and no slot is needed to enforce role when the template itself already is that type ('I introduced slots too early'). Vocabulary (5 concepts): Project template (reusable source, distinct from Jinja templates); Role (base/specialization/overlay, declared in the template's OWN metadata, e.g. role: overlay — design-time intent, stack.yml doesn't invent it); Stack (ordered list + requires constraints); Layer (internal organization within one template); Application (optional — only when the same template appears more than once, e.g. ci-template with dev-CI vs release-CI answers, each needing its own answers file + update history). Answers files: .copier-answers.\<template-name>.yml — never slot-named (.copier-answers.python.yml), never role-in-filename (role is metadata, not update identity; changing a template's role must not orphan its answers file). Slots return only if multiple independent applications of one template are later supported. Mechanics: \[\[01a1d855-dfd0-7a99-9d02-602c72f4923b]].

<!-- lore:01a1d8d9-24ae-7ace-9ee6-611ef121d734 -->
* **copier-cat stacks: app-level configuration, optional catalog capture (Q17/Q23)**: Stack design (grilling round 6, 2026-11-13): Q17 — reusable stack definitions may capture template selections, shared answers, and composition policy while project templates stay independent; a stack ('Python CLI with agent workflows') is a reusable, shareable application configuration referencing templates and their supported Copier inputs, NOT a hidden dependency required to render either template. Q23 — after a successful assembly, OFFER to save its reusable configuration as a named catalog entry; saving is optional and excludes project-local edits and secrets; entries are reused locally or shared by explicit file sharing; never published automatically. Builds on the stack.yml recipe \[\[01a1d8b2-94c2-7a17-b3fb-7dd399efb431]].

<!-- lore:01a1c2ed-826e-7732-9408-49140b4d0a3d -->
* **CTCT-12: alint-pre-commit mirror owns the check; mise pin is lsp-only (alint-check task removed)**: Dependency split for alint (CTCT-12 → copier-template-copier-template PR #2; APT-43 → agentic-project-template PR #11): the documented asamarts/alint-pre-commit mirror pinned v0.22.0 owns the hook's prebuilt binary (no Rust toolchain); mise declares github:asamarts/alint = latest ONLY for \`alint lsp\` / export-agents-md / direct use. Dedicated mise alint-check/alint-fix tasks REMOVED (2026-11-09 user ruling): \`mise run lint\` = \`prek run --all-files\` already runs the hook — one check, one owner; on-demand fixes use \`prek run alint-fix --all-files\` (mirror's manual stage). Rejected: \`language = "system"\` local hook (CI runners lack alint on PATH); keeping jdx/mise-action in CI (mirror installs its own wheel). Accepted version skew: mise resolves latest while the mirror pins v0.22.0 — drift absorbed by the export-agents-md refresh hook; who owns bumping the mirror rev (Renovate vs manual) is an open question. Verify the real hook by executing it in a fresh stamped project, not by inspecting stamped config.

<!-- lore:01a1856d-547a-7589-a02c-a8b9d72ff253 -->
* **Decided to use the write tool**: decided to use the write tool.

<!-- lore:01a18ad4-c809-7adf-aec9-1ac40dd17ec5 -->
* **decision-1 item 4: incidents become backlog post-mortems; definition via DOT-275**: Author ruling (2026-10-29, decision-1): incidents become post-mortems in the backlog as decisions with linked remediation tasks. What counts as an incident requires a code review or design review to define BEFORE capture can be applied mechanically — DOT-275 ('Define what an incident is via code review or design review') is queued for exactly that. Do not apply incident capture mechanically, and do not invent a private incident definition, until DOT-275 lands.

<!-- lore:01a1a7c3-d4aa-7c6f-8f74-e9c18eb2138f -->
* **decision-1: VM-verify config & symlink deployments at 3 checkpoints — renders-clean insufficient**: decision-1 ADR (2026-10-29; from two same-day incidents: the e9fe86b payload move left ~/.config/mise/config.toml dangling until the next apply, and \`lore setup opencode\` left every provider routed at a gateway that later died): any global-config change that can destabilize user-level usage is verified in a VM matching the target machine at 3 checkpoints — before PR open, before merge (all approved commits), after chezmoi apply/update and on git pull — enforced via pre-commit + CI (first two) and a post-apply hook (third). All managed symlinks tested by default, especially on location changes (the class that broke mise). A template change that moves managed files is NOT code-complete until the VM gate runs — 'renders clean' is insufficient evidence for deployment-affecting changes. Chose VM integration gates over unit tests because both incidents passed their suites and broke only at apply time on the target machine. Ruling 5 (symlink→copy) landed as DOT-276 \[\[01a18b39-ea36-7f15-94d0-6ad2adcf7046]]; ruling 4 (incidents→post-mortems) \[\[01a18ad4-c809-7adf-aec9-1ac40dd17ec5]].

<!-- lore:01a18b94-d0e8-78d3-b7fd-32ceb5573fe3 -->
* **decision-3 VM-verify (23fae6f): 2026-10-29 config incident — VM-verify global config, test symlinks, one config per tool**: 2026-10-29 config incident (commit 23fae6f; filed as decision-1 at the time, renumbered to decision-3 'VM-verify' in the dot-283 collision cleanup — decision-1 is now Provisioning-answers): the payload move broke the global mise symlink AND the lore-generated opencode.json kept every provider routed to a dead gateway — both passed every existing gate and broke only at apply time on the machine. Standing rulings: (1) VM-verify destabilizing global-config changes at three checkpoints; (2) test all managed symlinks by default — 'a dangling managed symlink must fail loudly, never warn-and-continue' (DOT-272); (3) ONE config file per tool — multiple only where official tool docs establish layered behavior with cited source; 'never leave a second file behind' (DOT-274; surviving file opencode.jsonc); (4) capture incidents as backlog post-mortems. DOT-276: global mise config deploys as a chezmoi COPY, not a symlink — git pull can never change the live config. Supersedes closed PR #90's symlink approach. Classified 2026-11-14: the only post-mortem among the four decisions. \[\[01a1c67b-dcfb-73a0-8fea-393d3096b751]]

<!-- lore:01a1eb58-8d72-7635-a9d2-8855d81ad46f -->
* **decision-6 ADR: Homebrew GNU tools primary on macOS — GNU-first PATH is intentional**: ADR (commit 73735295, 2026-11-17): Homebrew GNU tools are the primary toolset on macOS — rationale: same tools and same bash across the fleet (managed Mac + Debian host), eliminating the dialect drift that caused real bugs (BSD \`sed -i ''\` run against GNU sed). GNU-first PATH ordering is intentional — do NOT 'fix' it back to BSD-native. Measured caveat: GNU-first currently holds ONLY in interactive zsh; login zsh, /bin/sh, launchd, and Ansible still resolve BSD /usr/bin/sed until DOT-368 (gnu-tools topic installs/removes its own packages, GNU first everywhere) lands; DOT-370's docs paragraph is the stopgap. Chose GNU over BSD-native because the fleet's other host is Linux/GNU; rejected per-context dialect documentation as the end state — it documents the problem instead of removing it. Trap details: \[\[01a1eb8d-7557-77c1-b7f0-5e738ce8c218]].

<!-- lore:01a1e0e9-25cf-7e0d-88c1-946936e2163e -->
* **DOT-209 spec-driven-repo-template: own repo at ~/code/personal/, not a dotfiles directory**: DOT-209 routing record: factor a general git repository template out of the dotfiles repo using Copier (9.17.2); it lives as its OWN repository at ~/code/personal/ alongside ai-prompt-library and impromptu — never a directory inside dotfiles and never under ~/code/work/. Working name spec-driven-repo-template (not settled). Scope = only what has earned its place and is not dotfiles-specific: the document set's separation of concerns (PRD what-for, spec what-must-stay-true, ROADMAP what-is-next, TASKS what-is-now, AGENTS how), the RECL loop, and the verification practices (both-directions, prove-the-test-fails, property/fuzz guidance). Iterating on it hits the copier-renders-from-tag trap \[\[01a1d842-37a8-7ca8-957d-371f0e15e008]].

<!-- lore:01a1e0e6-a851-7441-8df9-b4ca1da99aad -->
* **DOT-275 bug-vs-incident boundary: detection source discriminates; Class A–D vs decision-3 system-failure naming collision**: DOT-275 open design positions (2026-11-15, assistant proposals pending user confirmation): severity boundary — a failure a gate caught or that reverted cleanly is a BUG; one needing manual recovery on a live machine is an INCIDENT. Blast radius (one machine vs fleet) changes urgency only, not the capture. Detection source may do most of the discriminating work: gate-caught = bug (the gate is the evidence); silent failure discovered by the author = incident. AC #3 collision judged real but narrow: Class A–D classify agent behaviour, while decision-3 incidents are SYSTEM failures (payload move breaking a symlink; generated config capturing a dead route) — different subjects that can coexist but need distinct vocabulary, since 'Class B incident' currently means an agent faked evidence. Class definitions themselves: see the DOT-275 incident-classes entry.

<!-- lore:01a1e1da-af8f-73d9-81c8-077adfccbcc3 -->
* **DOT-275 cost attribution: CodeBurn chosen; budi rejected (not OpenCode-supported)**: Decided 2026-11-15; supersedes the same-day hand-rolled git-notes cost schema — 'commit-level cost attribution' is an established named practice (CodeBurn already correlates sessions↔commits), so build nothing. Ranking: CodeBurn CHOSEN (dedicated OpenCode provider doc; \`codeburn yield\` correlates sessions↔commits); budi REJECTED — not in OpenCode's supported-provider list, the only hard requirement, despite looking superior (Rust, local-first, 13 MB, repo/branch/ticket breakdown); ranking by polish would have wrongly picked it. \`opencode stats\` insufficient alone (project totals only) \[\[01a1e1c6-1d75-7903-8bba-3d64b690688d]]; git-metrics/git-perf/git-kv rejected (notes storage, no AI cost source). Division of labor: CodeBurn MEASURES (reads existing session files, prices them, no daemon in the commit path); refs/notes/cost RECORDS the settled figure on a fault's commit (record spec: \[\[01a1e1c6-1d5b-7a5b-ba21-25049fb03651]]). Disclosure: raw per-session spend stays machine-local (CodeBurn local-first; notes ref doesn't push); aggregate spend never enters the repo — a specific fault's recovery cost does, as post-mortem context shareable with the second machine.

<!-- lore:01a1e15a-4216-7df4-bf4f-5a647eb94c90 -->
* **DOT-275 fault cost fields settled at close: time-to-recovery, functionality lost, spend**: DOT-275 fault cost fields (2026-11-15): cost is recorded ON the fault at close, never used to open one — cost is only known at the end, while a trigger must fire at the start. Required at close, three fields: Time to recovery (wall-clock noticing→working again), Functionality lost (which topics/commands, for how long), Spend (tokens and dollars burned on recovery — real and currently invisible). Two after-the-fact jobs for the data: (1) a fault costing six hours/$40 that the impact grade under-predicted is itself a signal the grading is wrong; (2) accumulated cost across faults sharing a cause is the argument for opening a Weakness — three faults at twenty minutes each is annoying, three at four hours each justifies structural work. Near-miss capture kept: a near miss costs almost nothing but the cost that didn't happen IS the finding (SRE near-miss logic).

<!-- lore:01a1e15a-420e-784d-9365-80caad391fdc -->
* **DOT-275 fault impact grading: Reach × Scope worse-of-two; dependency chains reach High**: DOT-275 fault impact grading (2026-11-15): grade on the WORSE of two axes; either axis alone reaches High. Reach (machines): High = every machine, no healthy peer to recover from; Medium = one machine, manual recovery; Low = one machine, fixed by re-running a normal command. Scope (topics): High = a topic unusable AND others require it; Medium = one topic degraded, nothing depends on it; Low = cosmetic or single-command. Why: the earlier machines-only table scored a bug destroying four interdependent topics on one machine as Medium — wrong, because the dependency case matches fleet-wide in the property that matters: it removes your recovery tools (precedent: when mise broke, backlog-py went with it — decision-3's chain). 'How many topics does this break' is computable from topic\_requires/requires\_map, not a judgment call. Pairs with measure-before-severity \[\[01a1e0c7-ba41-7edb-95a2-435bd5ee2d99]].

<!-- lore:01a1e15a-4206-73c1-9e19-6163a0a2ed4a -->
* **DOT-275 fault taxonomy: Incident/Fault/Weakness; 'problem' rejected for grep noise**: DOT-275 fault-ledger naming (2026-11-15): three terms. Incident = existing agent-conduct event (ledger entry, ages per Class A–D); Fault = one system failure on a date, closes when recovered; Weakness = underlying cause behind one or more faults, open until the cause is removed. 'Fault problem' dropped — fault is an event, problem a cause, not variants. ITIL's 'problem' means exactly this but was rejected: generic English word, \`grep -i problem\` returns noise forever, and 'a problem record about the gate' is ambiguous in prose where 'a weakness record about the gate' isn't. Weakness sits naturally beside fault and describes DOT-286 accurately (branch-snapshot scanner = weakness; each session's red gate = fault). Open: does a fault need an origin field (agent-authored change vs upstream drift vs user edit)? Solo-dev answer probably no (SRE blameless), but it gates trend analysis 'are agent changes causing more faults?'. Priority context \[\[01a1e0e6-a85a-7aa9-8197-815914e71c6c]].

<!-- lore:01a1e154-b066-7bc8-a1ac-0f9a8ea0292c -->
* **DOT-275 fault vs incident vocabulary: fault = system failure, incident = agent conduct; never cross-graded**: DOT-275 incident-definition review (2026-11-15; proposal — user's call pending on the word and record structure): decision-3's two incidents are system failures with no agent misbehaviour, so trust-ladder Class A–D vocabulary must not be reused — 'Class B incident' already means an agent faked evidence. Proposed table: Incident = agent conduct (Class A–D, rungs, ledger — unchanged); Fault = system behaviour (impact/urgency, post-mortem, decision record; example: payload move dangles the mise symlink); Fault problem = recurring fault cause, one record covering many faults (precedent DOT-286: queue-integrity phantoms fixed structurally via checkActiveBranches:false, not per-failure responses). 'Fault' chosen over defect/failure: standard dependability term (fault→error→failure), unused in the trust ladder, blame-free, and a fault can have no agent at all. Key principle: agents remain the only place we raise the alarm — a fault yields a post-mortem and remediation tasks; only an incident moves someone down the ladder; the two never grade each other.

<!-- lore:01a1e154-b07a-7b36-ab6c-7c0077d28d9c -->
* **DOT-275 impact grading: recovery capability, not users — fleet-wide removes the healthy-peer recovery tool**: DOT-275 blast-radius answer (2026-11-15, proposed; user's rollback argument accepted as correct): impact grades on recovery capability, replacing ITIL's users/revenue axis for a solo two-machine fleet — High = every machine affected (no healthy peer to recover from; full rollback, rare and significant); Medium = one machine, needed manual recovery; Low = one machine, recovered by re-running a normal command. Urgency stays separate (blocked now?); priority falls out of the pair, as ITIL does it. Radius is a field on the record and affects urgency, not classification — except fleet-wide, which is worse in KIND: with one machine healthy, the healthy machine IS the recovery tool; fleet-wide removes that.

<!-- lore:01a1db63-a516-754c-8506-36b88bad46fc -->
* **DOT-275 incident classification: post-mortem = decision subtype; deliverable is a decision procedure**: Ruling (2026-11-14): of the four backlog/decisions records, only decision-3 (VM-verify \[\[01a1c67b-dd15-7f05-811b-4cfdbd8b23f7]]) is a post-mortem; decision-1/2/4 are ordinary ADRs. 'Incident' is a subtype of decision distinguished by its Context being a failure — no separate record type is needed; DOT-275 exists for exactly this classification. DOT-275 reframed: since the end state is a shareable skill that self-detects its prerequisites, the deliverable must be a decision procedure — ordered questions with concrete examples such that two different agents classifying the same failure reach the same answer — not prose governance docs written for humans. Its four existing ACs stay valid; only the output format changes. Design review still needs the user's judgement on severity threshold, blast radius, and the Class A–D collision its ACs already flag.

<!-- lore:01a1e154-b074-7446-8805-e619275a5aaa -->
* **DOT-275 incident test: consequence gates capture, cause recorded inside (ITIL Incident/Problem split)**: DOT-275 gated-question answers (2026-11-15, proposed): ITIL framing accepted with one correction — ITIL splits Incident (unplanned interruption, judged by consequence) from Problem (underlying cause, judged by cause); cause is deliberately NOT part of the incident test. A trivial typo needing manual recovery IS an incident (cheap, closes immediately); a serious design flaw caught before apply is a problem with no incident attached. Capture threshold: 'needed manual recovery on a live machine' (consequence test, not cause test). Detection axis: gate-caught = bug (the gate working is the evidence); passes every gate and surfaces live = incident — the gap produces the remediation tasks. Repeated gate failures of one type open a problem record, not per-failure incidents. SRE's near-miss trigger covers catastrophic last-line catches (surviving by one layer is luck). Solo calibration: three incident records a week = miscalibrated procedure. Open: near-miss capture worth it solo; user's criterion unresolved — a bug affecting multiple topics or making one completely unusable (dependent topics) as a smaller catastrophic failure.

<!-- lore:01a1e1c6-1d80-74d0-b4df-70c2bb301542 -->
* **DOT-275 post-mortem design: retro/post-mortem split, cost-gated escalation, change-class field**: Design settled 2026-11-15: (1) Retro and Post-mortem are independent — Retro triggers when a session ENDS, asks 'was the environment wrong?', outputs checks/standards/navigation; Post-mortem triggers on a FAULT, asks 'was the system wrong?', outputs remediation tasks + decision record. (2) Escalation: impact opens the fault record at the start; cost decides at close whether it earns a post-mortem — a 20-minute fault earns a line; a 6-hour/$40 fault earns a post-mortem because recovery was expensive, not because it was more severe. A Low-grade/High-cost gap is itself the finding that impact grading is miscalibrated (measure-before-judging \[\[01a1e0c7-ba41-7edb-95a2-435bd5ee2d99]]). (3) Fault metadata is a CHANGE-CLASS field (agent-authored change, author-authored change, upstream drift, environment), not an author/blame field — SRE blameless means don't indict a person, not attribution-free; a solo developer's author field is a constant, while change-class answers 'do agent-authored changes produce more or costlier faults?'. Cost measurement/recording mechanics: \[\[01a1e1c6-1d5b-7a5b-ba21-25049fb03651]] + the CodeBurn tool decision.

<!-- lore:01a19fcb-c40e-7187-8fc9-62c7d09b60f9 -->
* **DOT-283: slow loop migrates topics off \`llm keys get\` onto vault CLIs**: Created 2026-11-02 (commit dbf4d20, backlog carve-out): a periodic, resumable loop taking one topic per iteration from ad-hoc secret reads to a named vault reference via roles/secrets-session \[\[01a19fa2-842e-7b03-99ba-262686adc77d]]. Rulings: the loop is the mechanism, not the conversion; configuration + a check, never a new tool (bespoke code beyond the seam = surface-and-ask); fail-closed — a locked vault must say so, never silently fall back to \`llm keys get\`. Order: loreai first (live fitz defect \[\[01a19fa2-840a-70e3-8df1-409a985f0b39]]), then ori + ollama-evals (same llm channel), then reconcile uv (1Password) / deb-get (Bitwarden) divergent backends; \`llm keys get\` demoted to documented fallback. Chose extending secrets-session over a new resolver because the seam already has two consumers. Open questions captured, not guessed: per-machine vault (bitwarden=personal / 1password=work) and loop cadence.

<!-- lore:01a1ec9b-2988-7a52-a614-da91b415098f -->
* **DOT-340 two-layer deprecation gate: static scan + per-topic runtime check**: DOT-340 (amended 2026-11-17) detects injected-facts deprecation in topic defaults with two layers: (1) parsed-expression static scan over home/dotfiles/ — primary gate, allow-list of surviving magic vars, no prose/ansible\_facts\[...] false hits; (2) per-topic runtime check (user's ask): harness play loads defaults via vars\_files, renders every var, runs real ansible-playbook in check mode, asserts zero DEPRECATION WARNING on stderr; failures name topic + file:line origins (dedup per (file,var)); parametrized per topic; skips without the binary like tests/topic\_resolution. Why two layers: starship's ansible\_date\_time renders nowhere today — only the every-var render sees it; the runtime layer can't see facts-less paths or unrendered conditionals (DOT-339 AC #4's defined-guard territory). Two fault classes, two checks. Runtime layer needs mutation proof (AC): re-adding a bare ansible\_env read to a topic default must turn its test red, recorded in trailers. Sequencing DOT-339 → DOT-340 unchanged; after DOT-339 flips inject\_facts\_as\_vars=False the class is structurally prevented; update.sh full deprecation warnings stay cosmetic until ansible-core 2.24.

<!-- lore:01a1ec1e-f15d-7a8d-9f0a-43a909551c1c -->
* **DOT-371 ansible python 3.14 pin expires when ansible ships core ≥2.22 meta-package**: DOT-371 (CLOSED 2026-11-17) pinned the ansible toolchain via the mise config (source home/dotfiles/apps/mise/files/config.toml → deployed ~/.config/mise/config.toml): python = "3.14", ansible meta-package pinning ansible-core to ~=2.21.6.
