The pin MUST live in SOURCE, not just the deployed config: update.sh upgrades ansible by rebuilding the venv, and without the source pin that rebuild follows the global python (3.15) → the dataclasses.\_is\_type crash returns (PR #182 landed the source pin). Verified end-to-end 2026-11-17: \`mise exec -- ansible-playbook --version\` green (core 2.21.6 on Python 3.14.8); \`run-role.sh opencode\` converged clean (user-run). Root cause reconfirmed 2026-11-17: the outage was the Python↔ansible version matrix (Python 3.15 removed dataclasses.\_is\_type, breaking ansible-core 2.21) — NOT deprecation noise; distinct fault class from DOT-340's injected-facts deprecation debt. The pin is CONDITIONAL: retire when the ansible meta-package allows core ≥2.22 (native Python 3.15 support); don't remove while the ~=2.21.6 coupling holds.

<!-- lore:01a1ce48-8d07-75a0-b99c-46bc8c9eed67 -->
* **DOT-57 coordination design: per-worktree index THE fix, frozen leases never call resolver**: DOT-57 (dotfiles backlog, To Do, labels now+blocked, priority high): one worktree per agent session with its OWN git index is THE fix for the mixed-commit contaminations (b3ad740, 5692a86, f5ac99f7) — rule-only enforcement has now failed three times, so do NOT add a prek hook as a competing fourth layer. One branch per task, subtasks as commits; .git read-only inside worktrees (Codex forces this); rerere on globally; protected-ref deny for anything irreversible. Lease design (author's settled position): resolver authors the expansion from topic.yml declarations, then the lease is FROZEN to a literal path list + metadata hash + resolver version + TTL — enforcement reads the frozen artifact and NEVER calls the resolver (removes the TOCTOU widening hole). Computation may only narrow; a literal deny-list (policy/egress/secrets/deployed tree/resolver code/lease store) outranks everything = the always-human-review tier. Labeled blocked — blocker unknown; check before queuing against it. The backlog/ carve-out (writes to main checkout) keeps sessions in the shared index and needs an answer WITHIN DOT-57's design, not beside it.

<!-- lore:01a1d20c-7305-73ba-8038-69ac6ad17fef -->
* **DOT-57 per-worktree index owns mixed-commits fix (b3ad740/5692a86/f5ac99f7); git commit -o interim**: DOT-57 owns the fix for contaminated main-checkout commits (b3ad740, 5692a86, f5ac99f7 — all landed on main, none in a worktree; the backlog/ carve-out is the one path that returns sessions to the shared index). Fix direction: per-worktree git index. Retro evidence accumulates IN DOT-57 (third data point f5ac99f7→4176bd27 added 2026-11-12 via 510c6f0a) rather than being forked into new tasks. Blocked label applies ONLY to sub-item (d), the deferred DAG scheduler (blocked on instrumenting 4 throughput numbers); the worktree-per-session core is unblocked. Interim mitigation: \`git commit -o \<paths>\` — commits exactly the named paths with 7 other sessions' changes sitting untouched beside them. A prek hook was REJECTED as mitigation: it would make the carve-out permanent by making it survivable. Related: \[\[01a1d13b-c063-756c-93cb-c7772eed1198]].

<!-- lore:01a1e0c7-ba33-7bd4-8af4-0c62dd82fb73 -->
* **DOT-57 session-state.sh declined: probe cost ~2% of context; summarizer discards needed classification**: Measured 2026-11-15 (dotfiles): git-status probe pair ≈290 tokens vs summarized session-state output 36 tokens → ~250 tokens saved per probe; 12+ occurrences → ~3k tokens ≈ 2% of a ~150k smart-zone window — real but minor. Dominant cost: git status prints 11 dirty paths from OTHER sessions every run, which must be re-read and re-classified to find your own 2 lines. A summarizer ('unstaged: 4, untracked: 7') discards the needed classification (which paths, whose work), so the assistant just re-runs full git status — the obvious fix doesn't fix it. Root instability: 7 other sessions write to the same checkout \[\[01a1db78-572c-7b5f-8f6a-812a1d4a471b]], so a 2-minute-old probe can't be trusted; worktree isolation removes the need to re-probe at all. Declined to queue session-state.sh; recorded as evidence on DOT-57. Revisit only if multi-session measurement shows 20–30% (not 2%) of context.

<!-- lore:01a1e0e6-a85a-7aa9-8197-815914e71c6c -->
* **DOT-57 worktree isolation ruled priority one; guard stands until isolation holds**: User ruling (2026-11-15): DOT-57 (worktree isolation) is priority one — the worktree-rule guard stands until isolation actually holds; DOT-57 is the root cause of three separate retro findings. The ruling records the user's reversal of the assistant's earlier decline, with reasoning preserved in the task so the override stays legible later. Assistant's supporting ranking: DOT-360 (owned instruction block) second as cheapest high-impact (one CLI command; blocks DOT-361/DOT-359), then DOT-275 (human-gated, blocks DOT-358); DOT-362/363/364 are parallelizable research. Related measurement precedent \[\[01a1e0c7-ba41-7edb-95a2-435bd5ee2d99]].

<!-- lore:01a1ebb8-3fb0-78a6-b1bb-b3d06e00a443 -->
* **DOT-59 ruled prek over pre-commit — patch-to-cache, never git stash**: DOT-59 ruled prek over pre-commit — patch-to-cache, never git stash: DOT-59 (closed 2026-11-08 via PR #146) ruled prek OVER pre-commit as the dotfiles hook runner: \`prek.toml\` at repo root, \`.pre-commit-config.yaml\` deleted, \`pipx:pre-commit\` removed from mise source and never re-added (gone from the deployed config since the 2026-11-17 apply, as predicted); the installed \`.git/hooks/pre-commit\` begins \`# File generated by prek\`. Deciding evidence: prek's unstaged-change mechanism is patch-to-cache (\`git write-tree\` → \`~/.cache/prek/patches/\` → apply-back), NOT \`git stash\` — hook runs never touch the shared \`refs/stash\`. The 'Unstaged changes detected... Temporarily saving them to ~/.cache/prek/patches/' lines during commits are prek working normally. Confirmed settled 2026-11-17: 30+ commits passed cleanly including two proportionality rejections — the mechanism doing its job. Do not re-litigate the runner choice when touching adjacent config (e.g. the opencode.jsonc mdformat block pointing at \`uvx mdformat\`).

<!-- lore:01a1ed3a-6a54-7af3-b9bf-41124b18aebc -->
* **dotfiles git topic: topic\_requires: \[] deliberately — Homebrew requirement would block Debian; packages in prerequisites.yaml**: home/dotfiles/apps/git/topic.yml (DOT-352) declares topic\_requires: \[] even though gita and Chrome declare git in their topic\_requires — git itself has NO requirement, notably not Homebrew. Chose \[] over a homebrew requirement because a brew dep would make the topic unschedulable on Debian (topic\_os: \[Darwin, Debian]); git is a checked system dependency with no mise backend. Package installation lives in prerequisites.yaml, NOT in managers' duplicated core-package lists (documented in the topic's comments). topic\_tier: app. Trap: adding topic\_requires: \[homebrew] to 'complete the dependency graph' looks right but silently makes the topic unschedulable on the Linux host. Verified deployed and clean via scoped apply 2026-11-17.

<!-- lore:01a18985-3bca-79f4-ab3d-2315b776cb60 -->
* **dotfiles worktree-per-task (DOT-247): agent/\<slug> branches, .worktrees naming; plugin-recovery saga resolved**: Routing rules (DOT-247, PR #91 'the main checkout stays on main'): task work runs in its own worktree + branch — never edit task files in the shared main checkout; backlog/ task-creation/coordination commits go straight to main (author ruling 2026-10-23, AGENTS.md:398); config changes land by PR with human review. Branch \`agent/\<slug>\`, worktrees \`.worktrees/\<slug>\` (writer subtasks \`...\_t\<nn>\_\<slug>\`). Run \`git merge-base --is-ancestor\` before reaping — but squash-merged branches read UNMERGED (check gh pr first). Never rebase/push the shared main checkout unilaterally — inspect, flag, await authorization. Plugin-recovery saga RESOLVED 2026-11-05. Queue-config state: checkActiveBranches:false (113a14ac) is a TEMPORARY DOT-353 repair for stale queue records on other sessions' branches shadowing current tasks; DOT-286 owns the scanner fix and safe re-enablement — do not close DOT-286 as moot. Known failure mode: all three cross-session commit contaminations (b3ad740, 5692a86, f5ac99f7) hit the backlog/ carve-out on main — DOT-57 \[\[01a1c814-a8e8-7c4b-b7d1-f3abbf5e432f]] owns the structural fix.

<!-- lore:01a1cdbc-b6e8-7dc7-af17-1f947b5519b3 -->
* **flagship GLOSSARY 'world' widened to any location; FS-1 collision review**: User decision (2026-11-11, flagship commit f445f3e): 'world' = ANY location, inside or outside the federation — membership is a property a world has, not a separate kind; 'Federation' now reads 'the worlds you govern'. Routing rule: a route can COMMIT at a member world, only PROPOSE (upstream PR/issue) at a non-member. Supersedes the earlier 'world (SNW)' = org-outside-federation framing. Backlog task FS-1 ('Review world vs location for domain collisions', To Do) will list collisions once the map schema and routes use both words, then decide merge/keep/split; user directive: re-review later if the widened term causes too many domain/conceptual collisions.

<!-- lore:01a1a9e2-e0f5-7ae8-a598-a040d9d1c94b -->
* **git notes: machine channel — one namespace per producer, never force-overwrite (refs/notes/ai)**: Git notes are the MACHINE channel (AGENTS.md § Commits, 2026-09-19 ruling): machines write, machines read; humans use \`git log\`/\`git notes show\`; one namespace per producer; never force-overwrite a notes ref. refs/notes/ai is the git-ai tooling's AI-session provenance trail (61 notes commits) — fetched as its own unowned namespace and left alone. commit-hygiene § Notes rules: never overwrite another producer's note — your own fresh note may be replaced, but foreign notes get \`git notes append\` or a question; \`git notes add\` failing when a note exists is the guardrail, not an obstacle to route around with -f. Repair pattern: a note can attach to any commit — e.g. a note recording that a commit's trailers' tests landed in a later commit repairs the audit trail without history rewrite (rewrite discouraged once pushed).

<!-- lore:01a1c31b-943f-796a-a80c-502715619d9e -->
* **gita bridge Round 3 (all yes): work-tree registrations machine-local; try/ declared; report-only discovery**: User-approved (2026-11-09, Q1–Q5 all yes): (1) manual gita work-tree registrations stay machine-local — never in the shared repos.yaml catalogue, never 'unaccounted', same safe-sync behavior. (2) \`try/worktrunk-isolation-trial\` is declared in the local machine registry and gita-registered — machine-specific ≠ unsynced; remote discovery may ignore try/ later but this clone stays managed. (3) Discovery is report-only in Ansible — no interactive prompts; gita install/upgrade report unregistered clones and registry drift, declaration changes happen via user YAML edit + next install; \`redge-cli update\` later supplies interactive reconciliation. (4) Routine sync stays quiet: clean fleet prints a short summary, per-repo detail on demand. (5) First VM-backed verification slice uses synthetic local remotes — proves clean clone fast-forwards, dirty clone fetched-unchanged, failed fetch reported, work-tree registrations survive; NOT GitHub discovery or auto-cloning. Boundary confirmed: DOT-306 resolves paths, DOT-173 safe sync, redge-cli owns CRUD — no new subsystem.

<!-- lore:01a1ae28-8141-718e-b56b-6702f9d20a35 -->
* **gita debian-lima scenario: approved design — registration-only slice, local git-bundle seeding**: User-confirmed 2026-11-05 design for apps/gita/molecule/debian-lima (DOT-289): first slice registration-only; the observable sync case lands separately (DOT-173). Seed repos by copying ordinary in-branch fixture files into the VM and running git init/commit inside — NO network clone (local/portable is the goal); sync slice uses git bundles cloned from a local bundle path; recreate linked worktrees in-VM (host-specific .git pointer paths). Rejected: bare remote + clone — unnecessary when seeding locally. Reusable capture = short scenario contract + gita reference implementation, NOT new runner code (Lima adapter has two consumers: apps/lima and apps/loreai). Layout: fixtures/ tracked, .molecule/ ignored disposable state — deleting the tree cleans all VM artifacts. Status 2026-11-09: PR #92 (catalogue re-point) CLOSED unmerged; gita registration landed via PRs #152/#156/#169; the VM scenario remains UNIMPLEMENTED — new work goes to a fresh task + stacked worktree, never PR #92. Caveat: Debian base image still downloads; fully offline VM creation is a separate caching concern.

<!-- lore:01a183b0-c92c-7188-b99c-6173106ec81a -->
* **herdr shell/cmd fallbacks author-ratified — 6 CLI + 3 probe floor, stat swap done**: Author ruling 2026-09-19 (commit b223887): the herdr topic's 14 command/shell sites are ratified — do NOT re-ask the author or 'improve' them; the ladder's ask-before-shell rule \[\[01a183ae-4bd0-7d84-a268-a51e7f1b3f6f]] is already satisfied here. Floor: 6 CLI invocations (herdr integration install/status/uninstall, herdr --version, herdr status server, mise use/unuse/upgrade — the CLIs ARE the interface; a wrapper module just moves the exec with no idempotence gain) + 3 probe classes where no module exists: grep content-probe (find needs globs, lineinfile mutates, slurp+jinja is more machinery), command -v (POSIX-preferred, roles/topic-resolution precedent — \`which\` is the same class), systemctl --user is-active (systemd\_service has no query op; state: started would mutate). The one module-able probe (systemctl --user cat) was swapped to ansible.builtin.stat on the unit file. Ratification comments live in install.yaml/remove.yaml. Topic: \[\[01a183ac-3478-716a-a4b8-101d35e1688e]].

<!-- lore:01a1e2ce-0974-7e2c-b1fd-4581fea5dbc9 -->
* **inbox skill: /inbox parks thoughts as backlog drafts (-l inbox); stage-1 loop edits Why/Ship/Constraints only; stage-2 ask-matt**: User's capture skill (2026-11-15 grill, six decisions settled): \`/inbox \<thought>\` (renamed from \`/park\` — user rejected park) in any session/repo writes the sentence as ONE backlog draft labelled \`inbox\`, commits it, replies 'parked as draft-N', no discussion. It's a skill, not a habit: a skill tells the session to RECORD the message, not act on it. Authored in ai-prompt-library, installed everywhere via dotagents \[\[01a18191-bc1a-70f9-9a28-abbefdee708d]]; repos without a backlog need a fallback (settled in the skill's own grill; fallback name \`/jot\`). Stage-1 loop edits drafts into Why/Ship/Constraints shape ONLY — barred from promoting, deleting, merging, or splitting drafts (promotion is a user decision \[\[01a1e1f5-8af5-7eb3-82f8-29b4073556fd]]). Stage 2 reuses the ask-matt flow. On-demand before scheduled \[\[01a1e1f5-8af4-7c97-a90f-e68fe3c8c680]]; model = current session model \[\[01a1e1f5-8ac8-70d3-ad2d-c05bc23b9a71]]. NEVER name the loop 'triage' — \`/triage\` is ask-matt's (issues arriving from outside).

<!-- lore:01a1d215-7b8d-76a1-9faa-67e775c07424 -->
* **Incident post-mortems: decision record type, references: field, AGENTS.md retirement gap**: Ruling (2026-11-12, extends decision-3 item 4 \[\[01a1c67b-dcfb-73a0-8fea-396e798d598d]]): incidents are captured as existing backlog-py \`decision\` records — an ADR whose context is a failure — NOT a new record type. Rejected alternative: backlog-py has four types (task, draft, decision, doc, plus milestone); a new type would need its own lifecycle, CLI verb, and queue semantics, duplicating \`decision\`'s Context/Decision/Consequences shape. decision-3 is the worked example. Remediation linkage: the \`references:\` frontmatter field links tasks to the decision (7 live instances: DOT-271–276, DOT-289). Two open gaps: (1) DOT-275 'define what counts as an incident' is deliberately open — decision-3 item 4 requires the user's code/design review before capture is mechanical; it is the user's blocker, not agent work. (2) AGENTS.md has no retirement path — rules are welded inline to incident evidence; when every linked remediation task reaches Done, the decision is resolvable and the AGENTS.md entry collapses to the imperative rule plus one link to the decision record (forensics stay in the decision; lessons persist in .lore.md).

<!-- lore:01a183dd-81b1-77c8-a077-79d570d02905 -->
* **LEDGER bypassed(reason) provisional + auditor re-quiz sampled from ledger**: Two LEDGER design decisions, author-answered 2026-09-22 (shift-left wrap-up): (1) Bypass state — schema carries \`bypassed(reason)\` alongside ran/failed so a \`--no-verify\` push is recorded, not hidden. Explicitly PROVISIONAL (author: 'we're still building and experimenting… this isn't wrong but I don't know yet') — a field, not settled policy on when bypassing is acceptable; revisit once real bypass cases accumulate. The field must exist before the first bypass or agents learn to hide theirs. (2) Auditor-of-the-auditor — periodic re-quiz sampled from the ledger (shuf over the trailing window; a trigger one-liner, not a build): pick one merged item, answer COLD without re-reading quiz notes — still understand it? still approve it? Outcome recorded (still-understand / drifted / would-not-approve-now); 'drifted' is a review-depth signal about the author's quiz depth, NOT an incident against the agent — never grounds to relitigate the merge. Cadence starts light (1 per ~10 merges or monthly, less frequent wins); it sits on the judgment track \[\[01a183db-8129-71f3-b676-8946d2c2f3ef]], so it earns tighter cadence over time.

<!-- lore:01a183df-2a28-7c0b-a704-4aa206c6fe45 -->
* **Molecule VM testing: Molecule CLI via run-molecule.sh; both-host matrix; shared templates**: pytest-ansible self-deprecated at v26.9.0 (EOL Dec 2026); pytest-molecule archived — the wrapper chain dead-ends at the Molecule CLI, no pytest wrapper. Scenarios live at apps/\<topic>/molecule/\<scenario>/, invoked via \`MOLECULE\_SCENARIO=debian scripts/run-molecule.sh lima test\`; harness pinned by tests/run\_molecule/; pytest-testinfra remains the standard verifier. Verify tool claims against the GitHub repo, not PyPI metadata. Author requirement (DOT-255): tests must run on BOTH machines (Mac ARM64 + Linux AMD64); macOS-guest-on-Linux disqualifies a provider; unsupported combos fail visibly, never silently reduce coverage. Emerging design: Vagrant+libvirt as common adapter for Linux guests (hvf on Mac, KVM on Linux); rejected Tart/Vetu (no Molecule adapter), Docker (not VM/systemd proof). Performance explicitly not a priority — accuracy and trustworthiness are. Extract shared VM-test templates now that lima + loreai are two real consumers (two-adapter rule); weigh chezmoi deployment constraints.

<!-- lore:01a1cdbc-b6fe-71e3-b5c7-d64bb2a770c1 -->
* **multi-repo-runner research reframed: redge-centric, redge-cli replaces gita**: User decision (2026-11-11): redge-cli will REPLACE gita (the gita bridge is interim). The multi-repo-runner research ticket flips from 'which tool should we adopt?' to 'what should redge learn or borrow from gita, mani, multi-gitter, git-xargs?' — measured against redge's own SPEC (docs/research/repo-manager/SPEC.md). push-it-forward belongs to the same research (multi-gitter/git-xargs propose changes across many repos). Backstage research must also answer: is redge's declarative catalogue + named groups the galaxy map itself, or something the map reads from? Chose redge-centric framing over tool adoption because redge is the user's own purpose-built tool with a binding spec.

<!-- lore:01a18b81-0cff-7ceb-90a6-87ef9f7010d1 -->
* **OpenCode V2 LSP restore: christensenjairus plugin + AGENTS.md over MCP bridge**: Evaluated 2026-10-29 after V2 dropped the LSP runtime; user approved proceeding. Corrected premise (2026-10-30): LSP does NOT cut token spend or re-reads — the agent still reads files to edit and LSP adds per-call tool cost; token reduction lives in compaction config \[\[01a18d52-57cc-7a02-8602-facceb7b0d92]]. LSP is worth adding ONLY for diagnostics + semantic navigation. Ranked: (1) AGENTS.md lint/typecheck commands — deterministic floor; gives up navigation and per-file incremental diagnostics but NOT correctness (LSP diagnostics derive from the same compilers: tsc --noEmit, go build ./...); (2) christensenjairus/opencode-v2-lsp-support plugin — lsp tool (definitions/references/hover/symbols) + diagnostics on write/edit; (3) STRd6/mcp-language-server bridge — the one genuine navigation token-saver (precise snippets vs grep-and-read). Chose stacking 1+2: client-agnosticism unneeded, MCP tools cost context. rename\_symbol is LSP's only atomic-edit win; atomic-change quality is otherwise instructions/review-skill territory. User's primary languages still needed to wire concrete commands/servers.

<!-- lore:01a19f9a-f61d-7681-b923-eb34ff13c8b9 -->
* **OpenRouter API key never written down; shell-start resolution, probe no\_log**: Standing security decision (restated across 8+ sessions): the OpenRouter API key is never written down in the repo or the rendered env file — it is resolved at shell start by the env file, and secrets stay template-owned. The loreai openrouter probe (\`llm keys get openrouter\`) runs with no\_log: true and only its exit code is consumed, so the key never appears in Ansible output or logs. Any new code touching the key must preserve both properties: no literal key in committed files, and no\_log on anything that could echo it.

<!-- lore:01a1e149-8ce4-7913-bfe0-21064f52a888 -->
* **pif drives, copier-cat supplies lineage — back-submission boundary; service-vs-capability now a pif design question**: User-specified division (2026-11-15): pif owns moving a finding to another repository — prepare, publish, track the response, queue the migration — for ANY target, related or not; copier-cat owns only what's unique to the child-parent relationship (which template stamped which project, therefore which template should receive a given lesson). Why the boundary holds: a submission to an unrelated repo needs no inheritance graph, and a hoist can't choose a parent without one — the graph already exists upstream (CCC-19 records lineage, CCC-23 walks it). Service-vs-capability is thereby narrowed to a pif design question, not a boundary question — either shape can consume copier-cat's lineage. User's cost argument: a per-repo capability multiplies across ten-plus repos; a service is written once. Extends decision-5 \[\[01a1e134-23bc-7e74-a4d9-dcf0476d796f]].

<!-- lore:01a1db63-a540-7337-8208-94987e1f072f -->
* **Post-mortem flow sequencing: DOT-275 reframe → decision retirement lifecycle → skill last**: Proposed queue (pending user approval; items 2–3 blocked on 1): (1) DOT-275 reframed as a decision procedure — design review with the user, the hard part; (2) retirement lifecycle — a decision record gains a resolution condition: when every task carrying its references: reaches Done, the decision resolves and the AGENTS.md rule it produced collapses to the imperative plus a link, forensics staying in the decision; mechanically checkable by walking references: and checking statuses; (3) the skill itself, last — a skill is a refactor of a flow that works, not a way to discover it; writing it first would encode a flow nobody has run end to end. Belongs in ai-prompt-library/skills/ as spec + evals per the backlog-task precedent; description routes on store type and repo shape so it declines cleanly elsewhere. Matches the recommendations-as-tickets workflow \[\[01a1d8a4-3b37-734f-ad5b-f6c25bda859f]].

<!-- lore:01a1e0e6-a85f-77ce-8097-1206336149b3 -->
* **push-it-forward: push project conclusions upstream to parent templates**: User goal (2026-11-15): many of this project's conclusions should be pushed upstream to the parent project templates — requires a sane way to contribute feedback and changes to other projects, eventually refactored into a mechanism named 'push-it-forward'. Open design idea: a stakeholders/customers field on tasks and decisions so it's clear who else needs to know and stay in the loop. Extends the upstream-conventions rule \[\[01a1db6f-617c-7ebd-8f23-3ecd7c8c6e70]] (APT-49): upstream submissions always use the receiving project's own conventions, never this repo's vocabulary.

<!-- lore:01a18498-a914-71b5-b077-336233dff191 -->
* **ruff standard enforced (PR #33): 88 cols, Google docstrings, W505@72 grandfather; prek ruff hooks**: Repo-wide ruff standard (PR #33, merged 2026-10-01): ruff defaults + line-length 88 with ruff format (black-equivalent), Google docstrings, W505 doc-line at 72, missing-docstring rules unselected. Enforced by prek hooks \`ruff-check\` + \`ruff-format\` (hook id is ruff-check; \`ruff\` is the legacy alias). W505/D205 are GRANDFATHERED per-directory (author chose grandfathering over a big-bang doc-line reformat); the list shrinks as files are touched. E501 (88) is NEVER grandfathered — wrap instead. NEW test dirs are NOT grandfathered — rewrap doc-lines to 72 (tests/uv/, then tests/hooks/ 2026-10-07); never add to the grandfather list. Gotchas: .bats/ is a vendored submodule, excluded (PR #39); dotfiles/apps/zim vendored app excluded. Pyrefly layer: \[\[01a184f3-0e58-719f-89d6-e29538ffd82e]].

<!-- lore:01a183da-0027-70b1-bd54-527ccd410f5c -->
* **Shift-left testing decisions (closed) + llm\_judge opt-in mechanics**: Shift-left study CLOSED; organizing principle: funnel of trust — commit-tier target <5s at full trust; order: repeatability → quality → speed → efficiency → tradeoffs. Settled: three-tier gates (fast hooks at commit; full suite as MERGE-gate for non-docs diffs, not pre-push; llm\_judge named-excluded); machine-shaped gate outputs (exit code + file:line + JSON); \`collected == 0 ⇒ exit 1\` tripwire; pytest-randomly; $HOME-sandbox autouse fixture gating xdist (full suite -n auto ~47s vs ~170-200s serial); skip census over coverage %; fix the slowest tests first. Rejected: pytest-testmon/picked (blind to shell/YAML change surface), coverage % (skip bodies count as covered), full suite on pre-push, standing cosmic-ray/mutmut. llm\_judge mechanics: pyproject addopts \`-m not llm\_judge\`; run via \`pytest -m llm\_judge\` with \`uv run --frozen --group test-llm\`; the \`llm\` CLI needs the llm-ollama plugin (models addressed \`ollama/...\`). CI runs \`--group test\` only, so llm\_judge tests never run in CI.

<!-- lore:01a1ea75-34d9-7ebc-a485-bd25b83712ba -->
* **skillify becomes a workflow; copier-cat owns the agent-package Copier template**: Grilling decisions (2026-11-11): skillify is a WORKFLOW, not one overloaded skill (user: 'perhaps skillify is really a workflow? are we pushing too much into the scope of a single skill?') — it orchestrates discovery, artifact selection, validation, and distribution; Skillet remains the leaf for ordinary skill specs (user has ideas for a better skillet later). Coverage: skills, agents, workflows, optional plugin adapters — not every protocol artifact at once. Standards win over installed tooling on conflict, but with first-class dotagents support. Template location: copier-cat, one separate repo per template in the stack; 'Copier is the generator; skillify calls it' (approved). alint checks on generated packages: required files, reference integrity, declared policy, manifest consistency. Proof bar: real isolated global AND project dotagents installs plus selected runtime projections. Packages follow the dotagentsprotocol.com .agents standard; portable authoring stays separate from vendor-specific runtime packaging (OpenCode adapter vendor-specific, optional). Three-session split: template build; skillify update; wrap-up skills authoring.

<!-- lore:01a1ed66-7d27-7339-8c01-40847f9c3d0c -->
* **Skillify redesign: orchestrator over Copier-cat generator; package ownership unsettled**: Settled (2026-11-17): skillify becomes a small authoring workflow — one orchestrator + focused helpers. Ordinary skill creation stays delegated to Skillet (npx -y @sentry/skillet@latest — never replace its lifecycle). Copier-cat owns generation: skillify calls its public command, NO second template engine. alint validates structure/manifest/declared policy/secrets only — never semantic quality; Skillet stays the semantic authority (alint is no substitute for behavior evals or install proof). Initial artifacts: skills, agents, workflows, explicitly requested adapters (defer memories, scheduled tasks, model config). Prohibitions: no TypeScript-plugin-everything, no second semantic evaluator or parallel state DB, no per-artifact-type workflows, no auto-invoking user-invoked skills, no silent commit/push/install. OPEN: package ownership (one-repo-per-package vs plugin monorepo vs ai-prompt-library inclusion) — earlier inclination (local repo per package) NOT settled; research primary sources, recommend ONE model with disqualifying reasons.

<!-- lore:01a19890-6247-7316-b732-2e63635b2701 -->
* **Switched from ~/.local/share/chezmoi/AGENTS.md to ~/code/personal/AGENTS**: switched from ~/.local/share/chezmoi/AGENTS.md to ~/code/personal/AGENTS.

<!-- lore:01a183dd-81b5-74c6-b1eb-d58e746ae7ec -->
* **Tests never touch real $HOME — mock keychain, avoid zsh -l; reopen trigger queued**: Author decision 2026-09-22 (shift-left wrap-up): NO test may touch real $HOME for now — find a workaround for every case (mock the macOS keychain, avoid \`zsh -l\` login shells) rather than naming exceptions, because the real edge cases aren't known yet ('we're still building and experimenting'). The $HOME-sandbox autouse fixture (HOME/XDG\_\*/cwd → tmp\_path) is the enforcement vehicle \[\[01a183db-8121-707f-8f1a-68a75e528dd3]]. A reopen-trigger task is queued in TASKS.md § Planned: revisit the exception list once the fixture has been exercised and real potential issues accumulate — when the list exists, it doubles as the audit trail. macOS cases that genuinely resist sandboxing (keychain, dscl, login shell) are the expected first candidates.

<!-- lore:01a183db-8129-71f3-b676-8946d2c2f3ef -->
* **Two-track trust model: deterministic seen-to-fail vs judgment correction-window (LEDGER schema)**: Settled with the author 2026-09-22 (shift-left questioning round; recorded in TASKS.md 'Build the LEDGER'): trust attaches to the MECHANISM, not the commit or session — earned trust is recorded once and referenced everywhere, so proven patterns transfer instead of re-earning per commit (prek hooks and tests graduate fast; frequently-uncorrected skills climb stages). Two tracks: (1) Deterministic (prek, pytest exit codes, ruff/ty) — entry gate = seen-to-fail once against a real injected defect, graduation = short clean streak, trusted permanently until the mechanism changes or a false-negative appears. (2) Judgment (agent code-gen, skill output, LLM-judge verdicts) — no binary fail; trust level moves with a rolling correction-rate window (~last 10–20 uses), never static. Rejected: one funnel applying a single evidence type to everything — HITL-ratio belongs only to the judgment track, streaks only to the deterministic track. Cross-ref: \[\[01a183da-0027-70b1-bd54-527ccd410f5c]]; feeds the agent→function graduation policy.

### Gotcha

<!-- lore:01a18498-a911-79b5-883b-d13f3cb8b408 -->
* **~/.local/bin/uv astral install shadows mise uv — deliberate, load-bearing; topic owns uv self update**: RESOLVED 2026-10-01: \`uv self update\` ran (0.11.29 → 0.12.21), clearing the 'Required uv version >=0.12.17' gate. The astral install at ~/.local/bin/uv remains DELIBERATE and load-bearing: docs/REFERENCES.md records the deviation from mise's cookbook; it shadows mise's uv, and mise's pipx: backend rides the SYSTEM uvx (aider, llm, pyrefly) — deleting it breaks pipx-backed tools, not just the PATH shadow. Going forward the apps/uv topic \[\[01a184af-69d4-7f24-aa7e-dd9edb32ebc3]] owns \`uv self update\` (install + upgrade tasks). Standing diagnostic: when every tool call fails, check cwd AND uv version before assuming lost work (second cause of the 2026-09-28 'would not run at all' session; first: deleted worktree cwd \[\[01a18498-a90c-7b82-859d-e6c727e7fab2]]).

<!-- lore:01a1d838-19dd-7928-8f5e-1d73522799de -->
* **agentic-project-template \`layer\` on empty dir silently skips base; CLI wraps copier.run\_copy**: agentic-project-template is a thin Python CLI over Copier's API — rendering is Copier (\`copier.run\_copy\`); the CLI adds ordering, two answers files, and four shared-file merges (mise.toml deep-merge, prek.toml repo-list, .gitignore append, AGENTS.md marked-section upsert). Trap: \`layer --non-interactive\` on an EMPTY directory exits green ('agentic layer stamped') but produces agent wiring with no Python package — \`\_maybe\_ask\_base()\` in src/agentic\_project\_template/cli.py early-returns on non-interactive OR empty dir, so the base-template prompt is skipped twice over; it reads as success. Fix: for a new project use \`new --base gh:bengineerdavis/python-project-template\` (stamps base → layer → merges; refuses non-empty dirs — use copier-cat adopt for existing repos). Plain \`copier copy\` twice works for empty dirs EXCEPT the layer's mise.toml overwrites the base's — the exact case the CLI exists for.

<!-- lore:01a1a26f-62b7-70fe-a00f-fcfef7754f20 -->
* **agentsview unpriced models (custom\_override\_count: 0) hide cost — gpt-6.1-sol-pro, kimi-k3.1-pro**: agentsview leaves 8 models unpriced (custom\_override\_count: 0), including openai/gpt-6.1-sol-pro — unpriced models are invisible in its cost breakdown, which is why a $26.33 line (628 turns, 67.2M cache-read tokens, ~29× deepseek-flash-latest's per-token cost) went unnoticed. kimi-k3.1-pro ($20.49 on 130 turns) is selected at runtime — not configured in any repo file, so it cannot be found by grepping configs. Fix: add pricing overrides in agentsview for unpriced models before trusting its cost report; treat a missing model row as 'unpriced', not 'free'.

<!-- lore:01a19843-935f-7fd4-9eee-e9d4099de7e0 -->
* **ai-prompt-library mdformat hook auto-reformats markdown — first run fails, re-stage and re-commit**: Trap: the ai-prompt-library pre-commit's mdformat hook FAILS on first run over new markdown because it auto-reformats files in place ('files were modified by this hook') — reads as a content lint error, but the markdown may already be fine. Fix: inspect the diff, \`git add\` the reformatted files, re-commit; second run passes (verified 2026-11-01). Why the wrong approach seems right: a FAILED hook line reads as a rule your content violates, when mdformat is an auto-fixer whose failure IS the fix being applied. Sibling hooks behave the same way (trailing-whitespace, end-of-file, codespell-fix); yaml/toml/json checks skip when no matching files exist.

<!-- lore:01a1c2bd-6c6c-7701-84d6-9b0f524b0ff2 -->
* **alint test policy fixtures: one case per YAML document; capabilities field required for command rules**: Two fixture-format traps in \`alint test\`: (1) each YAML file must contain exactly ONE policy case — multiple cases in one file fail with parse\_error "deserializing from YAML containing more than one document is not supported", aborting the whole run (exit 2, validation\_aborted). Trap: a multi-case file looks natural since cases share rule declarations; fix: split into one file per case (e.g. invalid-skill.yml, spec-only.yml, stale-skill.yml, skills.yml). (2) A command-rule case must declare the spawn capability under \`capabilities:\` (per the shipped e2e examples); a bare top-level \`spawn:\` key is an unknown-field parse error. Valid case fields: test\_version, name, tags, capabilities, given, when, expect, expect\_tree, snapshot, docs. Deliberate-failure cases expect exit policy\_failure; a passing check alone proves nothing — also observe the intended rejection.

<!-- lore:01a1c2bd-6c6f-7c2f-9186-60277e79933a -->
* **alint via mise shim: 'No version is set for shim: alint' — use explicit mise exec**: Running alint through its mise shim fails with "No version is set for shim: alint" (exit 1) when no global default version exists. Trap: \`mise use -g github:asamarts/alint@\<ver>\` looks like the fix, but it mutates the user's global tool config. Fix: invoke explicitly scoped \`mise exec github:asamarts/alint@\<ver> -- alint ...\`, which works without touching global config. Bonus: the shim also refuses to run against an untrusted (freshly stamped) project's mise.toml — the explicit \`mise exec TOOL@VERSION\` form bypasses that trust requirement while preserving mise's trust boundary. Installed binary path: ~/.local/share/mise/installs/github-asamarts-alint/\<ver>/alint.

<!-- lore:01a1ebc6-f291-7c75-b967-4b5979cf57ed -->
* **Ansible full-string {{ }} preserves native type; import when short-circuits undefined register**: Two verified Ansible behaviors that ruled out hypothesized bugs (2026-11-17, DOT-348 review): (1) when the ENTIRE template string is a single {{ }} expression, Ansible preserves the native Python type — create: "{{ bool\_var }}" receives a real bool; even if stringified, blockinfile's boolspec coerces 'True'/'False' to bool, so it works either way. (2) A skipped task's register does NOT crash a later when: conditions on inherited import\_tasks evaluate with short-circuit, stopping at the first false condition — later conditions referencing the skipped register are never evaluated. Also verified: Ansible templating runs with trim\_blocks/lstrip\_blocks enabled (clean blockinfile render); \`list | to\_json\` renders valid TOML; in Jinja, filters bind tighter than comparison ((list|length) > 0).

<!-- lore:01a18391-e057-7f0f-9a90-1410783642bc -->
* **Ansible Jinja: reject() after + concatenation binds to last term only; filter is intersect**: Two traps hit while wiring the vscode extension loop (install.yaml/remove.yaml). (1) Jinja precedence: in \`{{ a + b | reject('in', ignored) }}\` the reject binds ONLY to the last term b — the main list passes through unfiltered, silently. Looks right because the filter reads as applying to the whole expression; fix: parenthesize the full concatenation before the filter. (2) The set filter is \`intersect\`, not \`intersection\` — 'No filter named' surfaces only when the template actually renders, so a --check --tags remove run (not just provision/install) is what catches it.

<!-- lore:01a1ec2a-e3e4-7ae0-8d27-d36a6578be63 -->
* **ansible self-upgrade mid-run → module\_utils skew 'Unknown profile name'**: Trap: upgrading ansible inside an ansible run looks harmless — it's just a mise/pip operation on a tool already on PATH. Fix: ansible cannot safely upgrade itself mid-run; swapping its own venv causes ansible-core module\_utils skew → 'Unknown profile name'. That is why executable\_update.sh upgrades ansible FIRST out-of-band (\`mise upgrade --yes pipx:ansible\`, line 67) before any playbook runs (line 78). Removing or reordering that pre-step (e.g. moving the upgrade into a playbook task) reintroduces the failure. Operates on the same mise pipx:ansible toolchain as the DOT-371 python 3.14 pin \[\[01a1ec1e-f15d-7a8d-9f0a-43a909551c1c]].

<!-- lore:01a1ec9b-2992-7f95-a0b2-aef7b290e364 -->
* **ansible-lint blind to injected-facts deprecation (RFE #4857)**: Trap: reaching for ansible-lint as the injected-facts deprecation detector looks right — it's the standard Ansible linting tool and this failure class is exactly what linters are for. Fix: it cannot see this class — verified against installed 26.11.0 (\`--strict\` passes a bare-fact play clean); RFE chain #1611 → #3457 → #4857 open since 2021. pytest-ansible and molecule are runtime harnesses with no deprecation gate. Community practice is hand-rolled CI grep gates on warning output — DOT-340's plan matches the converged practice. Revisit trigger (recorded in DOT-340): if ansible-lint ever ships the injected-facts rule (#4857/#1611), the static scan can retire — check before re-deriving.

<!-- lore:01a1ad33-7726-7540-a49a-3daf50328c29 -->
* **Ansible: when-guarded set\_fact feeding unguarded loop raises AnsibleUndefinedVariable on skip path**: Trap: guarding a set\_fact with \`when: \<probe>.stat.exists\` while a downstream task loops the variable unguarded looks right because the happy path works — but on machines where the probe fails the variable is never defined and the loop template raises AnsibleUndefinedVariable, aborting the play (hit in apps/gita install.yaml: gita\_work\_registrations undefined on machines without ~/code/work, contradicting the documented 'skip the whole reconciliation'). Fix: drop the when from the set\_fact (a slurp with \`| default('', true)\` yields an empty list when skipped) or guard the loop with \`| default(\[])\`. Test-fixture corollary: a fixture that ALWAYS creates the optional path (run\_isolated\_install always mkdirs code/work) never exercises the skip path — add an isolated fixture for the absent case. Note: register on an empty loop yields results: \[] on ansible-core 2.21.5 — no undefined hazard there.

<!-- lore:01a1dae2-cfc6-7ac1-8318-a01952e83c61 -->
* **backlog --remove-ac renumbers acceptance-criteria IDs mid-sequence**: Trap: editing a backlog task's acceptance criteria with sequential \`--remove-ac\` calls renumbers the remaining AC IDs after each removal — planned removals/replacements keyed to ORIGINAL IDs hit the wrong criteria (hit during CC-7 cleanup, 2026-11-14: intended old criteria weren't replaced; IDs shifted instead). Why it seems right: AC IDs read as stable identifiers, and a single removal works fine. Fix: remove in descending ID order, or re-list the task's ACs after each removal before the next; for replacements, edit content in place rather than remove+append. CC-7's acceptance IDs still need cleanup during implementation planning.

<!-- lore:01a1e1c4-73c6-7084-bb13-644cd08e4a98 -->
* **backlog board 'In Progress' statuses go stale — verify active workers before trusting**: Trap: a task marked 'In Progress' reads as active work because the board is the task source of truth — but the status field persists after its session ends. Observed 2026-11-15 in agentic-project-template: six 'In Progress' tasks had no current worker or claim (APT-42/43/44/47 had worktree implementations and draft PRs but nobody active); only APT-34 had live workers. Why it seems right: multi-session parallel work means the status field lags reality, and nothing clears it automatically. Fix: before scheduling or reporting board state, cross-check active workers/claims and worktree/PR activity rather than trusting the status field; reconcile statuses when a session ends.

<!-- lore:01a1989b-6570-7131-80f3-fdbfa7a97675 -->
* **backlog task stale state: stale premise + stale orchestration lease mislead**: Two stale-state traps in backlog task files. (1) Premise staleness: the description reads as current state but can be weeks stale — before flagging collision or re-planning, read implementation notes and updated-at, which supersede the premise (TASK-230: description claimed machines.yml never adopted; notes recorded PR #79 merged 61bdcc6). An anomalous in-progress status is an inspection trigger, not automatically a hands-off claim. (2) Lease staleness: a task finished in a prior session can retain a stale orchestration claim plus an \`orchestration\` frontmatter block (TASK-1: Nov 10 lease still present at Nov 12 closure). On closure, release the claim and strip the block so the file reads like other done tasks (TASK-230 shape). Why it misleads: 'in-progress with a lease' reads as a live claim under the coordination rule.

<!-- lore:01a1c67d-b137-7bfa-8639-c8ab0b614b3b -->
* **backlog-md-py config set prefixes.task writes flat key (fork bug, unfiled)**: Bug in bengineerdavis/backlog-md-py (user's fork of rmusser01's upstream): \`config set prefixes.task\` writes a flat key instead of nesting under \[prefixes]. Deliberately NOT filed — filing in the fork requires explicit user approval (report + offer first); never file against rmusser01's upstream without asking. Relevant whenever template stamping must propagate the task\_prefix answer into backlog config.

<!-- lore:01a1ce48-8d12-791a-9152-b63a820b5890 -->
* **backlog-py autoCommit=false — MCP writes files, can't cause or fix commit contamination**: backlog-py MCP capability facts: autoCommit=false (writes task files to the working tree, leaves staging/committing to the session) and bypassGitHooks=false (respects hooks when it touches git). Trap: 'just let the MCP handle it' seems like it would prevent commit contamination because the MCP writes the files — but contamination is a property of WHERE commits are made (the shared main-checkout index under the backlog/ carve-out), not of who wrote the file. Turning autoCommit on would still commit from the shared index, so it neither causes nor fixes the hazard. In-between option if wanted: a backlog-py feature to commit queue writes from a dedicated worktree (adjacent to DOT-328 git-aware task-id allocation) — not a dotfiles hook.

<!-- lore:01a1e001-e9b7-7b39-8c6a-f0a73b77e67a -->
* **backlog-py claims invisible across worktrees — expired claim leaves task eligible in main queue**: Trap (retro finding 2026-11-15, high severity): task claims are per-worktree and invisible to the main queue — CCC-24 showed eligible in the main queue while its worktree (branch ccc-24-python-assembly) held an expired local claim; another agent could have duplicated the implementation. Why it seems right: the queue's eligible/todo status reads as authoritative global state. Fix: before implementing, check the task's worktree for an existing claim and unlanded commits; on cleanup, release an expired claim ONLY after verifying zero implementation commits/source changes (CCC-24 verified clean → claim removed, back to eligible/0 progress). Follow-up: add this scenario to APL-26's evaluation cases and the backlog-workflow adapter — session-end claim release + abandoned-worktree checks. Related worktree-ambiguity gotcha: \[\[01a1db78-572c-7b5f-8f6a-812a1d4a471b]].

<!-- lore:01a1d206-7b3b-7a42-b538-f857e9c3c119 -->
* **backlog-py CLI: \`doc\` not \`document\`; board/browser double as review queue**: backlog-py is a Python compatibility clone of Backlog.md. Commands: agents, board, browser, cleanup, compat, completion, config, daemon, decision, doc, doctor, draft, init, integration, milestone, orchestration, overview, search, task, tui. Trap: \`document\` looks plausible (sibling command 'decision') but errors 'No such command' — the command is \`doc\`. Board export flags: --force, --readme, --export-version. Design consequence (2026-11-12, research repo): backlog-py already provides searchable documents, task browsing, and a loopback browser — reuse board/browser as the research review queue instead of building a custom review application.

<!-- lore:01a1cda5-1a1a-75df-a0c7-63011bddda1b -->
* **backlog-py CLI: task create shape, verb traps, doctor duplicate-id resolution**: Tasks are created ONLY via CLI: \`uvx --from backlog-md-py backlog-py task create "\<title>" --no-dod-defaults\` — never hand-written. Description shape: Why (facts only) / Ship (one deliverable per bullet) / Constraints; one acceptance criterion per Ship bullet, one per verification command; the AC block is the single done source; DOD stays empty; never restate ACs in the description. Verb traps: \`task show \<id>\` exits 2 — the viewer is bare \`task \<task-id>\`; also task list/create/edit/archive/demote/renumber. \`backlog-py doctor\` reports task files no command can read and ids claimed by several files — both drop silently during normal reads, so a clean \`task list\` proves nothing. Resolution: FIRST candidate wins (completed/ beats tasks/, DOT-108); exits non-zero while anything is wrong (usable as CI check). Run doctor before trusting queue state.

<!-- lore:01a1db6f-6181-7ea0-9386-c8b1bdc7eba1 -->
* **backlog-py duplicate task IDs shadow silently — APT-32 collision**: Trap: when two task files share an ID (2026-11-14: agentic-project-template has two apt-32 files — 'Keep-task-branches-synchronized-with-main' and 'Enforce-the-worktree-rule-at-commit-time'), the resolver picks ONE file and silently ignores the other; only repeated 'duplicate task id' warnings during task capture reveal it. Why it seems right: task creation is file-only (autoCommit: false), so a duplicate reads as harmless noise, and the surviving task looks complete. Fix: treat the warning as a real defect — reconcile the collision through the files' owners before claiming any reconciliation complete (APT-49 constraint); verify unique IDs across ALL worktrees before writing \[\[01a1d13b-c063-756c-93cb-c7772eed1198]].

<!-- lore:01a1c67d-b131-71a9-b37a-fae0fd726103 -->
* **backlog-py stamp wiring: task\_prefix ignored at init (#13); decision-store conflict (#15)**: agentic-project-template backlog wiring bugs, reproduced on HEAD 50b611d. #13: fragments/tools/backlog-py/mise.toml:16 runs \`uvx --from backlog-md-py backlog-py init --defaults\`, which never receives the stamped task\_prefix answer — and backlog-py cannot change the prefix after init (wrong stamp = delete + reinit). #15: template says ADRs belong in backlog/decisions/ (SKILL.md.jinja:78, domain.md.jinja:16, agents-md-backlog-section.md:15) while the domain-modeling skill defaults to docs/adr/; base AGENTS.md:24 runs \`uv run backlog-py board\` though backlog-md-py is neither a dependency nor on PATH (works only via the maintainer's global pipx install; in a pylock.toml project it creates a stray uv.lock). Same conflict hits the glossary: .context/glossary.md is overwritten on template sync, so domain terms can't live there — proposed as decision ticket T11 on the template-stack wayfinder map (blocked on T7), awaiting user go-ahead before adding.

<!-- lore:01a19fcb-c48f-7034-a4b7-7ac4d28588a3 -->
* **backlog-py task create collapses newline-joined ACs into one line**: Passing acceptance criteria to \`backlog task create\` (backlog-py 2.1.0) joined with '\n' collapses all ACs into a single line in the task file. The CLI accepts the flags without error, so the collapsed result looks successful until the file is read. Fix: create the task, then edit the task file's AC/DOD sections directly (also the reliable way to add a missed label). DOT-283 was created this way (commit dbf4d20).

<!-- lore:01a1ce42-ac36-78cf-b786-4ffb61f05509 -->
* **backlog-py task show is not a command — use backlog-py task \<id>**: \`backlog-py task show DOT-233\` is not a valid command; the correct form is \`backlog-py task \<id>\`. Also seen: 'Task not found' for an ID whose file exists on disk. The CLI surface is upstream (rmusser01's backlog) — per the maintainer rule, do not submit issues or PRs upstream.

<!-- lore:01a1ed7b-98ab-7bc9-b2f2-0f0238abfe05 -->
* **backlog-py: call the mise shim on PATH; 'backlog' binary name doesn't exist**: Trap: \`which backlog\` returns not found (the binary is \`backlog-py\`), which reads as 'not installed here'. Fix: the mise shim ~/.local/share/mise/shims/backlog-py is on PATH and works — call \`backlog-py\` directly. Repeated \`uvx --from backlog-md-py backlog-py\` (~15× in one session, 2026-11-17) is redundant and slower on this machine; flagged as a retro candidate to document in docs/agents/issue-tracker.md. Why uvx seems right: flagship task files and older entries spell the uvx form, and the truncated 'backlog' name fails quietly at the shell rather than erroring.

<!-- lore:01a1a282-3cd3-7d11-aedd-89c78306408e -->
* **ballet .opencode/agents model pins uncommitted with no recorded rationale**: ballet repo (branch bal-1-usable-ballot-decision-mvp AND main) had 0 commits — all files untracked, including .opencode/agents/\*.md pinning expensive models: ballet.md + ballot-decide.md → openrouter/anthropic/claude-opus-5.5#max; ballot-research.md → claude-sonnet-5.5#high; ballot-verify.md → openrouter/openai/gpt-6.1-sol-pro#high. No rationale docs exist; model selection happened by DEFAULT, not by Pareto choice — trap: assuming the pins were deliberate decisions. Cost driver found: 84/453 OpenRouter models have tiered pricing overrides; gpt-6.1-sol-pro doubles above 272K context, and 7% of the day's turns crossed that threshold carrying 21% of token volume. Queued follow-ups: DOT-257 (worker model Pareto), draft-15 (model cost/latency budgets by service role), DOT-142 (extract model-bench/model-scout into their own repo).

<!-- lore:01a1e131-1161-7fdd-a994-6767cbf5e811 -->
* **bash unquoted heredoc: param values not re-expanded — no $(...) injection via variables**: Trap: an unquoted heredoc (<\<EOF) interpolating a variable whose value contains $(...) or backticks looks like command injection — during the pii-redactor shim review this nearly produced a false code-execution finding (override value \`$(rm -rf ~)\` 'would execute' on the error path). Fix: bash expands $(...) written directly in the heredoc BODY, but parameter-expanded VALUES are not recursively re-expanded — x='$(id)'; cat <\<EOF ${x} prints '$(id)' literally. Verified empirically 2026-11-15. Flag injection only when untrusted text lands verbatim in the body or is eval'd downstream; the status≠0 heredoc in the same shim is quoted (<<'EOF') and doubly safe.

<!-- lore:01a1d838-19d6-7460-9789-b18120bfef4c -->
* **C2 render-ownership rule breaks under template stacks — per-chunk attribution needed**: Open problem recorded in CC-7 (2026-11-13, not decided): C2's ownership rule — 'derive ownership from the render: template-rendered paths are template-owned' — breaks under a stack. With two layers both owning mise.toml, a single render can't tell which layer an edit belongs to (a python pin change belongs upstream to the base; a roborev pin change belongs to the agentic layer; same file, different destinations). Natural answer: reuse CC-6's chunk-level attribution so ownership becomes per-chunk instead of per-file — making CC-6 a prerequisite for phone-home under stacks, not just composition. Why the rule seems right: for a single template, render diffing cleanly attributes every path. Closed-loop rationale: forward → residue → upstream → forward — a conflict resolved once in research/ stops being a conflict in the next ten projects, which is why C2 belongs in the same design rather than a later phase.

<!-- lore:01a1dad1-1483-7efb-a298-4bf34bd551cc -->
* **CC-5 parity promise covers merge/mergers/adopt only, not the assembly surface**: copier-cat backlog/docs/cli-workflow-review.md (lines 86-103): CC-5 currently promises parity only for the shipped merge, mergers, and adopt surfaces. Trap: reading CC-5 as coverage of the proposed assembly surface (init, stack operations, assembly, approval, errors, machine-readable results) — it isn't; the doc is slated for revision after the workflow review. Why it seems right: CC-5 is the parity contract and the assembly surface is part of the same CLI, so parity reads as covering it. Fix: treat CC-5's scope as the three shipped surfaces until the revision lands; the user's first-use feedback is required before freezing the agent surface.

<!-- lore:01a1dad1-1464-7d35-b5f0-f0c3825b8037 -->
* **ccc alias = cc-assembly-ux-prototype prototype.py — simulation-only, FROZEN 2026-11-14 (no further tuning)**: The user's \`ccc\` alias is \`uv run --with prompt-toolkit --with rapidfuzz python3 ~/code/personal/cc-assembly-ux-prototype/prototype.py\` — a synthetic UX prototype, NOT the production copier-cat CLI. Simulation-only: no real rendering, config writes, downloads, or project tests — a green run validates nothing about real Copier behavior. State 2026-11-14: 17 simulated cases pass (tolerant confirmation parsing + simulated init state added). FROZEN by user decision 2026-11-14: remaining interaction details are reserved for implementation with in-session feedback — do NOT keep tuning or expanding the prototype. Config is simulated: CCC\_CONFIG\_FILE env var overrides the config path; a marker file's mere existence (contents never read) stands in for persisted config controlling first-run init. Guided-questions requirement recorded in copier-cat CC-17/CC-14 and backlog/docs/cli-workflow-review.md. CC-7 MVP composition design: \[\[01a1dac4-d588-7416-b7f8-30ee77e97ea5]].

<!-- lore:01a1daea-bbbc-7d3d-b65e-360774fe9399 -->
* **ccc assemble CLI: positional arg = destination project dir, not stack name**: Trap: in \`ccc assemble research --stack agentic-python --dry-run\`, \`research\` reads like a stack/template selector — it sits beside \`--stack\` and collides with the research repo's name — but it is the NEW project's destination directory (proposes ./research). \`--stack agentic-python\` picks the template composition; \`--dry-run\` prints the plan without writing files. Any destination name works (\`ccc assemble support-dashboard --stack agentic-python --dry-run\`). Part of the copier-cat CC-7 MVP surface \[\[01a1dac4-d588-7416-b7f8-30ee77e97ea5]]; using \`research\` as the destination matches the dogfood target of recreating the research repo from stack.yml \[\[01a1d88d-e279-7ebf-8581-5246281539e3]].

<!-- lore:01a1e134-23c7-749e-86c3-317b225dffec -->
* **ccc is copier-cat's CLI alias (CCC-15/Q30); cc is clang; PyPI CCC is unrelated Django CRM**: Trap: abbreviating copier-cat's CLI as \`cc\` looks natural (the runner is 'cc' in design docs and the stack.yml recipe), and on this machine \`cc\` resolves to clang. Settled in CCC-15/Q30 (2026-11-15): \`ccc\` is the local CLI alias; \`copier-cat\` is the project/package/registry identity. PyPI \`CCC\` is an unrelated Django CRM app (one release, 2019) — no collision, no PEP 541 transfer; fallback name \`tpls\`. The earlier 'invoke as copier-cat, not cc' note was a half-fact read off a help string.

<!-- lore:01a198df-1c63-7b71-b4a2-6bf87c162481 -->
* **chezmoi apply deploys shared checkout's current branch — verify branch=main before apply**: Trap: after PR #110 merged the lore-plugin-disable (9fcf037), the author's \`git pull --ff-only\` reported 'Already up to date' and scoped \`chezmoi diff\` was empty — reads as 'nothing to deploy'. Root cause: the shared chezmoi checkout (~/.local/share/chezmoi) sat on branch plugin-recovery, whose opencode.jsonc.tmpl still enabled the V1 plugin; apply faithfully deployed THAT branch's config. Fix: before handing the author any chezmoi diff/apply, verify the checkout's branch is main and main == origin/main (\`git branch --show-current\`); an empty scoped diff can mean a stale branch, not no change. Why it looks right: pull, diff, and apply all exit clean. Related: shared-checkout occupancy \[\[01a18526-0d15-704c-8994-b20abb8682bd]]; mise re-apply 'main checkout only' \[\[01a18b39-ea36-7f15-94d0-6ad2adcf7046]].

<!-- lore:01a1cdbf-e0ae-71ae-be78-f1391b709b28 -->
* **chezmoi apply single file: stat error when deployed parent dir missing — apply the parent dir**: Trap: \`chezmoi apply \<file>\` fails with 'stat \<target-dir>: no such file or directory' when the file's parent directory is missing in the DEPLOYED tree while the source exists (observed 2026-11-11: dotfiles/apps/claude-code/files/zsh/ absent under ~/dotfiles/apps/claude-code). The error names the deployed path, so it reads as a source-path typo. Fix: apply the parent directory (\`chezmoi apply ~/dotfiles/apps/claude-code/files\`) — chezmoi creates missing parents when the target is a directory but only stats the exact target when it is a file. Also: silent exit after apply is normal — verify with scoped \`chezmoi status\` plus ls/mtime on the deployed file, never by expecting output.

<!-- lore:01a1c19b-8cba-73f9-9c8a-bb9fdff68558 -->
* **chezmoi CLI: path args are target paths; diff hangs in Meld; apply executes run\_ scripts**: Three quirks: (1) \`chezmoi status -- bin/clipped\` reports 'not managed' because path args are interpreted as TARGET paths — pass absolute or \`~/bin/clipped\`. (2) \`chezmoi diff\` hangs (meld GUI, exit 124 after ~15s, leaked semaphores at shutdown) because diff.command = meld — fix: \`--use-builtin-git-diff\` or \`chezmoi cat \<target> | diff - \<target>\`; PR #112 (czd) wraps builtin diff for machine-readable output; the GUI-vs-terminal diff.command decision is still open — don't edit diff.command without the author's call. (3) \`chezmoi apply\` EXECUTES run\_before/run\_\* scripts (diff shows but does not run them) — a blanket apply can retire/modify live config; preview read-only via \`git archive origin/main .chezmoiroot home | tar -x -C \<tmp>\` then \`chezmoi -S \<tmp> status/diff\`. Also a full \`chezmoi diff\` may print secrets from config files — run \`chezmoi status\` first and scope diffs to the target path.

<!-- lore:01a18483-460d-7da1-ade8-a8bd4ab659c9 -->
* **chezmoi init required once templates read new config keys (isMac failure)**: Trap: after PR #27 added machine facts to .chezmoi.toml.tmpl (promptStringOnce 'machine' + computed arch/isMac/distro) and dot\_zshrc.tmpl gated on .isMac, every chezmoi command (status/diff/apply) failed 'map has no entry for key "isMac"' until \`chezmoi init\` regenerated ~/.config/chezmoi/chezmoi.toml. Looks right because init was previously optional — it only silenced the post-template-change warning when the rendered diff was trivial (PR #7). Rule: init is optional ONLY while templates read no new keys; when .chezmoi.toml.tmpl gains keys templates reference, init is REQUIRED first. init asks one question (machine label, hostname default); computed facts are not prompts. Preview before handing the author init+apply: render the template to a temp config and run chezmoi --config \<file> status/diff to census apply's effect (51 entries, 2026-09-28) without touching the real config. Verify after: rendered config shows the new keys; .zshrc renders unchanged.

<!-- lore:01a1a202-a74d-76d5-9087-f2938d6152ae -->
* **chezmoi managed omits unmanaged paths — assert Ansible-owned dirs via \`chezmoi unmanaged\`**: Trap: testing that an Ansible-owned destination (e.g. ~/.config/loreai, written by apps/loreai prompts.yaml from loreai-env.zsh.j2) is not chezmoi-managed via \`chezmoi managed\` passes vacuously — managed already omits paths with no chezmoi source. The command whose answer changes is \`chezmoi unmanaged\`. \`chezmoi ignored\` is also wrong for directory checks: ignored directories are pruned whole, so per-file assertions fail wrongly (hit: docs/PYTHON.md in tests/chezmoi/test\_governance\_not\_deployed.py). Fix: assert absence from \`chezmoi unmanaged\` output; measured 2026-11-03 on pop-os: \`chezmoi unmanaged ~/.config | grep -c loreai\` → 1, → 0 after adding \`.config/loreai\` + \`.config/loreai/\*\*\` to home/.chezmoiignore. Stakes: ~/.config/loreai/env.zsh is mode 0600 in a 0700 dir holding the live OpenRouter key — an accidental chezmoi adoption would deploy a rendered template over a secret.

<!-- lore:01a1a9f0-b699-7998-80d6-3ef2dfa7539b -->
* **chezmoi pre-commit hook: /bin/sh resets PATH — dirname not found; invoke prek directly**: Trap: retrying a failed commit unchanged looks right because the failure ('dirname: command not found' at .git/hooks/pre-commit line 5; prek hook docs-links-local can't exec python3 scripts/check-docs-links.py) seems transient — but the prek-generated hook runs under /bin/sh, which resets PATH on this machine, so a supplied PATH is ignored. Fix: invoke prek directly with a minimal absolute PATH (GNU dirname at /opt/homebrew/opt/coreutils/libexec/gnubin/dirname; /bin/sh PATH resolves to /opt/homebrew/bin:/usr/bin:/bin); never edit the hook file. Also: the session shell loses PATH between tool invocations (a command loop that passed once failed identically on retry; 'zsh: command not found: git', exit 127) — run dependent commits ONE AT A TIME with the minimal environment that just passed. Two git versions coexist (2.54.0 Apple Git-157, 2.56.0). Related Mac shell quirks: \[\[01a1a92c-a0fb-7c59-ab37-f2b2a25e9e19]].

<!-- lore:01a1f51e-501b-7281-b458-d8c0955ad246 -->
* **chezmoi present-but-unconfigured: dotfiles machine-state tests fail instead of skip**: Trap (proven 2026-11-19): installing chezmoi in .github/workflows/tests.yml (PR #185) un-skipped 6 machine-state tests — 3 × tests/chezmoi/test\_gitconfig\_tools.py ERROR ('map has no entry for key "email"': fixture renders home/dot\_gitconfig.tmpl via \`chezmoi execute-template\`, needing DEPLOYED config data absent on CI) and 3 × tests/merge\_gates/test\_real\_managed.py FAILED (live\_managed() runs \`chezmoi managed\` against the DEFAULT source dir; chezmoi exits 0 with empty output on a missing source dir → empty frozenset, not 'cannot answer'). Why skipif shutil.which('chezmoi') is None looks complete: it guards the absent binary; 'install the binary' reads as the fix — but presence ≠ configured. Fix (landed in PR #185 d73b202, proved under an isolated unconfigured-chezmoi shim): live\_managed() returns None → skip on empty answer; gitconfig tests skip without config data. Scoping the install to credential tests fails (single pytest invocation); EXPECTED\_FAILURES node IDs rejected (environment-dependent). CI env: \[\[01a1ebc6-f280-78e5-834c-4c1d859f81c5]].

<!-- lore:01a19fcb-c4a5-7fb9-b75a-5a7339df259d -->
* **chezmoi status MM with empty diff = stale record; confirm with chezmoi diff**: \`chezmoi status\` showing \`MM \<target>\` has two distinct meanings: (1) a stale last-applied state record — \`chezmoi diff\` on the source is EMPTY (source and target match); (2) real external drift — the deployed file was rewritten outside chezmoi (instance: deployed opencode.jsonc prettified, missing the \`opencode\` provider block with baseURL 127.0.0.1:3207/v1 and its trailing newline). Trap: treating MM as automatically requiring action, or dismissing it as stale without checking — both wrong. Fix: always run \`chezmoi diff \<target>\` (or diff the source render against the deployed file) before any fold-or-discard decision; unresolved drift blocks \`chezmoi apply\`.

<!-- lore:01a1eaba-37c7-7669-a32d-0e0e57aa7205 -->
* **claude-credential tests assume /bin/zsh — absent on Ubuntu CI; install zsh, resolve via PATH**: Trap: dotfiles claude-credential tests invoke zsh assuming /bin/zsh exists; Ubuntu CI runners ship no zsh, so the tests fail with a missing-binary error that reads like a broken test rather than a missing CI dependency. Fix (verified 2026-11-17, commit 1bcaea2b on agent/claude\_credential\_path): install zsh in the CI workflow and resolve the binary from the test environment's PATH instead of hardcoding /bin/zsh. Provisioning recorded in backlog task Provision-zsh-for-Claude-credential-tests.

<!-- lore:01a18985-3bd1-7234-9c5c-dc774ddb6e50 -->
* **Code Mode tool catalog: search() inside execute; catalog replaced on restarts**: The Code Mode tool catalog is replaced on server restarts (twice on 2026-10-29 alone) — after any restart, re-read the catalog instead of trusting remembered tool names. Inside \`execute\`, discover tools with synchronous \`search(query?, namespace?, limit?, offset?)\` → items\[{path, description, signature}], then call by path; never guess names. The opencode group (list\_mcp\_resources, models, read\_mcp\_resource, session\_move, session\_rename, worktree\_create/list/remove) is callable ONLY inside execute code. Semantics: session\_move/rename act on the CURRENT session when sessionID is omitted; worktree\_remove \`force\` discards modified/untracked files — set only after user confirms. read\_mcp\_resource: always return FULL contents, never truncate; it is not for local files.

<!-- lore:01a1e1da-af9c-7194-9d08-2f066da9e60d -->
* **CodeBurn timestamp-window attribution: OpenCode sessions land in ambiguous (concurrent sessions)**: Trap: CodeBurn's per-commit dollar figures read as measurements; they are \`methodology: "timestamp-window"\` heuristics — each commit credited to at most one session (the tightest window containing it). Ambiguous bucket = session ran parallel to another; the window's commits went to the tighter one. The squash-merge rescue (abandoned/ambiguous → productive) needs branch metadata in session logs — Claude Code records it, OpenCode does not, so OpenCode sessions land in \`ambiguous\`; the user runs 7+ concurrent sessions in one checkout, the exact worst case. DOT-275 fault-cost figures could be mostly \`ambiguous\` and still look authoritative. Fallback (if verification confirms): keep CodeBurn but record wall-clock time + functionality loss as primary fault-cost, dollars as supporting evidence. Verify cheaply: \`npx codeburn\` is zero-install and read-only — one run against this repo shows the ambiguous share. CLI: \`codeburn yield\`, default last 7 days; -p today|30days|month; --format json; run from project dir.

<!-- lore:01a19fa1-749b-7e7c-afb4-976a43d49b31 -->
* **codex via Lore gateway: 401 auth gap; ~/.codex/config.toml is lore+git-ai owned**: codex IS routed to the Lore gateway (openai\_base\_url=http://127.0.0.1:3207/v1) but every request fails 401 — local loopback mode deliberately disables gateway auth, yet codex is rejected; \`lore run codex exec\` fails identically; the OpenRouter key as bearer is also rejected. The real defect is gateway auth, not a missing provider block — DOT-104's premise is measured stale. Tracked in draft-2 (gateway auth beyond trusted loopback). codex is installed on this Linux box via mise (npm-openai-codex), so a Darwin-only task scoping misses a broken host. ~/.codex/config.toml is written by \`lore setup codex\` (idempotent converge) and git-ai adds hooks plus project trust entries — machine-local state that changes constantly. A chezmoi-managed config.toml would clobber all of it on every apply. If codex needs chezmoi-managed config, use codex's profile layering (\`-p \<name>\`, \`-c key=value\`) instead of owning the base file.

<!-- lore:01a1ea68-1cfa-7b21-9f78-f564623c4bd1 -->
* **commit-proportionality hook: --amend diffs 0 lines; trailers must be final block**: dotfiles commit gate budgets commit-message words against changed lines (30-word budget). Root cause confirmed (DOT-375, queued): scripts/check\_commit\_proportionality.py:89-102 reads \`git diff --cached\`, so ANY \`git commit --amend\` computes a 0-line diff and fails with '0-line change, budget is 30' — even a pure message amend, which seems like it should pass since no code changed; the hook simply cannot see amend diffs. Workaround until fixed: soft reset and re-commit. Never pad the diff to satisfy the budget — the sanctioned escape is a \`Proportionality:\` trailer declaring the disproportion. Trap 2: \`Mutation:\` / \`Failing-test:\` / \`Proportionality:\` trailers placed mid-body are invisible to \`git interpret-trailers\` (it parses only the final trailer block), so gates read them as absent even though they sit in the message. Fix: trailers last, one per line, nothing after. Verified 2026-11-17.

<!-- lore:01a183c4-16c4-79bd-a509-1cffc5947fea -->
* **Concurrent session forks: re-check git status + § Claimed; sibling commits in your worktree**: Concurrent sessions on one repo are routine — detect via uncommitted changes you didn't make, the opencode session list (PID + cwd), or UNEXPECTED COMMITS in your worktree. Protocol: stage only your paths (git add -p TASKS.md), never git add -A; snapshot the sibling's diff, re-verify after each commit; prek's unstaged-patch save/restore protects sibling work through commits; git checkout main inside any worktree fails (main lives at ~/.local/share/chezmoi). Traps: edits that VANISH between checks = sibling actively writing — re-check git status + TASKS.md § Claimed/§ RESOLVED; untracked bin/executable\_\* files may be sibling WIP — verify before deleting; author says a sibling handles it → stand down READ-ONLY. 2026-10-03 intrusion RESOLVED: the sibling commits (9cd0940/9181346) were it completing the SAME gate-checker handoff — merged agent/gate\_checker as main 882eeb7, pruned the worktree mid-run. Before executing a handoff's Next Move or landing uncommitted work, re-check § Claimed + git log main — a sibling may have already merged it. On unexpected HEAD: investigate (reflog, git show) first; confirm authorship with the author.

<!-- lore:01a1d842-3795-7ad4-b29c-bc9ba02d8ec5 -->
* **copier --conflict rej strands local edits in .rej files — cc stack update must fail loudly**: Trap: after a conflicted \`copier update --conflict rej\`, the working file holds the TEMPLATE's version and the local edit survives only inside \`\<file>.rej\` — probe-verified 2026-11-13 (~/code/try/cc-stack-probe/rejdemo): mise.toml landed \`pytest --maxfail=1\` while the local \`pytest -vv\` edit sat unapplied in mise.toml.rej; everything reads green if the .rej goes unnoticed. Why rej over inline: inline markers break TOML parsing; rej keeps the file valid/parseable — machine-checkable. Fix: \`cc stack update\` \[\[01a1d83e-58c5-7552-b26d-db118c07cf3a]] uses \`--conflict rej\` PLUS a hard stop — FAILS LOUDLY while any \`.rej\` remains; resolution = re-apply the hunk by hand, then \`rm \<file>.rej\`. NEVER auto-resolve: this is problem B (user edit vs newer template) — a genuine semantic conflict where .rej is copier correctly refusing to silently discard the user's edit; the CC-7 data merge kills .rej only at stamp time (problem A). Sibling hazard: \[\[01a1d83e-58db-7c03-8878-e8c5af35ac4a]].

<!-- lore:01a1d83b-24b1-7e09-93fa-e3d3551ad676 -->
* **copier --data replaces whole mapping; \_vars stripped from context; -x breaks includes**: Layered-copier probe traps (2026-11-13, ~/code/try/cc-stack-probe/, 9 experiments): (1) \`--data\` partial override REPLACES the whole mapping — overriding \`\[tools]\` left only the override's keys; the layer's roborev entry vanished. Reads like a merge; it isn't — merge mappings BEFORE handing to copier. (2) Underscore-prefixed copier.yml keys are stripped from render context — an \`\_layers\` ordering variable reads as Undefined. (3) \`-x\` exclude removes fragment dirs from the Jinja search path too, breaking includes. (4) \`{% include %}\` can't reach outside the template root (TemplateNotFound). (5) Concatenating fragments as text renders duplicate TOML tables (\`Cannot declare ('tools',) twice\`); \`{% do %}\` is unavailable (extension not enabled). Two plain renders into one dir print 'conflict mise.toml' and silently keep only the base. Working shape: structured \`type: yaml\` mappings in copier.yml + Jinja emitting \`\[tools]\`/\`\[tasks.X]\` via dictsort — one render, both layers, valid TOML, no cc needed at stamp time.

<!-- lore:01a1d83e-58db-7c03-8878-e8c5af35ac4a -->
* **copier -d replaces entire mapping value — per-layer --data calls silently wipe earlier layers**: Trap: \`-d 'tools={"python":"3.13"}'\` reads as a per-key patch, but copier replaces the ENTIRE variable value with the supplied YAML — sibling keys vanish silently. Probe-verified 2026-11-13 (~/code/try/cc-stack-probe): defaults rendered tools {python:3.12, roborev:latest}; after the override roborev was gone while the unoverridden \`tasks\` variable stayed intact. Why it seems right: \`-d\` looks like a targeted update, and surviving sibling variables make the render look complete. Fix: deep-merge all layers' contributions per key and hand copier ONE complete \`--data-file\` — never two per-layer \`--data\` calls (the second wipes the first). copier-cat implication (revised 2026-11-13): CC-7 no longer merges data before render — it composes structured render results from isolated native renders \[\[01a1d89d-27d2-7458-9fde-8fc306fa68d4]]; the one-complete-data-file rule applies only if cc ever drives a single combined render. Note: the user's real research/mise.toml was verified intact — APT's CLI deep-merges TOML properly; the loss is specific to raw copier -d/--data overrides.

<!-- lore:01a1d838-19c3-7a0c-8387-cd692ae70e6b -->
* **Copier answers files: never hand-edit; commit them — they power copier update**: Trap: .copier-answers.python-project-template.yml and .agentic-answers.yml look like generated config you might tidy or reformat, but both carry explicit no-manual-edit headers — base: 'Changes here will be managed by Copier; NEVER EDIT MANUALLY'; layer: 'Changes herein will be handled by \`agentic-project-template update\`. Do not edit by hand; commit this file (it powers updates).' The answers file IS the update chain's state: the \_src\_path and \_commit inside it determine what \`copier update -a\` merges against. Hand-editing it (or dropping it) orphans the layer's update lineage. Fix: never edit answers files by hand; always commit them.

<!-- lore:01a1d842-37a8-7ca8-957d-371f0e15e008 -->
* **copier clones template git tag, not working tree — commit and re-tag before probing**: Trap: copier renders from the template repo's git TAG, not the working tree — during the 2026-11-13 cc-stack-probe iterations, template edits were silently untested until committed and re-tagged. Why it seems right: editing template files in place looks like it should change what copier renders, and the probe runs green either way (you're just re-testing the old tag's content). Fix: when iterating on copier template probes (agentic-project-template, python-project-template), commit each change and move the tag before rendering — otherwise every probe run tests stale content. This cost several failed probe attempts before diagnosis.

<!-- lore:01a1cda5-1a17-7ddc-a69a-b3b772d35a2c -->
* **Copier has no parent-template inheritance — 'inherits' claim verified false; answers-files composition only**: Trap: Copier's documented claim 'templates can inherit from a parent template' reads as real inheritance support and makes Copier look strictly more capable than it is. Verified FALSE (2026-11-11): grepping the installed Copier source for extends/inherit/parent\_template/base\_template finds nothing — composition happens only through separate answers files. Context: retro tool-economy comparison of templating tools; Cookiecutter (the most widely used) cannot pull a template change into already-generated projects at all, so ranking by adoption puts the failing tool first — the review-best-practices 'popularity is not fit evidence' worked example. When designing copier-template-copier-template layering, never assume inherits-style keys exist; layering must go through answers files.

<!-- lore:01a1daa6-8bd1-7614-868a-867b3735aca4 -->
* **copier-cat naming resolved: \`ccc\` alias accepted, \`cc\` rejected (/usr/bin/cc collision)**: Naming decision (2026-11-14, supersedes the Nov 13 status where \`ccc\` was prototype-candidate-only pending the naming workflow): \`ccc\` is the accepted short alias for copier-cat; \`copier-cat\` remains the package identity. \`cc\` stays rejected — it collides with /usr/bin/cc, the system C compiler, and shadowing it breaks toolchains that invoke the compiler by name. Recorded in the CC-15 canonical-guidance design docs; no commits yet as of 2026-11-14.

<!-- lore:01a19f98-6357-7ea8-953f-43cbbb90ec1c -->
* **czd drift script: meld bypass, exit contract, stat-mode stderr phantom drift**: home/bin/executable\_czd deploys as ~/bin/czd. Rationale: the user's chezmoi.toml sets \[diff] command = meld — a GUI printing nothing to stdout and exiting 0, so piped callers read 'no differences' from files that differ; czd wraps \`chezmoi diff --use-builtin-diff\` and \`chezmoi status\` for machine-readable output. The meld config is deliberately left alone (author's interactive preference). Exit contract: 0=no drift, 1=drift, 2=chezmoi missing or unmanaged. Drift detection parses \`chezmoi status\` col2: non-space = drift (' M' means dest reverted to match target — judgement call per AGENTS.md). GOTCHA (reported 2026-11-02, unfixed): \`out=$(chezmoi status ... 2>&1)\` in stat mode folds chezmoi's stderr warnings (rc=0) into the parse — a warning line's col2='h' counts as drift, so czd --stat exits 1 falsely on a clean tree.
