The guard's own message says report upstream rather than hand-edit content back — when it fires for that reason, leave it red and report. Was red 2026-11-15 (two rules dropped); other sessions should expect this failure and not treat it as their own breakage.

<!-- lore:01a1ad9b-ae52-7b8c-9809-066b87721b82 -->
* **tests/test\_python\_floors.py sweep finds 0 files in .worktrees checkouts (EXCLUDED\_PARTS vs absolute path parts)**: tests/test\_python\_floors.py filters REPO.rglob('\*.py') by EXCLUDED\_PARTS (includes '.worktrees') against set(path.parts) of ABSOLUTE paths. Any checkout under ~/.local/share/chezmoi/.worktrees/ excludes every file → PYTHON\_FILES empty → test\_the\_sweep\_finds\_real\_files fails ('sweep missed scripts/check-docs-links.py, smartparts/python/interactive\_setup.py') and the parametrized parse test yields zero cases. Trap: 'reproduces on detached main' reads as pre-existing main debt, but detached main checked out in the same worktree fails identically — the failure is strictly worktree-local (passes at the canonical ~/.local/share/chezmoi checkout), unlike the queue-integrity failures \[\[01a1ad94-b19b-7d88-80c3-f00055298cb4]]. Fix: filter on path.relative\_to(REPO).parts. Sweep purpose: catches ruff format at a py3.14 target stripping parens from \`except (A, B):\` (PEP 758, SyntaxError ≤3.13); home/ payload files carry no pyproject floor because Ansible runs them via absolute /usr/bin/python3 (3.10.12) while mise python3 is 3.14.8.

<!-- lore:01a1c67d-b12b-773e-8ad5-b03f6e5b9f09 -->
* **tomli\_w dump + Jinja loops fail tombi format on fresh agentic stamps (#14)**: Fresh stamps from agentic-project-template fail the layer's own tombi-format hook on first commit. Causes: tomli\_w.dumps (merge.py:102) indents 4 spaces where tombi expects 2 (agents.toml, prek.toml args, mise.toml 'sync' entries); Jinja loops leave stray trailing blank lines in agents.toml; pyproject.toml places \[build-system] (hatchling) at top where tombi wants it at end (python-project-template#3 — present even in base-only stamps). Trap: looks like tombi misconfiguration because base-only mise.toml/prek.toml are clean — the dirt comes only from the agentic merge step (tomli\_w), not the base templates. All four bugs reproduced on HEAD 50b611d before filing agentic-project-template#14 and python-project-template#3.

<!-- lore:01a1eca0-d80f-763e-b4df-120b1ed37f6c -->
* **topic tests: fixture must include provider topics (homebrew/mise), not host binaries**: Trap: a topic test fixture without its provider topics passes locally — the host already has homebrew/mise installed — then fails in CI, reading like a flaky or host-specific test. Fix: the fixture must explicitly include the provider topics (homebrew, mise) so tests exercise the scheduler against fixtures, never host binaries. Fixed 2026-11-17 in tests/topics/test\_git\_topic.py for the git topic (second CI failure on the pushed branch); 29 focused tests green and mutation testing still catches a missing Git provider. Why the wrong approach seems right: local runs are green because the host provisioned the providers, masking the hidden dependency.

<!-- lore:01a1e2bb-41bd-76ec-9497-a02643b71946 -->
* **topic-resolution order-pass.yaml: set\_fact needs two tasks; parenthesize before subset filter**: Gotchas from the Kahn's dependency-ordering pass (roles/topic-resolution/tasks/order-pass.yaml): 1. set\_fact evaluates every value before any is assigned — computing the ready set and consuming it in one task reads an undefined name; split into two set\_fact tasks (compute, then consume). A single task reads as natural dataflow. 2. Parenthesize the concatenated candidate set before the subset filter — a filter after \`+\` binds to the last term only (\`a + b | subset(...)\` parses filter-first), so a valid order reads as a cycle. 3. Inside the loop use the named outer loop variable (ordering\_pass), never the parent's implicit item. Contract: each iteration appends the alphabetically first ready topic; other-tier providers count as satisfied; cycle detection asserts ordered == total topic count. Verified 2026-11-15 (27/27 tests green): line 28 already folds external\_dependency\_tokens into the ready set; line 41 asserts progress, not completeness — unprovided dependencies are handled, never mislabelled cycles. Trap: a contrary diagnosis came from a stale revision (a concurrent session edited the file mid-investigation); re-read the file at diagnosis time before proposing fixes.

<!-- lore:01a1ea78-3db4-7f50-a5c9-c9116403e9b9 -->
* **topical new --repo default expects legacy dotfiles/ layout — pass --repo \<checkout>/home --into \<checkout>/home/dotfiles**: Trap: \`topical new \<name>\` with the default \`--repo \<checkout>\` fails because the tool expects the LEGACY dotfiles/ location, not the chezmoi repo's home/dotfiles layout — the invocation errors and creates NO files, which reads like a tooling bug rather than a path-shape mismatch. Fix: pass the layout explicitly: \`topical new git --os Darwin --os Debian --manager homebrew\_formula --manager apt --repo \<checkout>/home --into \<checkout>/home/dotfiles\`. The scaffold emits install/remove manager blocks targeting BOTH Darwin and Debian — the topic must guard Homebrew to Darwin and apt to Debian before use. Verified 2026-11-17 scaffolding the apps/git topic (DOT-352).

<!-- lore:01a18570-fd1b-75f6-971e-57f72f724ca9 -->
* **Uninstall policy: never blanket brew/apt autoremove; ask author, record package owner**: Standing rule (backlog DOT-261): package removal is opt-in and must distinguish topic-owned state from pre-existing packages, other projects' packages, shared Vagrant boxes, and user VM data. NEVER use blanket package-manager autoremove or prune (apt autoremove, brew sweeps, vagrant box prune). Why blanket cleanup reads as safe: it targets 'unused' packages — but state on this machine is shared across topics, projects, and VMs invisible from inside one topic, so 'unused' is unknowable. When cleanup is needed, enumerate the specific packages the topic owns and remove exactly those, explicitly.

<!-- lore:01a18498-a906-72bd-9271-ecd128757b5e -->
* **uv run without --frozen re-locks uv.lock to employer (Sentry) PyPI index**: ALWAYS pass --frozen to \`uv run\` (author directive): without it uv re-locks uv.lock against the machine's configured index — this Mac's employer (Sentry) private PyPI mirror via /etc/uv/uv.toml + PIP\_INDEX\_URL — rewriting 353 lines with employer-identifying URLs into a COMMITTED file (spec.md § Data violation). Looks right because the command runs fine and the diff seems mechanical. Refinement: --with packages already in uv.lock don't re-lock; --with a package ABSENT from the lock does. Finding a re-locker: run suites one at a time, checking \`git status --porcelain uv.lock\` after each (the apps/uv guard test catches them live mid-suite). Known contamination: branches backup/pre-reword-8f6c31b and feat/ollama-manifest-scope carry the leak in tools/skill-trust/uv.lock — never push unchecked. Agents can't read ~/.config/uv (employer-owned); existence checks without values pass.

<!-- lore:01a1aa17-67c4-78bc-b6a4-09df4b795ba0 -->
* **Worktree reap: merge-base --is-ancestor gate before branch delete; false negatives (cherry-picks, squash merges)**: A worktree branch is deleted only after \`git merge-base --is-ancestor \<branch> main\` proves the work landed; never \`git branch -D\` before that check. Trap: deletion looks safe when the work 'obviously' landed, but a branch whose commits were cherry-picked into buckets never becomes an ancestor — copies carry new SHAs, so merge-base fails on landed work. Fix: prove cherry-picked landings with \`git cherry main \<branch>\` — every \`-\` line means the patch is already on main. Crash recovery: \`git worktree remove --force \<path>\` then \`git worktree prune\`. Pairs with never removing another session's worktree \[\[01a1a92c-a08b-79f3-94e1-814817416312]].

<!-- lore:01a1cdc6-d3a7-7fe7-af19-f8abd86a90d3 -->
* **worktrunk wt switch: --yes; dirty-main refusal fail-closed (even for others' dirt) → sanctioned worktree-add recovery**: \`wt switch --create \<branch> --base origin/main\` in the shared chezmoi checkout hits two blockers: (1) the pre-switch hook needs interactive approval — append \`--yes\`. (2) The hook is fail-closed: 'main checkout is dirty' fires even when the dirt is another session's (it can't tell whose changes they are) — NO bypass; \`--no-hooks\` defeats the guard and stashing discards others' work. On a dirty-main refusal, STOP and report. Sanctioned recovery when branching is approved: \`git fetch origin main\` FIRST (\`git worktree add\` builds only from LOCAL refs; ls-remote doesn't fetch), then \`git worktree add \<path> -b \<branch> origin/main\`; commit only the repair; never stash others' dirty files. Both blockers are mechanical, not authorization problems.

### Pattern

<!-- lore:01a1cdbc-1126-79e3-9dde-cd43c4d79f50 -->
* **.chezmoiignore must exclude Ansible-owned destinations (two-writers rule)**: bengineerdavis/dotfiles home/.chezmoiignore must exclude every path a topic's Ansible tasks write at install time — a rendered template landing on provisioning-owned state creates two writers for one file. Examples: .config/loreai/\*\* (apps/loreai writes ~/.config/loreai/env.zsh holding the OpenRouter key at 0600 on first install; re-answering means deleting the file and re-running the topic), \*\*/.venv and \*\*/\_\_pycache\_\_ (each tools/\<name>/ grows a venv on first \`uv run --project\` — otherwise a later apply copies an entire virtualenv into the deployed tree), starship preset\_choice.yml, crontab. Enforced by tests/chezmoi/test\_ansible\_owned\_paths.py. Trap: 'chezmoi does not manage this' is also true of any path nobody considered yet — a missing managed file is not automatically a gap; check the ignore list and topic ownership first.

<!-- lore:01a1c813-60ae-773b-888b-fde4f3559382 -->
* **/pr skill PR body shape: smallest visual, before/after evidence, one-way/two-way door call**: PR bodies follow the /pr skill's shape (applied 2026-11-10 to dotfiles PR #174, skill-trust resolve --revisit): (1) the smallest visual showing the change — a minimal diff or snippet that makes the change legible at a glance; (2) before/after evidence — observable behavior demonstrating the change works; (3) a one-way/two-way door call — an explicit reversibility assessment. Pairs with the commit-hygiene arc \[\[01a1c784-ffdd-7179-adfe-74436c02f581]]: the commit argues the change, the PR demonstrates it.

<!-- lore:01a1a885-82c0-7257-9cbc-c7cb0311747c -->
* **~/code/personal workspace root is not a git repo**: /Users/ben.davis/code/personal is NOT a git repository ('fatal: not a git repository') — it is the workspace root holding multiple repos (ai-prompt-library, forks, .lore.md). When a session starts here, the answer to 'which project/worktree are we in' is none — operate across repos and name the specific repo per task. Repos seen in sweeps: ~/.local/share/chezmoi (dotfiles), ~/code/personal/ai-prompt-library, ~/code/forks/skillet, ~/backlog (backlog store).

<!-- lore:01a1e2bb-41cc-70fd-a043-d7e31c497466 -->
* **Adopting dead-session uncommitted work: test first + control-check failing test on main**: Steps: 1. Run the inherited work's test suite BEFORE building on it — DOT-352: 10/24 failed. 2. Control-check a failing test on main — passing on main but failing in the worktree proves the uncommitted edits (not staleness against main) broke it. 3. Diagnose root cause before choosing repair vs. narrow adoption. Gotchas: - Branch N commits behind main with zero unique commits reads as 'stale, worthless' — its commits were already rebased onto main; all remaining value is uncommitted-tree-only. - Repairing another session's half-finished algorithm feels like part of 'adopting' — it exceeds the task scope; present repair-vs-narrow-adoption to the user rather than assuming (DOT-352 deferred the choice explicitly). Verify: - \[ ] Root cause named — e.g. DOT-352's unconditional Kahn's pass in roles/topic-resolution/tasks/main.yaml made diagnostic states (NO\_TOPIC, TOPIC\_NOT\_RUN) hit failed=1 during ordering, so said(result, '\[NO\_TOPIC]') never matched.

<!-- lore:01a1c67d-b12e-7b97-9652-f258359733ca -->
* **agentic-project-template merge.py: deep\_merge by name key, base wins, never removes keys**: src/agentic\_project\_template/merge.py semantics: deep\_merge (lines 110-115) merges lists-of-dicts by the \`name\` key (dotagents \[\[skills]]/\[\[mcp]], prek \[\[repos]]); on conflicts base wins and overlay's unseen entries are appended — it never removes keys, so a fragment can only un-set a base key via text-level strip on fresh stamps. dump\_toml writes via tomli\_w.dumps (line 102). merge\_prek\_config (line 293) merges native prek.toml as TOML or appends hooks to legacy .pre-commit-config.yaml in YAML — prek reads only ONE config, so coexisting configs silently drop legacy hooks (a warning is emitted); \[\[repos]] entries are deduped by \_repo\_signature ('kept existing' on duplicate).

<!-- lore:01a1cdbc-112d-7e79-8ce0-a266de26e0a6 -->
* **ai-prompt-library repo conventions: prompts/ flat-vs-structured, generated/ bundler output, never force-push**: ai-prompt-library conventions (AGENTS.md): prompts/ is the library — flat collections (single .md or dir with SKILL.md; bundle.py detects flat by absence of events/ and roles/) vs structured (events/, roles/, skills/, templates/, context/). generated/ (root + per-collection) is bundler output — overwrite freely, never hand-edit. impromptu/ is a separate sub-project being extracted to its own repo — never apply library conventions to it. tests/, tools/, examples/ are empty placeholders; no test suite. Always run Skillet via \`npx -y @sentry/skillet@latest\` (never a bare install). research/\<topic>/ holds research for proposed project principles. NEVER force-push the repo. Commit style: Scoped Commits. No CI configured — gh reports no checks on library branches.

<!-- lore:01a1c67b-dd26-7f27-b652-55a2066b1c00 -->
* **ai-prompt-library: npx -y @sentry/skillet@latest only; never force-push; generated/ is bundler output**: ai-prompt-library repo conventions (2026-11-10): always run Skillet via \`npx -y @sentry/skillet@latest\` — never a bare/local install. NEVER force-push the repo: one-time sync notes are resolved by whoever pulls deleting the block, committing, pushing. prompts/ is the library: flat collection = single .md or a dir with SKILL.md; structured = events/, roles/, skills/, templates/, context/. generated/ is bundler output — overwrite freely, never hand-edit. impromptu/ is a separate sub-project being extracted to its own repo. Commit style: Scoped Commits; no CI configured (gh reports no checks on library branches).

<!-- lore:01a1cdbc-1137-7877-bd4c-504173effb16 -->
* **Ansible remove-tag rule: uninstall is always --tags remove; remove tasks tagged never**: dotfiles Ansible topic rule: uninstalling is always a deliberate \`--tags remove\`; remove tasks are tagged \`never\`, so a bare (untagged) run does provision only and can never tear a topic down. New topics inherit this from template\_dir. Always scope real runs with a tag (\`--tags install\` is the common one). Tag semantics (docs/ARCHITECTURE.md): provision = bootstrap+prerequisites+upgrade+install; bootstrap = first-time system-tool setup (never silent); prerequisites = core deps (git/curl/xcode-cli); upgrade = system-level refresh; install = topic packages; remove = teardown, explicit only. Topic tag hierarchy: pkg-manager (apt/homebrew/docker) → runtime-manager (mise/ollama) → app (everything else).

<!-- lore:01a1a9e2-e0bb-7e77-983c-c1d1594923e3 -->
* **Ansible topic test harness: real ansible-playbook, -i localhost, inline host; play vars beat host facts**: Harness convention for Ansible topic tests: invoke the REAL ansible-playbook binary (never mocks); throwaway play under tmp\_path imports the role/tasks file directly; run with \`-i localhost,\` (trailing-comma inline host list); inputs via \`-e\` JSON-encoded vars; cwd = dotfiles root; skip with 'ansible-playbook not on PATH — run: mise install' when absent. Assertions must make broken states fail loudly — a silent skip is treated as a bug. Precedence gotcha: play \`vars:\` rank ABOVE gathered host facts (host facts ~#10, play vars ~#11), so a chain test's \`ansible\_hostname: pop-os\` play var overrides the discovered hostname even with gather\_facts: true — intuition says discovered facts win; they don't. Helper gotchas: task\_skipped() scans the 3 lines after 'TASK \[loreai : \<name>]' for 'skipping:'; effective() requires BOTH 'effective' AND 'telemetry=' because the divergence warning repeats the same key=value text (bare substring false-positives).

<!-- lore:01a1addb-d5d9-7933-bba2-97eaace065ab -->
* **apps/gita: CLI limits, safe-sync semantics, argv conventions, machine-local layout capture**: gita 0.16.8.2 (source-verified, PR #92): \`gita rm\`/group commands validate against get\_repos — they CANNOT remove ghost registrations (prune step was dead code); \`gita add -g\` only groups NEWLY-registered repos — a separate \`gita group add\` set-union sweep is needed; \`gita group ls\` takes NO arguments; \`gita add -r\` glob misses dot-dirs but registers worktree/submodule .git files; group storage can't round-trip names with spaces. Ansible: pass gita/mise invocations as block-style \`argv:\` — a flow-style list in \`cmd:\` gets STRINGIFIED (\`\[Errno 2] ... b'\[gita,'\`); unquoted key=value extra vars truncate at the first space — pass overrides as JSON. Sync-on-upgrade (G4): fetch every repo incl. dirty ones; ff-only merge clean repos; a failed fetch means UNKNOWN and can never count as 'up to date' despite exit 0; the deviation from TOPIC-RECIPES' fail-once-at-end is recorded in the topic README. The layout capture (~/.local/state/gita/layout.yaml) is machine-local per spec.md § Data — never commit/paste its contents; cite by path only. Provenance ruling comments and work-repo-name secrecy rules survive catalogue edits.

<!-- lore:01a1c67d-b134-732e-987d-817bbc552c12 -->
* **backlog-py CLI: decision create --status for ADRs; docs via subcommands, never hand-edit**: backlog-py (from backlog-md-py) facts: \`backlog-py decision create \[OPTIONS] TITLE\` exists with -s/--status TEXT — usable for ADRs in backlog/decisions/. Doc subcommands: create, list, update, view; update takes --title, --content, -p/--path (move), -t/--type, --tags. Use these instead of hand-editing backlog doc files — backlog files shouldn't be hand-edited. \`backlog-py task complete\` does not exist — closing is \`task edit -s Done\`; use uvx forms everywhere since backlog-py is not on PATH in fresh stamps. The maintainer's machine has backlog-py globally via pipx under mise (mise/installs/pipx-backlog-md-py/latest/bin with shim) — the only reason \`uv run backlog-py\` works locally.

<!-- lore:01a1a26f-62ad-7360-b4fe-16353a4065d3 -->
* **ballet worktree fan-out: top-level opencode invocations (is\_subagent=0), not subagents; agent model pins**: The ballet worktree's ballot-contest fan-out spawns separate top-level \`opencode\` invocations — is\_subagent=0 on all 336 sessions in the Nov 2 burst — NOT opencode subagents, so subagent settings and per-agent rate limits will not touch them. Signature: many sessions with ≤2 messages (211 sessions, $58.47 in ~2 hours). Agent model pins: ballot-verify.md → openrouter/openai/gpt-6.1-sol-pro#high; ballot-research.md → openrouter/anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5#high; ballot-decide.md and ballet.md → openrouter/anthropic/claude-opus-5.5#max. Cache is healthy (94–100% hit rate, create=0) — the cost driver is turn volume × context size (~125K mean per turn), not cache failure.

<!-- lore:01a1d84e-e855-7e4a-a03a-5410331d429f -->
* **CC-7 mise.toml data merge: two renders lose roborev; concatenation hits TOML duplicate-table**: CC-7 Scenario A mechanics (probe 2026-11-13): layers declare render variables in copier.yml — \`mise\_tools: {type: yaml, default: {roborev: latest}}\` is question syntax, not the final file; BASE contributes mise\_tools python 3.12 + mise\_tasks test: pytest; AGENTIC adds roborev: latest + review task. cc deep-merges per key, then ONE Jinja template renders mise.toml from merged data (dictsort loops emit \`\[tools]\` entries and \`\[tasks.\<name>]\` blocks). Name variables mise\_tools/mise\_tasks, not the probe's tools/tasks — they name the destination. Two rejected approaches, both look reasonable: (1) two plain \`copier copy\` renders → copier reports \`conflict\`, keeps base, roborev silently lost; (2) concatenating fragments → TOML rejects \`Cannot declare ('tools',) twice\` — concatenation preserves both texts, not one valid structure. A layer shipping a literal mise.toml fragment works standalone but can't join data-shaped composition until it offers mappings. CC-7 split \[\[01a1d849-fb94-7bc5-8884-4e6d2d502ce2]]; root cause \[\[01a1d83e-58db-7c03-8878-e8c5af35ac4a]].

<!-- lore:01a1cdc6-d3ab-71de-900e-9e1631561f6c -->
* **check-docs-links.py lychee backend probe: fall through only on failure-to-execute, never on findings**: scripts/check-docs-links.py probes backends in order — cargo:lycheeverse/lychee first, then aqua:lycheeverse/lychee (Mac fallback) — and runs the first that executes. The probe is a cheap \`--version\` and must fall through ONLY on failure-to-execute, NEVER on link findings: falling through on findings would let a broken link silently pass whenever the primary backend is installed. If no backend executes, exit non-zero with the reason; the hook must fail loudly rather than skip (load-bearing property). mise config prefers the cargo entry; the aqua entry stays as the Mac fallback. Verified: \`lychee --offline --files-from -\` on ROADMAP.md → 0 errors, rc=0.

<!-- lore:01a183e3-8552-7edd-897a-2c2c98cec6d9 -->
* **chezmoi managed --path-style=source-relative lists deployed-tree paths mechanically**: \`chezmoi managed --path-style=source-relative\` prints every deployed target as its repo-source path, already .chezmoiignore-aware (verified live: 1015 entries; AGENTS.md correctly absent — it is ignored). This is the mechanical answer to 'is this file in the deployed tree?', which was previously prose-only judgment. Found while scoping the merge-gate-checker handoff (TASKS.md § Now, commit 91ba8af): it fully solves the checker's deployed-tree bucket with zero judgment calls. Reuse for any classifier or audit needing the deployed set — e.g. confirming a new repo-root file needs .chezmoiignore \[\[01a18383-6740-7245-89c5-3e8b28efd79a]]. Grounds the deployed-tree-executes doctrine \[\[01a1839c-29ba-7768-a16d-73e9737a81ce]] with a checkable list. Sibling: \`chezmoi source-path\` resolves a single target's source path. Related: \[\[01a183e2-19e8-73f6-ae1e-8da2b6992eea]].

<!-- lore:01a1ea90-1339-7025-af23-0220c87be8cf -->
* **chezmoi per-task workflow: clean-main check, wt switch --create, orchestration claim, PR via --body-file**: Steps: 1. Read AGENTS.md, spec.md, docs/WORKTREES.md first; verify \`git branch --show-current\` is main and \`git status\` shows nothing of the user's — if main holds another session's dirty work, STOP and report; never stage/stash/commit it \[\[01a1ea8d-753a-75b9-b099-c72881081abc]]. 2. Per task: \`wt switch --create \<task-id-lowercase>-\<slug>\` (its pre-switch hook syncs main); rename the session \`\<TASK-ID>-\<slug>\`; claim via backlog-py orchestration claim. 3. Bugs: diagnosing-bugs skill — red reproduction before any fix. Code: tdd with Mutation:/Failing-test: trailers. 4. PR: hand the user the push command; once pushed, run the roborev review, then \`gh pr create --repo bengineerdavis/dotfiles --body-file\`; record the PR in the task; release the claim. Gotchas: - \`wt switch --create\` refuses on a dirty main — that is the guard working, not a blocker; fall back to plain \`git worktree add\` per docs/WORKTREES.md; never bypass the guard. - Never push — pushes go through the user. Verify: - \[ ] Session renamed \<TASK-ID>-\<slug>; task claimed. - \[ ] PR recorded in task; claim released.

<!-- lore:01a184b3-87c0-7f11-a940-fb754bfc00b1 -->
* **chezmoi: source-state mechanics & worktree safety**: \`chezmoi execute-template\` resolves .chezmoidata from the ACTIVE --source dir, not cwd — test templates against FIXTURE source dirs for deterministic output. Templates expose \`env\`, NOT \`getenv\` ('function "getenv" not defined' is the signature); use {{ env 'VAR' }}. Keep a probe in exactly one source dir — copying into both main and worktree poisons the experiment. home/.chezmoiignore lives at home/ (chezmoiroot=home/), not repo root; default hidden=false globs miss it — use ls. .chezmoiignore must mirror .gitignore: never deploy \_\_pycache\_\_/\*\*/.venv (each tools/\<name>/ venv would be copied into the deployed tree on apply); .config/loreai/\*\* is ignored (env.zsh is 0600 in a 0700 dir holding the live OpenRouter key — accidental adoption would deploy a rendered template over the secret). With chezmoiroot=home/, repo-root files are outside source state.

<!-- lore:01a1ddea-d9ca-7cbf-9c13-1398dde9dbb5 -->
* **copier-cat .scratch queue superseded by CCC-24–29; archive-then-remove migration**: In copier-cat (2026-11-14) the six .scratch/deterministic-project-assembly/issues/ tickets were reconciled into native backlog-py tasks CCC-24–29 in ticket order (01 Python assembly→CCC-24 … 06 distribution design→CCC-29), preserving dependencies, ready-for-agent labels, and acceptance criteria; DOC-10 archives the originals verbatim with the full mapping; PR #11 then removed the duplicate queue. Migration procedure: (1) create native tasks preserving edges/labels/AC; (2) archive originals verbatim in a doc with the mapping; (3) merge; (4) remove the .scratch queue as a separately approved cleanup — never bundle deletion with migration or leave both queues live. Why: two live queues risk agents implementing from different queues or updating one without the other; .scratch was untracked residue while backlog/tasks/ is version-controlled. APL-26 owns the reusable reconcile/routing component. Generalizes the ask-matt tracker rule \[\[01a1db6f-618e-7923-a676-2da1407a9dd4]].

<!-- lore:01a1db63-639c-7fd4-95b4-dace1e7f96e2 -->
* **copier-cat 2026-11-14 retro: backlog integrity check, stacked-PR procedure, MVP boundary**: Retro follow-ups in priority order: (1) Add a small prek check for Backlog task-record invariants — frontmatter ID matches filename, no duplicate IDs, dependencies resolve; the cc-\*→ccc-\* migration landed additions while cc-\* deletions sat unstaged, and \`backlog doctor\` misses file-pair breaks. (2) Document a stacked-PR maintenance procedure — update base metadata before changing branch contents, verify every PR stays open with its intended diff; prefer merge-based stack updates unless rebase is required (improvised force-with-lease duplicated migration commits; PR #3 closed and replaced). (3) Keep a small MVP contract beside the exploration log (first usable outcome, one workflow, minimum inputs, non-goals) — design accumulation repeatedly displaced the usable milestone; classify new ideas usage-blocking vs deferred before they enter the milestone. Also: \`backlog-py task --plain\` for inspection; publish prototypes on prototype/\<name> branches; preserve the testkit spec via the standalone testkit project before removing that worktree.

<!-- lore:01a1d855-dff2-7ea3-b68c-2ec67bf5f75e -->
* **copier-cat adapter contract: 8 behavioral questions + cross-format test matrix**: Testing strategy for copier-cat composition adapters (2026-11-13): test the shared pipeline once, then every adapter against the same 8-question behavioral contract: (1) parses input; (2) preserves unrelated content; (3) identifies corresponding chunks; (4) applies declared layer order deterministically; (5) produces valid output; (6) re-parsing rendered output gives expected structure; (7) unresolved conflicts surfaced, never silently dropped; (8) idempotent (second run changes nothing). Add a cross-format matrix asserting identical behavior (additive composition, collision handling, idempotence, conflict reporting) across all adapters. Format-specific essentials: TOML (distinct/same keys, arrays, comments, nested tables); YAML (nested mappings, lists, anchors, multi-doc, comments); JSON (nesting, arrays, scalar root, formatting); Markdown (named regions, duplicate headings, unmarked prose, markers inside code fences); Text (line union, ordered concat, contradictions, no final newline, encodings); Binary (identical, single contributor, conflicting).

<!-- lore:01a1daa6-8bc6-7afe-a8b7-0c717e9d7e74 -->
* **copier-cat assemble/catalog CLI: catalog.yml recipes, four output modes, three-level validation (Q25–Q29)**: Confirmed Q25–Q29, extending CC-7 \[\[01a1d8b2-94c2-7a17-b3fb-7dd399efb431]]: local YAML catalog — catalog.yml maps familiar names to templates/recipes; each recipe a separate self-contained YAML file (recipes/python-agentic.yml), shareable as a file; git-backed sync is post-MVP. CLI: \`copier-cat assemble\` (single/multi) + \`catalog\` group (list/show); invariant operation order: discover → preview → approve → assemble → report. Terse one-command assembly with visible defaults, not an extended interview; staged execution optional; first-class recipe create/edit. Output modes: default (short outcome + next action), -v (resolved plan, composition decisions, validation evidence), --debug (internal diagnostics), --json (one structured result on stdout). Validation (Q28) at three levels: rendered-file validity (parse composed TOML, verify contributions), assembly invariants (answers files, provenance, idempotence, preview cleanup), generated project's own tests — a clean TOML merge alone is NOT a successful assembly. Provenance (Q27): .copier-cat-stack.yml records the recipe; disposable, never a runtime dependency.

<!-- lore:01a1db63-6392-7679-8154-7eccb6423dc6 -->
* **copier-cat assembly test boundaries: CLI acceptance + composition engine, no private-helper inspection**: Assembly MVP tests observe two boundaries only: (1) CLI acceptance — real commands, observable effects; (2) composition engine — deterministic structured merge behavior. Never inspect private helper calls or duplicate production logic in tests. Required coverage: render Python alone AND Python plus the independent agentic overlay; run generated-project tests; prove validation failure is not success (a validator that never fails is a broken gate, not a passing one). Prior art/fixtures use harmless names \`example-project\` and \`example-stack\`. Why: testing past these boundaries couples tests to implementation, and a never-failing validator reads as green while validating nothing.

<!-- lore:01a1db58-c1b6-73c3-aff7-31749f7ae29c -->
* **copier-cat backlog doc separation: DOC-9 spec vs decision ADRs vs research evidence**: copier-cat backlog structure (settled 2026-11-14): backlog/docs/cc-assembly-mvp-spec.md (DOC-9) = feature requirements, user stories, MVP scope; backlog/decisions/decision-1..7 = composition/assembly ADRs; decision-8 = human help and JSON agent help share ONE command-description contract; decision-9 = agent skill installation is optional and open-standards-compatible — must work with or without dotagents (dotagents optional, never a required dependency); research docs = supporting evidence only. Implementation tickets reference spec + ADRs; DOC-9 explicitly points to the ADR layer, its 'Implementation Decisions' section kept as feature-level constraints. Why: separates what-to-build (spec) from why-decided (ADRs) so tickets cite requirements and rationale independently.

<!-- lore:01a1db58-c1c0-7510-90b7-0e44768312c1 -->
* **copier-cat backlog task prefix migrated cc → CCC (PR #1, 0af7d61)**: copier-cat's backlog task prefix changed from \`cc\` to \`CCC\` (config.yml, 2026-11-14); PR #1 landed the mechanical store migration + reference updates (main at 0af7d61 'backlog: migrate task IDs to CCC'). New tasks are CCC-\*; older docs/commits may still cite CC-\* IDs — same tasks, migrated IDs, so treat stray CC-\* references as pre-migration aliases when looking up work. Standing lesson from the dotfiles repo applies here too: task IDs are not globally unique across branches — identify tasks by title/content, never ID alone \[\[01a1c67b-dcfb-73a0-8fea-393d3096b751]].

<!-- lore:01a1d85b-d92a-7f26-9068-92aa3d8fe93d -->
* **copier-cat CC-8: four stack relationship types; compatibility is not dependency**: CC-8 (2026-11-13) splits four relationship types the design previously mixed: Composition ('what else belongs in this project?' — python + agentic: both work alone, overlap on shared files, contributions merge per CC-7's data approach \[\[01a1d855-dfd0-7a99-9d02-602c72f4923b]]); Specialization ('what kind of project is this?' — web-app + python: web-app cannot generate its intended project alone, expects Python's foundation → requires a DECLARED prerequisite); Variant ('which option within a template?' — CLI vs library); Sub-layer ('which concern owns this inside a template?'). Ruling: compatibility is not dependency — without it stack.yml is an ordered list with no semantics (order alone can't distinguish additive/required/replacing/incompatible/shared-file-merge). Ownership: foundation = language/build/test/package layout; specialization = web app/CLI/library/service; cross-cutting = agentic workflows/review loops/task management; copier-cat = ordering/compat reports/shared-file resolution/updates/contribution — never language or agent concerns. Open question: first-class specialization? Inclination: first-class prerequisites, NO inheritance machinery.

<!-- lore:01a1dac4-d59c-7ca8-8f6b-27aa1ff8a2eb -->
* **copier-cat CLI: stack new/edit/show, --stack use, assemble, --guided five stages**: Revised copier-cat interaction model (2026-11-14, prototype-implemented; design docs + CC-15 updated, no commits yet): \`stack new/edit/show\` manage the stack.yml recipe; \`--stack STACK use TEMPLATE|SOURCE\` adds a template/source to a stack; \`assemble\` creates the composed project; direct stack invocation supported; \`--guided\` walks five explicit review stages. Misspellings suggest corrections but never auto-execute them. Stack selection: NO implicit default stack — fuzzy selection for humans, explicit \`--stack\` for automation (supersedes the Nov 13 \`cc research\` default-stack proposal). All 15 prototype interaction cases pass. Extends CC-7 composition semantics \[\[01a1daa6-8bad-751a-9173-0b5cbd38e1ea]]; short alias per \[\[01a1daa6-8bd1-7614-868a-867b3735aca4]].

<!-- lore:01a1dfe0-642b-790e-8e04-0418e8318312 -->
* **copier-cat HANDOFF.md archived as DOC-11 history; living docs route to Backlog**: copier-cat convention (PR #14): superseded seed/implementation handoff docs are archived, never deleted — root HANDOFF.md became backlog/docs/seed-and-implementation-session-handoff.md as DOC-11 with frontmatter (type: history; tags: history, provenance; id: DOC-11) preserving the complete original handoff as historical evidence. After archiving, zero living handoff references remain: README, AGENTS.md agent router, spec.md, ROADMAP.md, and task files all point to Backlog as the coordination surface. Why: handoffs are session-provenance evidence worth keeping, but a stale root HANDOFF.md competes with the Backlog router and misroutes agents. Write new handoffs expecting eventual DOC-\<n> archival.

<!-- lore:01a1dfe0-641b-77c4-b61b-7b92ededcd44 -->
* **copier-cat prototype/ dirs pin probe evidence; external scratch removed after capture**: copier-cat preserves experiment/probe evidence in prototype/ directories pinned at commits: prototype/cc-assembly-ux @ bf924fd, prototype/cc-composition-evidence @ 5d0d5ad (34 probe artifacts + findings guide), prototype/cc-composition-cli retained. External scratch dirs (~/code/personal/cc-assembly-ux-prototype, ~/code/try/cc-stack-probe) were deleted only AFTER their evidence was preserved in prototype/ (verified absent 2026-11-14). Why: probes are expensive to reproduce — prototype/ is the durable evidence store backing the CC-7 composition design \[\[01a1db58-c1d6-7fcb-9f8f-d30f9ea83e13]]; scratch dirs are disposable. Never delete a prototype/ dir or a scratch dir before confirming its evidence is captured in the durable store.

<!-- lore:01a1d829-2c07-7ada-9de0-0498053b0b9c -->
* **copier-cat stack.yml: stack-as-data design; chunk naming blocker (CC-7/APT draft-2)**: Design direction (CC-7 + APT draft-2, 2026-11-13): stack = data — stack.yml lists ordered layers (source + answers file): gh:bengineerdavis/python-project-template (.copier-answers.python-project-template.yml), then agentic-project-template (.agentic-answers.yml). Three things derive from it with no new concepts: composition (render in order, merge shared files via the merger registry), compatibility (a report, not a maintained contract — layers are compatible where overlapping files have a resolving merger; degradation: unknown format → text-span fallback → human review), and the resolution report. Blocker (APT draft-2): chunks are TOML keys or marked AGENTS.md sections; arbitrary text has nothing addressable, and markers are a single literal pair — chunks need by-name identity + explicit region orientation; an unnameable chunk can't be reported, edited, or made compatible. Unresolved (user decides, CC-7): stack.yml location — project vs template collection vs derived from answers files (deriving = zero new files but implicit stack). CLI surface: \[\[01a1d13b-c05f-7589-a7af-a2d3fe1ba079]].

<!-- lore:01a1db53-5274-7427-8835-7d51bafefbdf -->
* **copier-cat stacked PRs: retarget dependents when base merges; .worktrees/ccc-\* worktrees**: copier-cat (~/code/templates/copier-cat) work lands as stacked PRs — e.g. #6 (research/agent-workflows-v2, CC-17 human/agent workflow contracts) → main, with #4 (docs/followup-tasks) stacked on #6. When the base PR merges, retarget each dependent PR onto the new base, then re-verify mergeability and the test suite before reporting. Worktrees are worktrunk-managed under .worktrees/ccc-\<slug>; several clean per-feature worktrees coexist and branch names map to PR head branches. Leave the local main checkout behind origin — pulling/fast-forwarding main is a git mutation requiring authorization \[\[01a1d1d4-d5b9-774e-9669-0627cbc5e529]]. Adjacent to the CC-7 composition design \[\[01a1dac4-d588-7416-b7f8-30ee77e97ea5]].

<!-- lore:01a1a282-3cca-7e7f-ae47-39d35650ea56 -->
* **Cost post-mortem tooling: agentsview stats, opencode stats, lore data subcommands**: No dedicated cost-post-mortem skill exists. Inventory: agentsview stats (window-scoped analytics; --since 28d|YYYY-MM-DD, --agent, --format json; automated and one-shot sessions excluded by default; config at ~/.agentsview/config.toml + AGENTSVIEW\_DATA\_DIR env; \[\[remote\_hosts]] array drives \`agentsview sync\` per host). opencode stats gives project-wide session/token totals. lore data subcommands: list, show, clear, delete, move, split, merge, consolidate, export, recover, reground-entities (needs running gateway), dedup, contradictions, reindex — no cost-specific subcommand. OpenRouter API: /models, /models?supported\_parameters=tools, and /providers are reachable WITHOUT auth; /activity (usage/cost data) requires the API key. Closest existing skills: /retro, /diagnosing-bugs (tight feedback loop — for cost: a 'session cost exceeds budget' query), /research.

<!-- lore:01a1e149-8cf7-7ea6-8ee1-8006dbb16dc8 -->
* **docs/AGENTIC-REPOS.md survey: 10 repos, 437 tasks; AGENTS.md size tracks evidence not maturity**: Survey written 2026-11-15 to docs/AGENTIC-REPOS.md: 10 repositories, 7 live queues, 437 open tasks (user's '10+' estimate accurate). Findings: (1) four of five template repos are themselves agentic — concrete reason CCC-21 insists on reading the target's contributor docs rather than assuming a house format travels \[\[01a1db6f-617c-7ebd-8f23-3ecd7c8c6e70]]; (2) AGENTS.md size doesn't track maturity (range 28 → 1,063 lines; second largest, 800, is worktrunk-isolation-trial with zero tasks) — size tracks how much evidence got pasted in, making DOT-357's retirement-path problem fleet-wide; (3) roborev: 347 lines of instructions, no queue — agentic work without task capture, the inverse of DOT-360's problem.

<!-- lore:01a19f95-f596-765d-8191-4bc280ee5713 -->
* **dot-255 Molecule VM matrix: 4 approved test seams, vertical slice first**: Author directive (dot-255, maintainer-approved test-design seam): 'A skipped or unavailable platform must never count as a pass' — coverage reporting must record each requested host/guest/architecture/provider combination as passed, failed, or explicitly unsupported. dot-255 DRAFT defines 4 Molecule VM-matrix seams: (1) VM lifecycle via Molecule create/destroy asserting a real VM with usable systemd (catches container substitution); (2) per-topic converge/idempotence per guest (OS-specific provisioning regressions); (3) externally observable integration (real guest chezmoi apply, service manager, gateway health); (4) public coverage report. First slice: one Debian ARM64 VM lifecycle proof on the Mac before bulk-writing a provider matrix. macOS-on-Linux is an explicit open capability, not an assumed libvirt feature. Pairs with the VM-verification gate \[\[01a18b66-b456-74db-9c36-38aef87e3b2c]].

<!-- lore:01a1f526-09ce-7fd5-b1c2-ada7e7c74c8d -->
* **DOT-305 lore daemon re-distillation corrupts DB; export reproduces corruption**: DOT-305's .lore.md retention failures stem from corruption in the LoreAI knowledge DB itself: the daemon's re-distillation writes bad entries (title/body swaps, deleted-correct entries, wrong pointers) into the knowledge store. Neither \`lore import\` nor \`lore data export\` repairs it — export faithfully reproduces the corrupted DB entries into .lore.md/AGENTS.md, so regenerating files cannot fix a corrupted source. Fix path: upstream report to BYK/loreai about the re-distillation corruption, plus the interim guard in PR #145 canarying the three load-bearing rule anchors. Verified 2026-11-19.

<!-- lore:01a1ed66-7d17-7c55-a09b-a0bc6b4ac8d1 -->
* **dotagents 3.4.0 measured: trust add non-idempotent; refresh vs projection trust scopes differ**: Measured reference behavior (2026-11-17, isolated $DOTAGENTS\_HOME, dotagents 3.4.0): a minimal Agent Plugins v1 bundle installs to $DOTAGENTS\_HOME/plugins/\<name>/; \`list\` and \`doctor\` pass; bundled SKILL.md preserved; .agents/agents/\<name>/agent.md is NOT generated — no orchestrator install without separate proof. trust add is NOT idempotent for an already-trusted source. Project refresh and later projection evaluate DIFFERENT trust scopes — always test fresh-user behavior, not only an already-trusted machine. Local-path sources must live inside the relevant scope/project root. Upstream Matt Pocock software-factory is the one malformed skill: dotagents projects 38 of 39 plugin skills.

<!-- lore:01a1ebc3-5714-7385-bbcd-509eb2e70f1f -->
* **dotagents\_cli pytest marker: L2 networked test opt-in, excluded from default CI via addopts**: dotfiles pyproject addopts is \['-m', 'not llm\_judge and not dotagents\_cli'] — the L2 test (test\_add\_preserves\_predeclared\_targets\_and\_copilot\_exclusion; networked, marked dotagents\_cli + needs\_ansible) is opt-in and NEVER runs in default CI; convention mirrors llm\_judge (opt-in model tests). The L2 test deliberately FAILS (not skips) when the dotagents binary is missing (shutil.which check, 'run mise install'). CI lacks ansible-playbook so behavioral (needs\_ansible) tests skip there regardless; the AGENTS.md fast-loop command silently excludes dotagents\_cli tests. Fidelity note: the L2 test seeds \[trust] github\_repos BEFORE projecting, unlike the real flow (declare first, then trust add) — believed harmless because validateTrustedSource only fires on source addition. Verified 2026-11-17 review.

<!-- lore:01a1f535-3848-7798-bdc6-5bcafbfaba50 -->
* **dotfiles CI installs zsh+chezmoi globally; suites must skip under binary-but-unconfigured**: The dotfiles test workflow installs zsh (apt-get) and chezmoi (get.chezmoi.io installer, tag v2.74.1, -b /usr/local/bin) so test\_profile\_deployment can run — but the global install flips every suite gated on shutil.which('chezmoi') from skip to seeing a binary with NO configured source state. Design rule: chezmoi-dependent suites must skip-or-pass under binary-but-unconfigured, never fail. Skip mechanisms: live\_managed() returns None when \`chezmoi managed\` errors or yields an empty set (fixture skips; empty set impossible on dev worktrees — chezmoi reads the main checkout source); the gitconfig fixture skips on stderr 'map has no entry for key' (Go text/template missing-map-key error). Suites passing -S with the repo payload, fixtures, or --override-data pass unconfigured — this hinges on \`managed\` not rendering template contents. Baseline hazard: a test moving from EXPECTED\_FAILURES reason A (missing binary) to failure reason B keeps the same node ID — check\_suite\_baseline stays green while the test breaks differently; hence skip-not-fail is the goal.

<!-- lore:01a1d213-65db-78b1-97e6-e22a67309d38 -->
* **dotfiles gate system: tests.yml CI backstop, EXPECTED\_FAILURES baseline, .lore.md guard (DOT-305)**: .github/workflows/tests.yml is the CI backstop — every other check runs from a prek hook, so a fresh clone has none and a bypassed hook leaves no trace. PR events check out the MERGE commit; fetch-depth: 0 is required (a shallow clone makes \`git diff\` fail 'bad object', indistinguishable from a lint finding). \`uv sync --frozen --group test\` — --group test is REQUIRED: no runtime deps means bare sync installs nothing and pytest is absent. EXPECTED\_FAILURES entries are full pytest node IDs; the parser regex stops at the first ' - ' (fail-closed). AGENTS.md's known-failures table must match EXPECTED\_FAILURES (tabled suites: tests/clipped, tests/restart\_stale, tests/docs). The 2 .lore.md guard-test failures are known baseline (present on main CI, re-confirmed 2026-11-17): DOT-305 capture lost rule anchors upstream — do NOT hand-edit .lore.md or delete failing assertions to green the suite; resolve the loss via DOT-305 while retaining the guard.

<!-- lore:01a1ebc6-f288-7fd7-90cc-dd4c1b616712 -->
* **dotfiles pytest selection: opt-in llm\_judge/dotagents\_cli markers; needs\_ansible skipif; last -m wins**: pyproject addopts = \`-m 'not llm\_judge and not dotagents\_cli'\` — opt-in markers deselect expensive/networked tests from CI AND default local runs. dotagents\_cli (DOT-348): the L2 test runs the real pinned dotagents 3.4.0 binary in a networked isolated HOME (DOTAGENTS\_HOME set); no scheduled runner exists — it runs only when a human remembers, and it is the only guard against a silent revert of the Copilot exclusion. needs\_ansible = skipif(shutil.which('ansible-playbook') is None), evaluated at collection; CI doesn't install ansible, and a machine with a BROKEN ansible still fails on purpose ('that is a real signal, not a skip') — convention matches tests/topics/test\_apt\_tasks.py. Trap: a CLI \`-m llm\_judge\` looks like it might fight addopts — addopts are PREPENDED to CLI args, so the last -m wins and the opt-in run works as intended.

<!-- lore:01a1ebc6-f280-78e5-834c-4c1d859f81c5 -->
* **dotfiles tests.yml env: uv sync --group test only; gita==0.16.8.2 hard pin; pyrefly uv tool run**: CI installs ONLY \`uv sync --frozen --group test\` — the project has no runtime dependencies, so a bare sync installs nothing and pytest is absent (measured 2026-11-05); no ansible-playbook in CI, which is what makes the needs\_ansible skipif valid. gita==0.16.8.2 installs via \`uv tool install\` with a HARD pin, not latest: a floating tool in a gate means a green commit and a red re-run of the same commit — bump gita and the mise entry (home/dotfiles/apps/mise/files/config.toml) in step. Type check: \`uv tool run --from pyrefly==1.3.2 pyrefly check\` — pyrefly is not a project dep; the local version comes from mise (pipx:pyrefly = 1.3.2), absent in CI. Action pins: actions/checkout@11d5960a (v4; persist-credentials: false; fetch-depth: 0), astral-sh/setup-uv@d0cc045d (v6; enable-cache: true), actions/upload-artifact@ea165f8d (v4.6.2). A separate step asserts declared requires-python equals the actual interpreter.

<!-- lore:01a1e15a-41f5-7657-bb31-2e5d0d99fca9 -->
* **dotfiles topic\_requires/topic\_provides dependency declarations; requires\_map**: dotfiles topics declare dependencies in apps/\*/topic.yml via topic\_provides/topic\_requires (docs/TOPICS.md): topic\_requires lists package managers the topic depends on (line 32), tokens map to the binary to probe (e.g. topic\_requires: \[homebrew]), checked against three diagnostic states (line 95). requires\_map = {dep\_name: \[topic\_name, ...]} (line 130) lists every topic requiring a dep, built from every topic in apps/ (line 136). Hard rule (line 140): a topic must not uninstall a dependency another topic requires. apps/loreai/topic.yml: topic\_name loreai, topic\_provides \[loreai], topic\_requires \[] — standalone (no runtime deps, not even node), same shape as apps/oro; its OpenRouter key probe degrades to 'unavailable' when the llm plugin is absent. This makes 'how many topics does this break' computable rather than a judgment call — feeds DOT-275 impact grading.

<!-- lore:01a18b94-d0ec-7eb6-8b45-7adb13947f48 -->
* **dotfiles verification: syntax-check proves SOURCE, converge proves DEPLOYED; provisioning never owns logic**: Standing author rulings: (1) Two distinct proofs, neither substitutes for the other — \`ansible-playbook --syntax-check\` validates the SOURCE tree; converge/idempotence passes validate the DEPLOYED tree after \`chezmoi apply\` (agents never run apply). (2) Placement principle (AGENTS.md): provisioning may install and trigger a tool but NEVER contain its logic or state — those live in the tool's own managed config or the deployed tree; companion: a mechanism that can decline is proven to relent (governance tests prove ignore rules didn't swallow real dotfiles). (3) The deployed tree cannot be branched — ~/dotfiles is one file set regardless of branches; two agents applying from two branches overwrite each other; apply from main only, by the author or the main-checkout session.

<!-- lore:01a1e310-05ad-7223-ba13-fb253fe19645 -->
* **Flagship FS-2 research workflow: research/\<topic> branch, docs/research findings, wt merge rebase**: Steps: 1. One branch per ticket: research/\<topic> (backstage, storage, runners), worktree at .worktrees/research-\<topic>, branched from current main. 2. Write findings to docs/research/\<topic>.md; commit and push the branch. 3. User reads the findings file — that read IS the review (roborev is broken in flagship). 4. On approval, \`wt merge -b research/\<topic>\` from the main checkout; wt merge rebases onto main, so a fork point older than main's HEAD blocks nothing. 5. Close-out: write the gist + findings-file path into the ticket's final summary, then close the ticket (FS-2.1 → docs/research/backstage-catalog.md). Gotchas: - Green wt merge ≠ content review: its hook runs \`mise run check\` (alint + tombi) only \[\[01a1e1ca-c09d-7352-af75-3dd3668ed1af]]. Verify: - \[ ] findings file committed and pushed on research/\<topic> - \[ ] ticket final summary names gist + path before closing

<!-- lore:01a1e2e3-52dd-7907-a935-a3751c561769 -->
* **gita repo catalogue: fork-vs-own by GitHub fork metadata; work names never committed**: The gita repo catalogue in dotfiles (re-pointed by PR #177) carries three invariants: (1) fork-vs-own is decided by GitHub's fork metadata, NOT the remote owner string — the file's header rule (ruled Best-README-Template a fork: 'fork of othneildrew, registered pre-catalogue'); (2) work repos stay discovery-driven via recursive walk — no group: work entries exist and work repo names are NEVER committed (spec.md § Data; all 41 live-but-uncatalogued repos are work/\*); (3) provenance ruling comments (gitignore duplicate-basename, promptlint fork-of, bt-import not-a-fork — gh-api-verified 2026-10-18) must survive catalogue edits; only superseded caveats get replaced. Trap: dropping ruling comments or adding work names looks like tidy cataloguing but destroys the ruling record and leaks private repo names.

<!-- lore:01a19867-a87a-7172-bc02-3d49e976ed0d -->
* **grilling + domain-modeling SKILL.md: frontier rounds, sub-agent facts, GLOSSARY.md/ADR conventions**: User's skill conventions (read from ~/.config/opencode/skills/ SKILL.md bodies, 2026-11-01): grilling = HITL interview primitive; decisions mapped as a design tree worked in rounds; each round numbers ALL frontier questions (askable now without guessing) with recommended answers (➡️) worded so 'yes' accepts; settled decisions push the frontier outward; 'Finding facts is your job, never the user's' — dispatch a sub-agent with fresh context for environment facts; decisions go to the user and wait; session done when frontier is empty; do not act until user confirms shared understanding. domain-modeling = ACTIVE discipline for changing the model (challenging terms, edge cases, writing GLOSSARY.md/ADRs the moment they crystallise — merely reading GLOSSARY.md is not this skill); GLOSSARY.md at root, docs/adr/ with numbered ADRs; GLOSSARY-MAP.md at root signals multiple contexts (per-context GLOSSARY.md + docs/adr/ under src/\<context>/); create files lazily. grill-with-docs body is one line: 'Call the Skill tool twice, for grilling and domain-modeling'; disable-model-invocation: true. Flow context: \[\[01a19858-7721-7b0c-9cd6-3a167484f23e]].

<!-- lore:01a1e2ce-f795-7597-9393-b2f648ed5416 -->
* **Known-failures baseline gate: failure LIST match, not count; exact node IDs only**: dotfiles test gate (AGENTS.md § Known failures, enforced by the local baseline parser script): a change is green when the failure LIST matches the baseline, not when the count does. Baseline = exact pytest node IDs, NEVER directories/suites — an earlier suite-matching version let a brand-new regression inside a known-failing suite pass silently. The gate fails on three outcomes: (1) unexpected failures/errors not in baseline; (2) zero results — a run that collected nothing or died before printing a summary produces zero failures and must not read as success (require the summary line); (3) stale baseline — expected node IDs that no longer fail are REPORTED for list correction but do NOT fail the build: 'a baseline that is too generous is a lesser fault than a gate nobody can run.'

<!-- lore:01a1f534-25aa-7402-8aac-c3c062702533 -->
* **lore .lore.md corruption repair from git history (DOT-305)**: Repair path for lore DB/.lore.md corruption (DOT-305; recommended 2026-11-19, user decision pending): Steps: 1. Verify scope — compare DB, current .lore.md, and committed .lore.md git history. 2026-11-19 state: \`uv --frozen\` rule healthy; \`requires\_map undefined skips removal\` absent from DB and .lore.md; \`work repos NEVER enter committed catalogue\` body-swapped in DB (title intact, body replaced with upgrade-path text). 2. Checkout the last good .lore.md from git history into a scratch location — never the live file. 3. \`lore data recover\` pointed at that file — re-imports FROM .lore.md INTO the DB. 4. \`lore data export\` — regenerates .lore.md/AGENTS.md FROM the repaired DB. 5. Run the guard test — confirm both anchors survive. Gotchas: - Repair races the daemon's next re-distillation: if corruption is a recurring upstream bug (heal-deletes-correct-entry), the repaired DB gets re-corrupted — the upstream report to BYK/loreai is the real fix; DB repair is only a stopgap. - Alternative (re-tell lore the rules in a session) is simpler but loses original provenance/session linkage. - Never invoke lore with drifted PWD (DOT-386). Missing anchors are recoverable from committed .lore.md  \[truncated — entry too long]

<!-- lore:01a18d59-f415-74d8-a40a-0c1260373802 -->
* **lore disambiguation: entity registry, versioned entries, contradictions, dedup passes**: Four mechanisms resolve ambiguous learnings: (1) entity registry — alias/relation-based resolution, not text-matching ('LoreAI'/'lore'/'the memory gateway' → one entity; 'Ben Davis' + bengineerdavis@gmail.com → one person). Types: person/org/service/tool/repo/infra; alias types: name/email/github/slack/phone/nickname/url/domain; subcommands: list/show/add/edit/alias add+rm/relation add+rm/merge/dedup/search/delete. (2) Versioned ID-keyed entries — \`lore log \[\<id>]\` shows an entry's timeline, \`lore diff \<id>\` shows version diffs; latest version wins, older versions retained as history. (3) Contradiction detection (\`lore data contradictions\`) + rerank by directive strength for contradicting preferences. (4) Mechanical passes — data dedup (collapse near-duplicate learnings across projects), reindex, reground-entities (re-derive entities from history). Mental model: two names for one thing → alias/merge; which version is current truth → log/diff; opposite entries → contradictions; same learning twice → dedup; inconsistent recall → reindex.

<!-- lore:01a1989e-cff4-7960-bfa6-f4bce8ae884b -->
* **Lore gateway runtime: ports 3207/5673, LORE\_CONFIG\_DIR, remote-gateway mode, self-update via GHCR patch chain**: Lore gateway listens on 127.0.0.1:3207 (LORE\_LISTEN\_PORT/HOST); a second instance serves the /ui dashboard on 5673. Config dir ~/.config/loreai (env.zsh sets LORE\_CONFIG\_DIR; binary default is ~/.lore); data dir ~/.local/share/lore (gateway.log, gateway.pid, gateway.port, lore.db, lore.log). LORE\_REMOTE\_GATEWAY=true is ACTIVE: path-less sessions route to /\_\_lore\_unattributed\_\_/\<sessionID>; pass --local or LORE\_REMOTE\_GATEWAY=0 to disable. Banner exports ANTHROPIC\_BASE\_URL/OPENAI\_BASE\_URL to 3207. The gateway holds an OpenRouter key and forwards with provider=none/modelRoute=none; prefix-matched routes (nvidia → integrate.api.nvidia.com) get modelRoute set. Self-update mechanism: GitHub releases BYK/loreai + GHCR nightly tag, patch-chain updates walking fromVersion→toVersion with expectedSha256 from the release asset digest.

<!-- lore:01a184f3-0e62-72c4-b962-1b6c479c3831 -->
* **Merge PR queue in order: merge-into-branch conflict resolution**: Steps: 1. Census open PRs + stacked branches; merge-tree the whole set in one order, then reversed — proves only the known task-line conflicts remain. 2. Merge in dependency order; a stacked branch opens its PR only after its parent merges (PRs-target-main rule \[\[01a18483-45ff-71b3-8ebd-7277c9237083]]). 3. Task-line PRs conflict at TASKS.md section ends — resolve keep-both (oldest first) \[\[01a183f3-f164-7f36-b3e5-7848c815a4c0]] by merging origin/main INTO the PR branch, then push; the PR then merges clean. 4. \`gh pr merge\` failing 'Base branch was modified' or a spurious 'has merge conflicts' right after your own push = mergeability-recompute lag — retry succeeds. 5. After the last merge: trunk verify on main (ruff, pyrefly, skillet, full suite vs known-failure baseline), then reap worktrees proven landed (dirty=0 + ancestor of main). Gotchas: - Rebasing a PR branch needs force-push (deny-tier) — merge main INTO the branch instead. - Expect one keep-both conflict per task-line PR; fix as you go, don't batch. Verify: - \[ ] gh pr list shows zero open PRs - \[ ] trunk checks green except the known tests/policy baseline Post-burst review: \[\[01a18488-0343-7429-80a1-fc578fe63618]].

<!-- lore:01a1ad42-8549-756f-b6e0-dbe0779f8229 -->
* **Multi-repo coordination hub: flagship HQ, galaxy map, redge/copier-cat/push-it-forward**: User's multi-repo coordination hub project (started 2026-11-05 via ask-matt): centralized coordination across repos — 'constellations in a galaxy of domains' with a mothership. Components: redge (repo syncing/dependency management), copier-cat (templates; upstreams refactored changes), push-it-forward (automates upstream changes to third-party tools; location UNRESOLVED 2026-11-11 — not under ~/code nor in user's GitHub repos; may be idea-only). HQ repo = flagship (PRIVATE, task\_prefix 'fs', stamped from python-project-template + agentic-project-template). Galaxy-map vocabulary (world/location/route/mission) lives in flagship GLOSSARY.md. Round-2 direction: flagship v1 spec states what the map is FOR (the questions humans and agents ask of it — the spec's FIRST section, a requirements list), then storage (graph/DB still open), then how a route becomes missions filed at each location. Pending: user picks destination — A flagship v1 spec (recommended), B map-only spec, C ADR-only.

<!-- lore:01a1dae2-cfcd-774c-8eac-b900abf6900d -->
* **MVP contract vs exploration log (CC-15 retro candidate)**: Candidate pattern recorded in copier-cat CC-15 for retro (2026-11-14): separate an exploration log from a small MVP contract. MVP contract components: first usable outcome, ONE workflow, minimum inputs, explicit non-goals, feedback criteria. Retro question: how to preserve future ideas without letting them continually expand the next usable step. Status: candidate pending retro discussion — not yet a confirmed standing rule. Pairs with the MVP prioritization directive (only fix what blocks MVP usage).

<!-- lore:01a1840e-b6d4-74fc-bf28-fb009470e572 -->
* **On-behalf-of commits: exited session's uncommitted work + PROVENANCE notes**: On-behalf-of flow (sibling session's uncommitted work, author-directed): BRANCH + PR, never direct to main. Steps: (1) stash with a DESCRIPTIVE message naming source session + destination — that message is what identifies a folded stash later; (2) fast-forward local main; (3) branch + worktree, apply the stash; (4) split commits by concern; (5) fold sibling text word-for-word with a PROVENANCE git note per commit; (6) open the PR; (7) drop the stash only after the branch is on origin. Verify byte-for-byte; the mover does NOT fact-check — disclose as unreviewed. Gotchas: (a) a source worktree's uncommitted file persists after folding — git restore it post-merge or it blocks that branch's rebase; confirm no session runs there first. (b) Popping a FOLDED stash onto a later branch conflicts (content already landed) — read the message first; a failed pop KEEPS the stash (no data loss); git reset clears the unmerged index; verify your own work is committed before assuming the stash holds it — the visible stash may be the legacy folded one, not yours. Sibling detection: \[\[01a183f3-f167-7a97-ad26-aae05c28e470]].

<!-- lore:01a18b94-d0eb-7801-ba22-37e1fab0d874 -->
* **OpenCode plugin errors: raise-don't-whisper; cause classified by fixer (install vs loader)**: Author ruling (2026-10-27): broken plugin states RAISE, never whisper in debug. Cause is classified by who can fix it: 'install' (NpmInstallFailedError/LockTimeoutError in the server log) → hard fail; remedy \`opencode plugin add @loreai/opencode\` + \`opencode service restart\` — the shared server's in-process auto-install can wedge for hours with only a WARN in ~/.local/share/opencode/log/opencode.log. 'loader' ('Plugin must export' in API state.error) → fail with ignore\_errors; root cause was BYK/loreai 0.40.0 shipping a pre-v2 function-shaped default export the v2 loader rejects. Remediation: dotfiles PR #110 'loreai: disable the incompatible V1 plugin' merged on origin/main (by 2026-11-04). Verify plugins via \`opencode api get /api/plugin\`, not the table (columns drift between CLI versions); malformed/empty responses never count as loading.

<!-- lore:01a1e1c6-1d75-7903-8bba-3d64b690688d -->
* **opencode stats / opencode.db: cost is per-session; no per-commit cost surface**: opencode exposes no cost-query tool signature (session\_move/session\_rename exist; nothing for cost). Native surface: \`opencode stats --project .\` (per-project totals, scoped to the repo), \`--models\` (per-model breakdown), opencode.db (per-session data). Nothing NATIVE surfaces 'tokens spent on commit X' — the gap is filled EXTERNALLY by CodeBurn \`codeburn yield\` (chosen 2026-11-15 for DOT-275; reads existing session files, correlates sessions↔commits). Structural mismatch remains the design constraint: git notes attach per-commit but cost accrues per-session (observed: one session produced five commits; one commit took three sessions), so a cost note records an ATTRIBUTION, not a measurement — it must carry session/methodology provenance so the derivation stays checkable. Verified 2026-11-15.

<!-- lore:01a1c19b-8cc4-7785-ad0d-953632016d41 -->
* **OpenCode V2 plugin contract: plugins key, directory paths, Plugin.define, ctx domains**: V2 plugin contract (@opencode/plugin types + migrate-v1 doc): config key is \`plugins\` — the singular \`plugin\` key is silently ignored; configured paths must name DIRECTORIES (single-file path skipped with WARN). A V1 function-returning-hooks default export does NOT run in V2; the shape is default-export \`Plugin.define({id, setup(ctx)})\` with hooks on ctx domains: ctx.tool.hook('execute.before', …), ctx.session.hook('model.request', …), ctx.session.hook('context', …); setup's return value is ignored. ctx.event.subscribe() is an AsyncIterable consumed with \`for await … of\` and takes {signal} — the CALLBACK form is SILENTLY IGNORED (cause of several 'hook never fired' probes). SessionModelRequest {sessionID, agent, model: Model.Ref, kind, baseURL?, headers} — baseURL/headers are the mutable routing fields. Disable via config \`-\` prefix plus \`\*\`/\`.\*\` wildcards; NO CLI disable subcommand (the gap DOT-290 targets).

<!-- lore:01a18985-3bd6-7495-add1-65b8428259ec -->
* **opencode-copilot-kit opencode.jsonc: git push denied, commit boundary at ask**: The kit repo's opencode.jsonc permission tiers: Tier 1 free = git status/diff/log/show \*, bun test \*, bun run typecheck/lint \*/format \*; Tier 2 ask = shell \* catch-all + git commit \* (the commit boundary); Tier 3 deny = git push \* ('never negotiable'). Pre-hoc gates where mistakes are expensive: edit db/migrations/\* = ask, edit infra/\* = ask. A policies array hard-denies after all permission rules and saved approvals: shell:git push \* and shell:sudo \* → deny.

<!-- lore:01a1a30c-8257-7705-b34b-aaa8138a4319 -->
* **OpenRouter REST API: /api/v1/key works with inference key; /api/v1/activity needs management key**: Verified empirically 2026-11-03 (no \`openrouter\` CLI exists on the machine; official surface is REST): GET /api/v1/models → 200 unauthenticated (453 models, per-model pricing incl. input\_cache\_read/input\_cache\_write, context\_length, supported\_parameters); ?supported\_parameters=tools is a server-side filter; /api/v1/providers → 200; /api/v1/key → 200 with an ordinary inference key (returns usage, usage\_daily/weekly/monthly — no management key needed); /api/v1/activity → 401 unauth / 403 'Only management keys can fetch activity' with an inference key; /api/v1/generation?id= → 400 without a valid id. 84/453 models carry pricing.overrides (tiered pricing). For structured spend data use /api/v1/key, not /api/v1/activity — the user's standing cost-reporting questions (2026-11-03) route here.

<!-- lore:01a1dfe0-6437-7c19-b302-c1c176c9edec -->
* **Post-merge cleanup: verify-then-remove; never touch other sessions' state**: Steps: 1. Confirm each PR merged (merge commit on main, branch deleted) before touching local state — cleanup only follows verified merges. 2. Preserve evidence first: move probe/experiment artifacts into durable stores (copier-cat prototype/ dirs) before deleting any scratch directory. 3. Remove completed publication worktrees and merged local branches. 4. Remove duplicate local backlog task files ONLY after diffing against their merged records. 5. Remove superseded .scratch queues. Gotchas: - Duplicate task files look like clutter but may hold unmerged content — verify against merged records before removal. - NEVER cleanup targets: parent template's divergent main, existing worktrees, other sessions' files, unrelated ai-prompt-library worktrees \[\[01a1d1d4-d5b9-774e-9669-0627cbc5e529]]. Verify: - \[ ] External scratch dirs absent (cc-assembly-ux-prototype, cc-stack-probe). - \[ ] git status shows only your own removals.

<!-- lore:01a1db63-a53b-7e4b-a631-92f711aebfe6 -->
* **Post-mortem skill self-detection: mechanically answerable prerequisites (backlog/config.yml, references: frontmatter)**: The shareable post-mortem skill must self-detect its prerequisites, all mechanically answerable from files: (1) backlog store exists — backlog/config.yml + backlog-py on PATH; (2) repo captures incidents as decisions — backlog/decisions/ exists (decision-3 item 4 states the rule); (3) linkage field available — references: in task frontmatter (7 live instances to pattern-match); (4) incident definition settled — DOT-275 status: To Do = stop and ask, Done = proceed mechanically. The skill's gating prerequisite is a task in the queue it reads, so it can refuse to run and say why. Precedent: the backlog-task skill (~/.agents/skills/backlog-task/, spec-first via skillet, published to ai-prompt-library/skills/backlog-task/) — its description is a routing condition and it deliberately covers record creation only; closing/claiming/lifecycle is the gap the post-mortem flow fills.

<!-- lore:01a19fa1-74d5-7d36-8797-23723a242947 -->
* **Recording author rulings: backlog/decisions/ docs vs Decide-tasks; edit inside SECTION markers**: Two repo precedents for author rulings: (1) backlog/decisions/ docs with Context/Decision/Consequences sections (status accepted/proposed); (2) a "Decide …" task with labels \[planned] whose Description carries the full decision framing — model: DOT-127 ("Do not add it without the author's explicit decision — the telemetry is the whole question"). A task shaped as implementation whose answer changes what gets built should be re-cut as a decision. The ruling question can live inside the task itself (user confirmed DOT-104 as the venue): amend Implementation Notes with a date, leaving the original Description intact — notes record measurements. Trap: edits to backlog task files must stay inside the SECTION markers or backlog-py parsing breaks. Convention: writing or revising a queued task is itself a commit to backlog/ on main.

<!-- lore:01a1cdbc-b6f7-74f5-817e-1d22379a8c19 -->
* **redge dev conventions: uv sync --all-groups, mise run quality, wt switch, roborev fast loop**: redge repo workflow: \`uv sync --all-groups\`; tests via \`uv run pytest\`; \`mise run install\` (tools + hooks + agentic-state dotagents); \`mise run quality\` (lint + format-check + type-check). Agent work must be assigned in backlog/ BEFORE it starts — one worktree per task via \`wt switch \<redge-N-slug>\`. Every commit passes the roborev fast loop; check findings with \`mise run roborev-status\`. Mirrors flagship's agentic layer (backlog-py tasks, wt worktrees, roborev per-commit).

<!-- lore:01a1cdbc-b6f0-7b96-ad4e-5019a3cbace2 -->
* **redge SPEC R1–R11: inert scaffold, ff-only safety contract, update/upgrade semantics**: redge (github.com/bengineerdavis/redge, PUBLIC, Fair Source FSL-1.1-MIT) is an INERT SCAFFOLD: command surface registered (\`redge --help\`), every subcommand an honest stub. PyPI package redge-cli, command \`redge\`; the name never depends on the registry token; a Rust port may reuse the namespace (SPEC R11). Normative requirements: docs/research/repo-manager/SPEC.md (R1–R11); design sketch REDGE-ARCHITECTURE.md; implementation queued in backlog/ BOOTSTRAP.md order: normalize → manifest/state → registry → sync → role → CI. Safety contract R4 (binding): declarative presence with safe fast-forward, NOT a lockfile pinning revisions; only permitted working-tree mutation is \`git merge --ff-only\` on a clean, attached, non-diverged repo. update = fetch all + refresh state, never mutates. upgrade = ff-only toward the locked ref: dirty → 'skipped: dirty'; diverged/detached/no-upstream → 'blocked-since', tree untouched; pin advances only on merge success. Failed fetch reads UNKNOWN freshness, never 'up to date'.

<!-- lore:01a1d206-7b42-7d29-931b-064edead9db9 -->
* **research dispatch layering: one interviewer; interaction-questioning inside dispatch**: Interaction layering for research dispatch \[\[01a1d144-a863-77ae-a3b4-4b4c468f066d]] (grilling outcome 2026-11-12): grill-with-docs and interaction-questioning must never both interview the user simultaneously — conflicting interaction policies. Dispatch uses interaction-questioning internally; when confidence stays low it records the ambiguity and hands the unresolved point to grilling rather than opening another inline interview. Inference cues: 'just capture this'/'for later'/'does this block us?' → infer file-only delivery silently; 'summarize what you find' → notify on completion. Answers-vs-conclusions: default to answers; conclusions need a HIGHER confirmation threshold because they shape later thinking more than delivery. The two choices are independent — wanting an answer now does not imply wanting a recommendation; a conclusion may recommend a decision but never constitutes one.

<!-- lore:01a18b94-d0ed-7a0c-8554-838cb0c3e6e1 -->
* **restart-stale role: one inequality (process start < binary ctime); rejected alternatives**: dotfiles/roles/restart-stale detects stale processes with ONE inequality — process start < binary ctime. Rejected alternatives (author walkthrough 2026-10-03): mtime/birthtime (Homebrew bottles carry archive-internal dates — ollama installed Sep 22 reports mtime Sep 18); prefix/bundle process matching; dropping unparseable ps rows (unknown ≠ not-running; ps\_rows raises); SIGKILL (never — GUI quit is a negotiation: osascript quit with timeout → poll ps → reopen derived bundle only after confirmed exit); bare systemctl --user (needs XDG\_RUNTIME\_DIR; use the systemd module); per-topic notify handlers (topics append one check at end of install — ollama, 1password). GNU stat shadows BSD stat on this machine \[\[01a18150-951d-7b9e-8287-c587fa50eb99]] — use \`stat -c '%Y'\`.

<!-- lore:01a1ad05-29ea-7d7d-a14a-8944c752c695 -->
* **retro skill: 7 improvement categories; build deterministic checks over rules; review agent imposes standards**: User's /retro skill (~/.config/opencode/skills/retro, frontmatter disable-model-invocation: true — user-invoked only): conducts a retrospective on a coding session. Steps: load the writing-for-agents style guide; read primary sources for the specified session (default: current); hunt improvement candidates in 7 categories — Navigation, Automated checks, Coding standards, Global AGENTS.md, Tool economy, No-ops, Information access; present candidates to the user by severity. Doctrine: read the repo's own check command FIRST (package.json/lint scripts/CI workflow) so an existing-but-unwired check is the finding, not a reinvention; a repo with NO guardrail (no pre-commit hook, no CI running lint/typecheck/test) is itself a finding; mechanical violations → build a deterministic check rather than writing a rule; CODING\_STANDARDS.md is reserved for genuine judgement calls (add nav pointers once it exceeds 1,000 lines); the review agent (least context pressure, receives a diff) imposes coding standards, not the implementation agent; CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md used sparingly as navigation pointers.

<!-- lore:01a1861d-7f2b-7c2a-9e07-f9130b022773 -->
* **roborev MCP branch review: enqueue, poll, read verdict; NEVER --wait**: Flow: 1. roborev\_status first (confirm daemon). 2. Resolve roborev\_\* MCP tools via agent tool discovery — never substitute CLI reads. 3. roborev\_list\_repos resolves repo\_path (retain root\_path); first call can return empty — re-query. 4. Validate a supplied base branch with Git; review against the merge-base. 5. Enqueue \`roborev review --from-skill --branch\` — NEVER --wait: --wait looks like the natural way to get the verdict synchronously but parks a blocking shell; run without it and rely on auto-notification. If a \`roborev wait\` shell is already running, do NOT kill it — let it finish. 6. Poll roborev\_list\_jobs (repo\_path + branch), following next\_cursor. 7. Done: roborev\_get\_review (job\_id) + roborev\_list\_comments; for a panel use the synthesis parent's verdict, not a member's. Gotchas: empty verdict is NOT a pass — present it as empty; roborev\_complete\_fix needs the exact fix\_session\_id from the hook instruction. Sequencing: PR creation waits for the verdict; open the PR only after it, then the merge-readiness quiz.

<!-- lore:01a1eb9d-b06c-7b04-b096-d397eed68aa6 -->
* **roles/services scope edges: linger hint is behavioral not root; calendar jobs need own plist; backlog-daemon rename migration**: roles/services deliberately declines three things (README 'what it refuses' section; the 2026-11-17 quiz misses — 4/8 — clustered exactly on these edges, mechanics were solid): (1) enable-linger is a printed HINT, never a provisioning step — for one's own user it needs no root; the real reason is it changes login behavior (the service outlives the session), an author decision never done silently, same principle as never deleting logs on teardown. (2) The bundled plist models a DAEMON (KeepAlive/RunAtLoad); a monthly audit job needs StartCalendarInterval — a different plist shape — so calendar-job topics keep their own plist and borrow only the convergence rules; service\_systemd\_restart sets Restart= in systemd where no calendar value applies. (3) The backlog-daemon unit rename needs no reinstall — the mise entry is untouched; the only migration is \`systemctl --user disable backlog-daemon.service\` on a Debian host still holding the old unit (none exists here; macOS unaffected). Related: \[\[01a1eb56-93b1-7085-863f-15178849b421]].

<!-- lore:01a1ed7b-98a4-740b-9c2b-0cdfe9e40bc6 -->
* **run-role.sh backlog convergence: enable → bootout → bootstrap → daemon status health check**: Runbook for repairing/verifying the backlog daemon via run-role.sh (deployed PR #181 code; assistant is permission-walled from ~/dotfiles \[\[01a1ed66-7d47-7a27-ab7e-44b92a48a445]] — the USER runs it). Steps: 1. Confirm the argv non-empty guard in the deployed run-role.sh — the guard that would have caught the shim-only plist bug. 2. User runs: bash ~/dotfiles/run-role.sh backlog — it re-renders ~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.backlog-py.daemon.plist with the full argv (shim + \`daemon run --foreground --host 127.0.0.1 --port 18765\`). 3. Convergence order: enable first (clears any persistent disable override) → bootout the loaded label → wait → bootstrap. 4. Health check: \`backlog-py daemon status --json\` — a bootstrap leaving nothing listening fails the run. Gotchas: - Do NOT stop the crash loop before the run: bootout→bootstrap handles the looping label, and diagnosing in the exact broken state answers the mystery-writer question. Verify: - \[ ] \`launchctl list | grep backlog\` shows a PID (not '- 2') - \[ ] \`backlog-py daemon status --json\` exits 0 - \[ ] plist argv has 8 elements (not 1)

<!-- lore:01a1db78-5753-7db1-b8c5-dd0759cfdbfb -->
* **Session-records PRs: records-only scope; branch off origin/main when repo dirty**: Convention (CCC-22, 2026-11-14): session records (backlog task files capturing session findings) publish as dedicated per-repo PRs containing ONLY record files — never implementation tickets, prototype evidence, or specs. Scope boundaries: .scratch implementation tickets must be reconciled into native Backlog tasks before publishing (never implement from both task systems); prototypes live on prototype/\<name> branches as evidence only; a spec (e.g. copier-testkit) gets its own project path.
